The Gravity Restaurant SOP Library is part of The Gravity Standards Library within The Gravity System. Thomas
Monroe Books is the publisher.
The original purchaser may reproduce, adapt, post, and use the SOP pages in this book for internal training and
operational use at one restaurant location. This limited license does not permit resale, public distribution,
commercial duplication, posting the complete book online, or sharing the files with unrelated businesses.
These procedures are general operational templates. Each restaurant must customize them to its concept,
equipment, menu, staffing model, insurance requirements, company policies, and all applicable federal, state, and
local laws, health codes, fire codes, accessibility requirements, alcohol-service rules, employment rules, and
regulatory guidance.
Nothing in this book replaces legal, accounting, human-resources, food-safety, medical, emergency-response, or
regulatory advice. When a law, official code, manufacturer instruction, or approved company policy conflicts with a
template in this book, the controlling requirement must be followed.


HOW TO USE THIS LIBRARY
• Choose the SOP that most closely matches the process you need. Do not adopt it unchanged simply because it
appears in this book.
• Customize the responsibility, tools, timing, steps, performance standards, escalation authority, local requirements,
and approved forms.
• Assign an SOP identification number and owner. Record the version, effective date, approval, and next review
date before training employees.
• Train the employee using explanation, demonstration, supervised practice, real-shift observation, and manager
verification.
• Post only the approved version. Remove or clearly retire obsolete versions so employees are not trained from
conflicting standards.
• Review high-risk and high-frequency SOPs more often than low-risk procedures. Document changes and retrain
affected employees.

THE TWO-PAGE SOP STANDARD
Page 1 is the controlled procedure: purpose, scope, responsibility, tools, critical standards, and numbered steps.
Page 2 is the management page: verification checklist, common mistakes, customization fields, training sign-off,
and revision reminder.
Keep both pages of each SOP together. A procedure is not approved for use until the restaurant completes the
customization fields, assigns ownership, trains affected employees, and records manager approval.
Use Restaurant SOP & Station Operations Builder when the restaurant needs SOP-building instruction, station
posters, rollout tools, governance, readiness verification, or document-control training. This library supplies ready-to-
customize procedures rather than replacing that builder system.

OPERATIONAL GLOSSARY


PART 1

RECEIVING, STORAGE & INVENTORY

20 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control receiving, temperature and quality checks, storage, dating, rotation, counts, high-cost
products, and recall response.


1. Delivery Receiving SOP

SOP ID V2-001 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for delivery receiving are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Match the delivery to the approved vendor, schedule, purchase record, and receiving location.
5. Inspect the vehicle, seals, packaging, dates, cleanliness, and evidence of contamination or pests.
6. Separate accepted, held, and rejected items and obtain vendor acknowledgment of exceptions.
7. Sign only after quantities, condition, temperatures, and disposition are accurately recorded.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.


1. Delivery Receiving SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
delivery receiving.
□ Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
□ Match the delivery to the approved vendor, schedule, purchase record, and receiving location.
□ Separate accepted, held, and rejected items and obtain vendor acknowledgment of exceptions.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Starting delivery receiving before the approved source, order, product, tools, and receiving area are confirmed.
• Accepting or storing product before quantity, condition, temperature, date, and label checks are complete.
• Allowing questionable or rejected product to mix with accepted inventory.
• Recording exceptions after the driver leaves instead of at the point of receiving.
• Failing to obtain manager disposition and preserve the invoice, credit, and traceability record.
• Approved vendor, source, order, invoice, or recall system: _______________________________________
• Product temperature, condition, quantity, date, or quality limit: _________________________________
• Storage location, hierarchy, label, rotation, or restricted-access control: ________________________
• Rejection, hold, disposal, recount, or manager-approval authority: ________________________________
• Local food code, allergen, traceability, chemical, or recall requirement reviewed: ________________
• Restaurant-specific receiving, inventory, or high-cost product steps: ____________________________

REVISION NOTE: Record future changes in the restaurant master SOP index and retrain affected employees when the approved


2. Delivery Temperature Verification SOP

SOP ID V2-002 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify delivery temperatures with a clean, calibrated thermometer and reject, hold, or escalate
product that does not meet approved limits.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Select representative products and sanitize the calibrated probe between products.
5. Measure in the approved location without damaging packaging or contaminating food.
6. Compare results to the current approved receiving limits for each food type.
7. Reject or hold product outside limits and record product, lot, temperature, time, and manager decision.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.


2. Delivery Temperature Verification SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
delivery temperature verification.
□ Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
□ Select representative products and sanitize the calibrated probe between products.
□ Compare results to the current approved receiving limits for each food type.
□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Checking delivery temperature verification with an unclean, uncalibrated, or inappropriate thermometer.
• Measuring only the equipment display, container surface, or an unrepresentative location.
• Delaying the reading or recording a rounded, estimated, or backfilled result.
• Keeping product in service when the required limit or time history is uncertain.
• Failing to document corrective action, product disposition, and manager verification.
• Approved vendor, source, order, invoice, or recall system: _______________________________________
• Product temperature, condition, quantity, date, or quality limit: _________________________________
• Storage location, hierarchy, label, rotation, or restricted-access control: ________________________
• Rejection, hold, disposal, recount, or manager-approval authority: ________________________________
• Local food code, allergen, traceability, chemical, or recall requirement reviewed: ________________
• Restaurant-specific receiving, inventory, or high-cost product steps: ____________________________

3. Product Quantity and Quality Check SOP

SOP ID V2-003 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for product quantity and quality check are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Count cases, units, weights, sizes, brands, grades, and substitutions against the order.
5. Inspect freshness, color, odor, texture, packaging, dates, and usable yield.
6. Separate shortages, overages, substitutions, and quality failures before product enters storage.
7. Record the accepted quantity and obtain manager approval for material differences.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

□ Separate shortages, overages, substitutions, and quality failures before product enters storage.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

• Allowing questionable or rejected product to mix with accepted inventory.

4. Invoice and Purchase Order Verification SOP

SOP ID V2-004 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for invoice and purchase order verification are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Match vendor, purchase order, invoice, item, unit, price, tax, fee, credit, and delivery date.
5. Mark shortages, substitutions, returns, damaged items, and agreed credits on all required copies.
6. Do not alter quantities or approve unauthorized prices to make records agree.
7. Route signed records and discrepancies to the designated manager or accounting process.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.


4. Invoice and Purchase Order Verification SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
invoice and purchase order verification.
□ Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
□ Match vendor, purchase order, invoice, item, unit, price, tax, fee, credit, and delivery date.
□ Do not alter quantities or approve unauthorized prices to make records agree.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Signing the invoice before product, quantity, price, credit, and condition are verified.
• Ignoring substitutions, duplicate charges, missing credits, or unapproved fees.
• Changing the purchase record without preserving the original discrepancy.
• Allowing the driver to leave before rejected or shorted product is documented.
• Failing to route the final invoice and exception record to the responsible manager.

• Rejection, hold, disposal, recount, or manager-approval authority: ________________________________

5. Rejected Delivery SOP

SOP ID V2-005 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for rejected delivery are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Identify the exact item, quantity, lot, reason, and evidence supporting rejection.
5. Keep rejected food separated from accepted product and prevent accidental storage or use.
6. Notify the vendor and manager, document credit or replacement terms, and obtain acknowledgment.
7. Release rejected product only through the approved return or disposal process.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.


5. Rejected Delivery SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
rejected delivery.
□ Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
□ Identify the exact item, quantity, lot, reason, and evidence supporting rejection.
□ Notify the vendor and manager, document credit or replacement terms, and obtain acknowledgment.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Mixing rejected delivery items with accepted inventory or moving them into normal storage.
• Failing to mark, isolate, photograph, and document the exact reason for rejection or damage.
• Accepting a vendor promise instead of completing the approved credit or return record.
• Discarding product before manager and vendor disposition is confirmed.
• Failing to inspect nearby product for the same damage, contamination, or temperature problem.

• Product temperature, condition, quantity, date, or quality limit: _________________________________
• Storage location, hierarchy, label, rotation, or restricted-access control: ________________________
• Rejection, hold, disposal, recount, or manager-approval authority: ________________________________
• Local food code, allergen, traceability, chemical, or recall requirement reviewed: ________________
• Restaurant-specific receiving, inventory, or high-cost product steps: ____________________________

6. Damaged Product Documentation SOP

SOP ID V2-006 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for damaged product documentation are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Photograph or otherwise document damage before moving product when safe.
5. Check whether leakage, temperature abuse, broken seals, or contamination affects nearby cases.
6. Hold affected product and record item, lot, quantity, condition, invoice, and vendor response.
7. Obtain manager disposition and complete credit, replacement, return, or disposal records.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
damaged product documentation.
□ Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
□ Photograph or otherwise document damage before moving product when safe.
□ Hold affected product and record item, lot, quantity, condition, invoice, and vendor response.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Mixing damaged product documentation items with accepted inventory or moving them into normal storage.
• Failing to mark, isolate, photograph, and document the exact reason for rejection or damage.
• Accepting a vendor promise instead of completing the approved credit or return record.
• Discarding product before manager and vendor disposition is confirmed.
• Failing to inspect nearby product for the same damage, contamination, or temperature problem.

7. Allergen-Sensitive Product Receiving SOP

SOP ID V2-007 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To accept, reject, document, and route incoming product only after the required source, condition,
quantity, temperature, allergen, and invoice controls for allergen-sensitive product receiving are verified.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Identify allergen-sensitive items and compare labels, ingredients, seals, and substitutions to the approved order.
5. Reject unapproved substitutions or damaged packaging that makes ingredient identity uncertain.
6. Keep packages closed and separated from leaking or dusty products during receiving.
7. Label and transfer the item directly to its designated protected storage location.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

□ Keep packages closed and separated from leaking or dusty products during receiving.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Accepting product with missing, damaged, changed, or unreadable ingredient and allergen labels.
• Assuming a substitute or new formulation matches the previously approved product.
• Storing allergen-sensitive product where leaking or open ingredients can contact it.
• Removing manufacturer packaging before traceability information is retained.
• Failing to notify managers and recipe owners about ingredient or supplier changes.

8. Food Storage Hierarchy SOP

SOP ID V2-008 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To store food in the approved hierarchy so raw products cannot contaminate ready-to-eat food and
every item remains identifiable and accessible.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Place ready-to-eat food above raw animal products and separate products by required cooking risk.
5. Keep chemicals, personal items, damaged product, and returned items outside food-storage zones.
6. Use shelves, pans, and containers that prevent leakage and cross-contamination.
7. Correct misplaced product immediately and document repeated or high-risk findings.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

• Storing raw, ready-to-eat, allergen, chemical, or guest-use items in an unsafe relationship.
• Blocking airflow, drains, sprinklers, access, cleaning, or temperature monitoring.

APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.

14. FIFO Rotation SOP

SOP ID V2-014 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To rotate inventory using FIFO so older usable product is selected first and expired, damaged, or
questionable product is controlled.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Locate the oldest acceptable product by approved date, lot, or receiving sequence.
5. Move older product to the first-use position without hiding newer product or damaging packages.
6. Check quality and expiration before combining or using product.
7. Record and escalate excessive age, repeated rotation failures, or product that cannot be used safely.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

□ Check quality and expiration before combining or using product.

□ Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
□ Cool and portion prepared food in approved containers before storage.
□ Store in the correct location with airflow and separation from raw food.
□ Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
□ Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.
• Placing product into prepared food storage without verifying identity, label, date, condition, and approved location.
• Storing raw, ready-to-eat, allergen, chemical, or guest-use items in an unsafe relationship.
• Blocking airflow, drains, sprinklers, access, cleaning, or temperature monitoring.
• Leaving open, leaking, damaged, or unprotected containers in service.
• Failing to correct and document repeated organization, temperature, pest, or rotation problems.

17. Raw Protein Storage SOP

SOP ID V2-017 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To store raw proteins in the correct hierarchy, containers, temperatures, and locations to prevent
leakage and cross-contamination.
APPLIES TO: Receiving employees, stock employees, kitchen leads, managers, and approved vendors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiver performs and records checks. The manager authorizes rejection, correction,
storage exceptions, and vendor follow-up.
WHEN USED: During each delivery, storage transfer, inventory count, or product-control review covered by this
SOP.
• Approved order, invoice, or purchase record
• Calibrated thermometer and sanitizing supplies
• Receiving inspection and rejection record
• Labels, date marks, and storage equipment
• Manager and vendor contact information
• Only approved products from approved sources are accepted.
• Food is protected from contamination and moved promptly to approved storage.
• Quantity, condition, temperature, date, and invoice exceptions are recorded before acceptance.
• Management authorizes rejected, substituted, damaged, recalled, or questionable product.
1. Review the approved order, vendor, schedule, limits, storage plan, and prior manager notes.
2. Prepare a clean receiving area and gather calibrated tools, records, labels, and required PPE.
3. Confirm responsibility and keep delivery product separated from food preparation and guest traffic.
4. Keep raw poultry, seafood, whole cuts, ground meats, and other proteins in designated leak-proof zones.
5. Use pans or containers that contain drips and never store raw protein above ready-to-eat food.
6. Separate species or preparation groups when required for cross-contamination or allergen control.
7. Clean leakage immediately and evaluate affected food and surfaces.
8. Move accepted food promptly to approved storage and maintain cold, frozen, dry, and allergen controls.
9. Record rejected, held, substituted, damaged, short, or credited product and obtain required signatures.
10. Clean the receiving area and submit records, photos, and unresolved issues to management.

18. Inventory Count SOP

SOP ID V2-018 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete accurate inventory counts using approved units, locations, cutoff rules, product movement
controls, and variance review.
APPLIES TO: Authorized counters, kitchen leads, managers, and employees assigned controlled products.
RESPONSIBILITY: Counters record actual quantities without adjustment. Management verifies variances, custody,
and follow-up.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled count, shift cutoff, delivery review, variance investigation, or controlled-product
check.
• Current count sheet or inventory system
• Approved unit-of-measure and item list
• Scale, calculator, labels, or counting tools
• Secure access to controlled products
• Variance and manager verification record
• Counts use the approved item, unit, location, and business cutoff.
• Employees count actual product and never change quantities to match expected results.
• High-cost and controlled products remain secured and traceable.
• Management reviews material variances and assigns documented follow-up.
1. Review the approved count list, locations, units, cutoff time, and access controls.
2. Freeze or record product movement according to the count plan and assign independent sections.
3. Gather clean counting tools and verify scales, labels, and system access.
4. Freeze movement during the count and use the approved location sequence.
5. Count sealed, open, prepared, and in-use product in the approved unit of measure.
6. Record actual quantities immediately and mark items that require weighing or conversion.
7. Recount material differences and submit the count without undocumented adjustment.
8. Recheck material differences and secure controlled products after the count.
9. Submit actual quantities, conversion notes, movement records, and unresolved variances.
10. Management signs the count and assigns investigation or corrective action.


18. Inventory Count SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
inventory count.
□ Freeze or record product movement according to the count plan and assign independent sections.
□ Freeze movement during the count and use the approved location sequence.
□ Record actual quantities immediately and mark items that require weighing or conversion.
□ Recheck material differences and secure controlled products after the count.
□ Management signs the count and assigns investigation or corrective action.
• Counting inventory count with inconsistent units, unorganized storage, or active product movement.
• Estimating open containers without the approved method.
• Changing counts to match expectations instead of recounting and documenting variance.
• Allowing unauthorized access or failing to preserve custody during the count.
• Ending without manager review of material variance, waste, transfer, and purchase records.

19. High-Cost Item Control SOP

SOP ID V2-019 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect high-cost products through restricted access, accurate counts, documented movement,
variance review, and manager accountability.
APPLIES TO: Authorized counters, kitchen leads, managers, and employees assigned controlled products.
RESPONSIBILITY: Counters record actual quantities without adjustment. Management verifies variances, custody,
and follow-up.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled count, shift cutoff, delivery review, variance investigation, or controlled-product
check.
• Current count sheet or inventory system
• Approved unit-of-measure and item list
• Scale, calculator, labels, or counting tools
• Secure access to controlled products
• Variance and manager verification record
• Counts use the approved item, unit, location, and business cutoff.
• Employees count actual product and never change quantities to match expected results.
• High-cost and controlled products remain secured and traceable.
• Management reviews material variances and assigns documented follow-up.
1. Review the approved count list, locations, units, cutoff time, and access controls.
2. Freeze or record product movement according to the count plan and assign independent sections.
3. Gather clean counting tools and verify scales, labels, and system access.
4. Restrict access and record issue, return, waste, transfer, comp, and count activity for controlled items.
5. Use approved portions, scales, locks, cameras, or dual verification where assigned.
6. Compare usage and sales to beginning, received, transferred, and ending quantities.
7. Investigate and document unexplained variance without accusing employees or altering records.
8. Recheck material differences and secure controlled products after the count.
9. Submit actual quantities, conversion notes, movement records, and unresolved variances.
10. Management signs the count and assigns investigation or corrective action.

20. Product Recall Response SOP

SOP ID V2-020 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control product recall response consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Identify the affected item, brand, lot, date, supplier, and recall instructions.
5. Stop sale and use, search every storage and service location, and isolate affected product.
6. Mark the hold clearly and prevent return to inventory except through authorized disposition.
7. Document quantity, location, guest or sales traceability, vendor contact, disposal or return, and final verification.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.


20. Product Recall Response SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the product, approved source, required limit, record, and rejection or escalation authority for
product recall response.
□ Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
□ Identify the affected item, brand, lot, date, supplier, and recall instructions.
□ Mark the hold clearly and prevent return to inventory except through authorized disposition.
□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Waiting for perfect information before isolating potentially affected product.
• Mixing recalled product with normal inventory or failing to block it from use.
• Discarding product before lot, quantity, location, and authority direction are documented.
• Overlooking transferred, prepared, sold, or off-site product.
• Closing the recall without required notification, reconciliation, disposal proof, and follow-up.
• Approved vendor, source, order, invoice, or recall system: _______________________________________
• Product temperature, condition, quantity, date, or quality limit: _________________________________
• Storage location, hierarchy, label, rotation, or restricted-access control: ________________________
• Rejection, hold, disposal, recount, or manager-approval authority: ________________________________
• Local food code, allergen, traceability, chemical, or recall requirement reviewed: ________________
• Restaurant-specific receiving, inventory, or high-cost product steps: ____________________________

PREP & PRODUCTION

25 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control prep readiness, forecasting, ingredient preparation, batching, portioning, labeling, cooling,
thawing, waste, cleaning, and closing.


21. Prep Area Opening SOP

SOP ID V2-021 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure the prep station is clean, organized, stocked, and ready before prep work begins.
APPLIES TO: Prep cook, opening cook, kitchen lead, chef, shift lead, or manager on duty.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before prep work begins, before product is pulled, or before the prep cook starts the assigned prep
list.
• Prep list
• Recipe cards
• Cutting boards
• Knives
• Scales
• The prep station is clean, stocked, organized, food-safe, and ready before prep work begins.
• Wash hands before beginning prep.
• Set up sanitizer according to policy.
• Use clean and sanitized tools.
1. Wash hands before beginning prep setup.
2. Check that the prep table and work area are clean.
3. Remove trash, dirty towels, old labels, food debris, and clutter.
4. Set up sanitizer and clean towels according to restaurant policy.
5. Gather required cutting boards, knives, containers, portion tools, labels, and markers.
6. Review the prep list for the shift.
7. Review recipe cards or prep specifications if needed.
8. Check par levels and expected volume.
9. Pull product according to prep needs.
10. Check product quality, labels, dates, freshness, and storage capacity; obtain manager release before prep begins.

□ Review the prep list for the shift.
□ Check par levels and expected volume.
□ Check product quality, labels, dates, freshness, and storage capacity; obtain manager release before prep begins.
• Starting prep area opening without the current plan, recipe, ingredients, tools, PPE, and storage destination.
• Allowing contamination, allergen contact, temperature loss, or unapproved substitutions.
• Producing or restocking without checking on-hand product, shelf life, par, and expected demand.
• Failing to label, date, cool, store, and record the completed product.

22. Prep List Completion SOP

SOP ID V2-022 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete the prep list with accurate priorities, quantities, assignments, deadlines, and status
updates based on the approved production plan.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the production forecast, reservations, events, current sales trend, weather, and manager priorities.
2. Count usable carryover and on-hand product; confirm labels, shelf life, quality, and available storage space.
3. Compare projected need with on-hand quantity using the restaurant's approved par, yield, and batch standards.
4. List each item, required quantity, batch size, priority, station, and completion time.
5. Sequence work by food-safety risk, cook/cool time, shelf life, service urgency, and shared-equipment demand.
6. Assign each item to a trained employee and confirm recipe, tools, ingredients, and allergen controls.
7. Record start, completion, actual yield, waste, shortage, substitution, and unfinished work as production occurs.
8. Recalculate remaining needs when sales, staffing, product, or reservations change; obtain approval before overproducing.
9. Label, date, cool, store, stage, or transfer completed product according to the approved process.
10. Review the completed list with the manager and hand off shortages, variances, and next-shift needs.

□ Assign each item to a trained employee and confirm recipe, tools, ingredients, and allergen controls.
□ Recalculate remaining needs when sales, staffing, product, or reservations change; obtain approval before
overproducing.
□ Review the completed list with the manager and hand off shortages, variances, and next-shift needs.
• Building prep list completion from habit without current sales, reservations, inventory, carryover, and shelf-life
information.
• Ignoring yield, portion, equipment capacity, cooling space, or staffing limits.
• Producing full quantities too early instead of using approved waves and rechecks.
• Changing quantities without recording the reason and manager approval.
• Ending the shift without actual-versus-forecast review and handoff.

23. Production Forecast Review SOP

SOP ID V2-023 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To convert forecasted sales, reservations, on-hand product, shelf life, and staffing into a realistic
production plan before prep begins.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Gather recent sales, menu mix, reservations, events, weather, promotions, delivery timing, and day-of-week history.
2. Verify current inventory, usable carryover, shelf life, open product, thawing product, and known shortages.
3. Separate forecasted demand by meal period, menu item, station, batch, and high-risk or long-lead product.
4. Apply approved yields, portion sizes, waste assumptions, and safety stock rather than guessing from last shift.
5. Adjust for staffing skill, equipment capacity, cooling space, holding limits, and expected ticket concentration.
6. Identify products that should be prepared in smaller waves to protect freshness, quality, and food safety.
7. Compare the forecast with pars and flag unusual increases, decreases, substitutions, or purchase needs.
8. Obtain manager approval for material changes, expensive product, limited inventory, or planned overproduction.
9. Convert the approved forecast into prep quantities, batch timing, assignments, and recheck points.
10. Record actual sales, shortages, waste, carryover, and forecast error for the next review.


23. Production Forecast Review SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the recipe or plan, batch or yield, required controls, storage result, and manager escalation
point for production forecast review.
□ Verify current inventory, usable carryover, shelf life, open product, thawing product, and known shortages.
□ Apply approved yields, portion sizes, waste assumptions, and safety stock rather than guessing from last shift.
□ Identify products that should be prepared in smaller waves to protect freshness, quality, and food safety.
□ Obtain manager approval for material changes, expensive product, limited inventory, or planned overproduction.
□ Record actual sales, shortages, waste, carryover, and forecast error for the next review.
• Building production forecast review from habit without current sales, reservations, inventory, carryover, and shelf-life
information.
• Ignoring yield, portion, equipment capacity, cooling space, or staffing limits.
• Producing full quantities too early instead of using approved waves and rechecks.
• Changing quantities without recording the reason and manager approval.
• Ending the shift without actual-versus-forecast review and handoff.

25. Protein Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-025 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare proteins according to approved recipes, yields, sanitation, time-temperature controls,
allergen protections, labels, and waste standards.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the approved protein recipe, cut, yield, portion, batch quantity, thaw status, and production priority.
2. Wash hands, put on required PPE, and establish a cleaned and sanitized raw-protein work area.
3. Gather dedicated boards, knives, pans, scales, labels, and containers and verify they are clean and intact.
4. Inspect product identity, temperature, package condition, date, odor, color, and authorized source.
5. Keep raw protein separated from produce, ready-to-eat food, clean utensils, and allergen-sensitive work.
6. Trim, cut, grind, tenderize, or portion only according to the recipe and approved equipment method.
7. Weigh or measure portions and record usable yield, trim, waste, shortage, and unusual product condition.
8. Minimize time out of refrigeration and return completed portions promptly to approved storage.
9. Label and date each container and clean and sanitize tools, equipment, handles, sinks, and work surfaces.
10. Wash hands, dispose of waste through the approved route, and obtain lead verification of yield and storage.

26. Seafood Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-026 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare seafood safely and consistently while controlling temperature, cross-contamination,
allergens, yield, labeling, and waste.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the seafood species, source information, shellfish tags when applicable, recipe, portion, thaw status, and batch need.
2. Wash hands, put on required PPE, and prepare a clean cold work area with dedicated tools and containers.
3. Inspect package integrity, temperature, odor, texture, color, ice condition, and signs of spoilage or temperature abuse.
4. Preserve required shellstock identification and traceability records and do not mix lots before controls are complete.
5. Keep seafood refrigerated and separate from ready-to-eat foods, produce, and other proteins during preparation.
6. Remove shells, bones, skin, scales, or entrails using the approved method without splashing or contaminating nearby areas.
7. Cut and portion according to recipe, verify weight and appearance, and record yield, waste, and product concerns.
8. Return prepared seafood to covered labeled refrigeration immediately; do not allow prolonged room-temperature staging.
9. Clean and sanitize boards, knives, sinks, equipment, and handles and control shell, bone, and packaging waste.
10. Wash hands and obtain manager review for off odor, damaged packaging, traceability gaps, parasites, or uncertain quality.

27. Poultry Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-027 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare poultry safely and consistently while controlling time, temperature, cross-contamination,
yield, labeling, and waste.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the poultry product, recipe, cut, portion, thaw status, batch need, and approved cooking destination.
2. Wash hands, put on required PPE, and establish a dedicated raw-poultry work area away from ready-to-eat food.
3. Inspect product temperature, packaging, date, odor, color, purge, and condition before opening.
4. Use dedicated boards, knives, pans, and containers and prevent splash from sinks, hoses, or running water.
5. Trim, debone, cut, or portion only according to the approved recipe and safe equipment method.
6. Weigh or count portions and record yield, trim, waste, shortage, and any product rejection.
7. Keep poultry under refrigeration except for the active batch and never return handled product to original packaging.
8. Cover, label, date, and store prepared poultry below ready-to-eat food in the approved hierarchy.
9. Clean and sanitize all contacted tools, equipment, sinks, handles, floors, and surrounding splash areas.
10. Remove gloves, wash hands, dispose of packaging safely, and obtain lead verification of storage and sanitation.

28. Meat Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-028 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare meat safely and consistently according to approved cuts, recipes, yields, sanitation, labels,
and storage limits.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.

8. Cover, label, date, and store portions promptly in the assigned refrigeration location.
9. Clean and sanitize knives, boards, grinders, slicers, scales, handles, and surrounding surfaces.
10. Wash hands and obtain manager review for unusual odor, color, temperature, damage, or yield variance.

30. Soup Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-030 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prepare soup from the approved recipe with correct batch size, cook and cooling controls, quality
checks, labels, and yield records.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the approved soup recipe, batch size, ingredients, allergen information, equipment, yield, and service plan.
2. Verify ingredient identity, quality, dates, measured quantities, and required thawing or precooking steps.
3. Prepare clean sanitized kettles, pots, utensils, measuring tools, thermometers, and protected containers.
4. Follow the recipe sequence for sweating, browning, liquids, seasonings, thickening, and ingredient additions.
5. Control heat and stirring to prevent scorching, boil-over, unsafe splashing, and uneven cooking.
6. Verify required cooking temperatures and final texture, flavor, color, seasoning, and yield.
7. Correct only through approved recipe adjustments and record any authorized substitution or variance.
8. Transfer the batch to hot holding, service, or rapid cooling using the approved time-temperature process.
9. Label and date stored soup and record batch, yield, temperature, waste, and disposition.
10. Clean the kettle or pot, utensils, surrounding equipment, floor, and splash areas and obtain manager verification.

31. Batch Cooking SOP

SOP ID V2-031 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To batch-cook only the amount needed while protecting recipe accuracy, cook limits, holding time,
quality, and waste control.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the production forecast, recipe, approved batch size, equipment capacity, holding plan, and cooling space.
2. Verify all ingredients, quantities, dates, allergen information, tools, containers, thermometers, and labels.
3. Do not increase a recipe beyond the approved scale or equipment capacity without manager and recipe verification.
4. Follow the recipe sequence and start time so all components reach the required quality and safety standard together.
5. Monitor cooking, mixing, agitation, and temperature across the full batch rather than checking only one location.
6. Record actual yield and compare it with the recipe expectation before portioning or transfer.
7. Divide the batch into approved service, holding, or cooling quantities without mixing old and new product.
8. Label each container with identity, batch, time, date, and disposition requirement.
9. Record shortage, excess, waste, substitution, quality issue, and manager-approved correction.
10. Clean equipment and work areas and review batch performance before beginning another production cycle.

□ Label each container with identity, batch, time, date, and disposition requirement.

8. Check portion consistency during the shift.
9. Correct over-portions or under-portions immediately.
10. Notify manager if portion tools are missing, damaged, or unclear.

34. Recipe Scaling SOP

SOP ID V2-034 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To scale recipes accurately by using approved conversion methods, units, yields, batch limits, and
verification before production.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the current recipe, prep list, forecast, shelf life, allergens, and completion time.
2. Wash hands and prepare a clean and sanitized station with approved tools, PPE, containers, and labels.
3. Inspect ingredients for identity, condition, date, temperature, and required thaw or storage status.
4. Confirm the original recipe yield and the required production quantity.
5. Calculate the scaling factor and independently verify high-risk ingredients and units.
6. Convert every ingredient consistently without rounding critical amounts informally.
7. Record the approved scaled recipe, expected yield, actual yield, and manager verification.
8. Verify final temperature, quality, portion, yield, label, date, and storage or handoff.
9. Record waste, shortage, substitution, delay, equipment, and food-safety exceptions.
10. Clean and sanitize the area and obtain manager or trainer verification.

35. Recipe Card Use SOP

SOP ID V2-035 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To use the current approved recipe card for ingredients, quantities, sequence, equipment, yields,
allergens, temperatures, and presentation.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the current recipe, prep list, forecast, shelf life, allergens, and completion time.
2. Wash hands and prepare a clean and sanitized station with approved tools, PPE, containers, and labels.
3. Inspect ingredients for identity, condition, date, temperature, and required thaw or storage status.
4. Locate the current approved recipe version before gathering ingredients.
5. Read the full recipe, yield, equipment, allergens, temperatures, and critical notes.
6. Follow the listed sequence and measurements without unauthorized substitution.
7. Record questions, yield differences, quality problems, and any approved change.
8. Verify final temperature, quality, portion, yield, label, date, and storage or handoff.
9. Record waste, shortage, substitution, delay, equipment, and food-safety exceptions.
10. Clean and sanitize the area and obtain manager or trainer verification.

• Using unclean tools, wrong ingredients, unverified product, or an unprotected work surface.
• Losing time-temperature, raw-to-ready-to-eat, or allergen separation during production.

WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is

37. Cooling Prepared Food for Storage SOP

SOP ID V2-037 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To cool prepared food for storage within approved time and temperature limits, document results, and
take immediate corrective action when limits are missed.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Divide food into shallow approved containers or smaller portions and begin cooling without delay.
5. Use approved rapid-cooling methods while protecting food from contamination.
6. Measure and record temperatures at required intervals using a sanitized calibrated probe.
7. If the approved cooling limit is missed, reheat, recool, or discard only under manager direction.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.


37. Cooling Prepared Food for Storage SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the recipe or plan, batch or yield, required controls, storage result, and manager escalation
point for cooling prepared food for storage.
□ Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
□ Divide food into shallow approved containers or smaller portions and begin cooling without delay.
□ Measure and record temperatures at required intervals using a sanitized calibrated probe.
□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Beginning cooling prepared food for storage without the approved time-temperature method, container depth, label, and
storage space.
• Using room temperature, deep covered containers, standing water, or another unapproved method.
• Combining warm and cold product or overloading refrigeration and airflow.
• Failing to monitor and record required times and temperatures.
• Keeping product when the control history or limit is uncertain instead of escalating.
• Approved recipe, yield, batch, par, prep-list, or forecast standard: ______________________________
• Equipment, utensils, PPE, labels, containers, or storage system: _________________________________
• Time, temperature, cooling, thawing, marinating, or shelf-life limit: ____________________________
• Allergen, cross-contamination, hygiene, or protected-work-area control: _________________________
• Portion, waste, variance, restock, or manager-approval target: _________________________________
• Restaurant-specific ingredients, stations, production sequence, or handoff steps: _______________

38. Thawing Food SOP

SOP ID V2-038 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control thawing food consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and exceptions
receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device

• Manager and regulatory contact information

• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.

1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Select an approved thawing method based on product, quantity, package, and needed time.
5. Keep product protected and contained during refrigeration, running-water, cooking, or approved microwave thawing.
6. Prevent room-temperature thawing, standing water, leakage, and cross-contamination.
7. Label or cook promptly and record any time-temperature exception.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.

40. Cross-Contamination Prevention During Prep SOP

SOP ID V2-040 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prevent cross-contamination during prep by separating raw and ready-to-eat food, tools, surfaces,
storage, employee movement, and cleaning tasks.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Separate raw and ready-to-eat work by time, space, tools, containers, and employee practices.
5. Wash hands and clean and sanitize the area before changing products or tasks.
6. Use designated boards, knives, pans, gloves, and storage locations.
7. Stop work and evaluate all affected food and surfaces after any suspected cross-contamination.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.

- VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Starting cross-contamination prevention during prep without clean hands, protected ingredients, sanitized tools, and a
controlled work area.
• Using shared towels, gloves, utensils, containers, fryers, or surfaces without approved controls.
• Relying on visible removal instead of remaking product after possible contact.
• Failing to verify ingredient labels and communicate directly with the designated leader.
• Continuing when product identity, cleaning, or cross-contact history is uncertain.

42. Prep Waste Tracking SOP

SOP ID V2-042 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To record and reduce waste connected to prep work, spoilage, overproduction, trimming, mistakes, and
unusable product.
APPLIES TO: Prep cook, cook, kitchen lead, manager, receiver, or employee assigned to waste tracking.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Any time product is discarded, spoiled, trimmed excessively, overproduced, incorrectly prepared,
damaged, expired, or unusable.
• Waste log
• Scale if used
• Waste container
• Manager review process
• Labels
• Waste is recorded accurately, unsafe product is removed, and managers use waste information to reduce repeat problems.
• Do not use unsafe, spoiled, expired, contaminated, or questionable product.
• Discard product according to restaurant policy.
• Keep waste areas clean.
1. Identify the waste issue.
2. Stop and confirm whether manager review is required before discarding.
3. Record the product name.
4. Record the amount wasted if required.
5. Record the reason for waste.
6. Record the employee or station if applicable.
7. Discard product according to policy.
8. Clean and sanitize area if needed.
9. Notify manager of repeated waste, large waste, or unusual waste.
10. Review waste patterns during manager follow-up.

□ Clean and sanitize area if needed.

• Starting prep waste tracking without the current plan, recipe, ingredients, tools, PPE, and storage destination.

• Producing or restocking without checking on-hand product, shelf life, par, and expected demand.
• Failing to label, date, cool, store, and record the completed product.

43. Prep Restocking SOP

SOP ID V2-043 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To restock prep areas to approved pars using safe product, FIFO rotation, correct labels, and quantities
that do not create hidden waste.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is

• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies

• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
