3. Inspect ingredients for identity, condition, date, temperature, and required thaw or storage status.
4. Review pars, production needs, labels, shelf life, and remaining service time.
5. Bring only the safe quantity that can be used or stored correctly.
6. Rotate product, verify labels and temperatures, and keep raw and ready-to-eat food separated.
7. Update the prep list and report shortages, waste, or delayed production.
8. Verify final temperature, quality, portion, yield, label, date, and storage or handoff.
9. Record waste, shortage, substitution, delay, equipment, and food-safety exceptions.
10. Clean and sanitize the area and obtain manager or trainer verification.

44. Prep Area Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-044 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean and sanitize the prep area without contaminating food, losing labels or traceability, or leaving
equipment and supplies unready.
APPLIES TO: Prep cooks, station cooks, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: Before and during each assigned preparation or production cycle and whenever product is
transferred or stored.
• Approved recipe, prep list, and production forecast
• Clean and sanitized tools and work surfaces
• Calibrated thermometer and required PPE
• Portioning, labeling, and storage supplies
• Waste log and manager communication
• Only approved recipes, ingredients, tools, yields, and production quantities are used.
• Time, temperature, hygiene, cross-contamination, and allergen controls remain active throughout prep.
• Prepared food is portioned, labeled, dated, stored, or transferred promptly.
• Waste, shortages, quality failures, and recipe questions are reported before service is affected.
1. Review the current recipe, prep list, forecast, shelf life, allergens, and completion time.
2. Wash hands and prepare a clean and sanitized station with approved tools, PPE, containers, and labels.
3. Inspect ingredients for identity, condition, date, temperature, and required thaw or storage status.
4. Remove or protect food and disassemble approved removable parts.
5. Scrape and wash soil, rinse as required, then sanitize using approved concentration and contact time.
6. Air-dry and reassemble only clean equipment and tools.
7. Reset the area and report damage, pest evidence, chemical issues, or incomplete cleaning.
8. Verify final temperature, quality, portion, yield, label, date, and storage or handoff.
9. Record waste, shortage, substitution, delay, equipment, and food-safety exceptions.
10. Clean and sanitize the area and obtain manager or trainer verification.


44. Prep Area Cleaning SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the recipe or plan, batch or yield, required controls, storage result, and manager escalation
point for prep area cleaning.
□ Wash hands and prepare a clean and sanitized station with approved tools, PPE, containers, and labels.
□ Remove or protect food and disassemble approved removable parts.
□ Air-dry and reassemble only clean equipment and tools.
□ Verify final temperature, quality, portion, yield, label, date, and storage or handoff.
□ Clean and sanitize the area and obtain manager or trainer verification.
• Starting prep area cleaning without the current plan, recipe, ingredients, tools, PPE, and storage destination.
• Allowing contamination, allergen contact, temperature loss, or unapproved substitutions.
• Producing or restocking without checking on-hand product, shelf life, par, and expected demand.
• Failing to label, date, cool, store, and record the completed product.
• Leaving waste, shortage, equipment, or unfinished work without manager handoff.

45. Prep Area Closing SOP

SOP ID V2-045 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To close the prep station cleanly, safely, and completely so product is stored properly and the area is
ready for the next use.
APPLIES TO: Prep cook, cook, kitchen lead, dishwasher if assigned, or manager on duty.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee follows the approved recipe and production plan. A lead or manager
verifies quality, yield, labeling, and exceptions.
WHEN USED: At the end of the prep shift, after assigned prep is completed, during shift handoff, or before the prep
employee leaves.
• Prep Closing Checklist
• Labels
• Food containers
• Storage areas
• Approved sanitizer
• All prep is stored correctly, labeled, dated, cleaned up, communicated, and approved by the manager or kitchen lead before
the employee leaves.
• Store food at safe temperatures.
• Label and date all required product.
• Do not leave food on prep tables.
1. Review completed and incomplete prep with manager or kitchen lead.
2. Make sure all prepared product is labeled and dated.
3. Store all product in the correct location.
4. Communicate low product, missing product, waste, or incomplete prep.
5. Remove food scraps, packaging, and trash from prep area.
6. Send dirty tools, pans, containers, and cutting boards to dish or clean according to policy.
7. Clean and sanitize prep tables.
8. Clean scales, measuring tools, and equipment according to procedure.
9. Return labels, markers, containers, and supplies to correct locations.
10. Sweep and mop if assigned. Remove trash if assigned. Check that no food is left out. Ask manager or kitchen lead for final.

PART 3

COOK LINE & RECIPE EXECUTION

25 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control line setup, ticket flow, recipes, temperatures, plating, allergy orders, holding, station
equipment, cleaning, and closing.


46. Cook Line Opening SOP

SOP ID V2-046 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To open the cook line with clean and safe equipment, correctly held product, calibrated tools, stocked
stations, clear assignments, and manager release.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review the opening checklist, manager notes, menu changes, outages, reservations, expected volume, and staffing plan.
2. Inspect the line for cleanliness, pests, leaks, damage, grease, blocked exits, and unresolved closing issues.
3. Verify ventilation, fire-suppression status, gas/electrical condition, refrigeration, hot equipment, lighting, and hand sinks.
4. Confirm each station has approved recipes, utensils, thermometers, timers, labels, towels, sanitizer, and PPE.
5. Check product identity, date marks, quality, storage order, thaw status, pars, and allergy-order supplies.
6. Preheat or start equipment only according to manufacturer instructions and the approved startup sequence.
7. Verify required holding and equipment temperatures before loading food or beginning production.
8. Assign stations, backups, communication calls, allergy-order leadership, restocking, and break coverage.
9. Correct shortages or hazards and document equipment, product, or staffing limits before service.
10. Complete a manager line walk and authorize the cook line to open only when critical standards are met.


46. Cook Line Opening SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for cook
line opening.
□ Inspect the line for cleanliness, pests, leaks, damage, grease, blocked exits, and unresolved closing issues.
□ Confirm each station has approved recipes, utensils, thermometers, timers, labels, towels, sanitizer, and PPE.
□ Preheat or start equipment only according to manufacturer instructions and the approved startup sequence.
□ Assign stations, backups, communication calls, allergy-order leadership, restocking, and break coverage.
□ Complete a manager line walk and authorize the cook line to open only when critical standards are met.
• Starting cook line opening without the station, ticket, recipe, equipment, tools, product, and communication plan ready.
• Losing ticket sequence, modifiers, allergy information, portion, temperature, or plate standards.
• Continuing when equipment, product, staffing, or timing cannot support safe execution.
• Failing to communicate delays, remakes, shortages, and completed items.
• Ending without restocking, cleaning, product disposition, and manager verification.

47. Station Setup SOP

SOP ID V2-047 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To set up each cook-line station according to the menu, recipe, equipment, product, sanitation, safety,
and expected-volume requirements.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review the station assignment, menu, recipes, projected volume, specials, and known product or equipment limits.
2. Clean and sanitize the work area, food-contact surfaces, handles, utensils, and required smallwares.
3. Inspect assigned equipment, guards, cords, gas connections, controls, refrigeration, and ventilation before use.
4. Gather only approved ingredients and verify labels, dates, quality, thaw status, and storage hierarchy.
5. Stock the station to the approved opening par without blocking airflow, access, handwashing, or safe movement.
6. Arrange ingredients, tools, pans, towels, sanitizer, thermometers, timers, and waste containers in the approved layout.
7. Preheat equipment and verify operating or holding temperatures before placing food into service.
8. Separate raw, ready-to-eat, and allergen-sensitive work and establish the approved clean-tool process.
9. Test communication with expo and identify backups, substitutions, and restocking responsibility.
10. Ask the lead or manager to verify the station setup before accepting the first ticket.

48. Ticket Reading SOP

SOP ID V2-048 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure cooks read, understand, prioritize, and execute tickets correctly.
APPLIES TO: Cook, expo, kitchen lead, trainer, or manager on duty.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: Any time a ticket appears on a kitchen printer, kitchen display system, expo screen, or handwritten
backup process.
• Kitchen printer or display system
• Ticket rail if applicable
• Expo screen if applicable
• POS ticket
• Recipe knowledge
• Tickets are read accurately, modifiers are followed, allergies are recognized, and items are executed in proper timing.
• Allergy tickets must be handled according to restaurant policy.
• Do not guess on unclear modifiers.
• Ask the expo, server, or manager for clarification when needed.
1. Read the full ticket before starting.
2. Confirm table number, order time, and item count if applicable.
3. Identify items assigned to your station.
4. Read modifiers carefully.
5. Identify allergies or special instructions.
6. Ask for clarification if the ticket is unclear.
7. Communicate with expo or other stations if timing is needed.
8. Start items according to priority and cooking time.
9. Track ticket progress during the rush.
10. Call out concerns, delays, missing product, modifiers, and allergy details; verify completed items and organize tickets
according to the approved system.


48. Ticket Reading SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for ticket
reading.
□ Confirm table number, order time, and item count if applicable.
□ Read modifiers carefully.
□ Ask for clarification if the ticket is unclear.
□ Start items according to priority and cooking time.
□ Call out concerns, delays, missing product, modifiers, and allergy details; verify completed items and organize tickets
according to the approved system.
• Performing ticket reading without confirming the ticket, station, recipe, modifiers, seat/table, and timing.
• Guessing, skipping a call, or changing sequence without communicating with expo and affected stations.
• Sending food with wrong portion, temperature, garnish, presentation, or allergy control.
• Hiding delays, shortages, equipment problems, or remakes until service has already failed.
• Closing the ticket or handoff without final verification and required documentation.

49. Order Firing SOP

SOP ID V2-049 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To fire orders in the approved sequence so all components finish together at the required quality,
temperature, and ticket time.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review the station assignment, tickets, recipes, portions, allergens, shortages, and expected volume.
2. Wash hands and prepare clean tools, thermometers, timers, sanitation controls, and safe equipment.
3. Inspect and organize dated product by safe storage, cook sequence, and service priority.
4. Confirm ticket priority, course timing, cook times, and station readiness.
5. Call or acknowledge the fire using the approved kitchen language.
6. Start items in the sequence that allows the table to finish together.
7. Update expo immediately about delays, remakes, shortages, or changed timing.
8. Verify the complete item or station result against the ticket and quality standard.
9. Communicate and document remakes, delays, waste, shortages, equipment, and allergy exceptions.
10. Reset tools, product, sanitation, and station readiness for the next ticket or shift.

51. Cooking Temperature Verification SOP

SOP ID V2-051 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify cooking temperatures at the correct product location with a clean, calibrated thermometer and
take immediate corrective action when limits are missed.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Use a sanitized calibrated thermometer in the thickest or slowest-heating portion.
5. Check each required product or batch without relying on color, time, or appearance alone.
6. Compare the result to the current approved cooking requirement and record when required.
7. Continue cooking, hold for manager evaluation, or discard product that does not meet the standard.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.


51. Cooking Temperature Verification SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for
cooking temperature verification.
□ Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
□ Use a sanitized calibrated thermometer in the thickest or slowest-heating portion.
□ Compare the result to the current approved cooking requirement and record when required.
□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Checking cooking temperature verification with an unclean, uncalibrated, or inappropriate thermometer.
• Measuring only the equipment display, container surface, or an unrepresentative location.
• Delaying the reading or recording a rounded, estimated, or backfilled result.
• Keeping product in service when the required limit or time history is uncertain.
• Failing to document corrective action, product disposition, and manager verification.
• Approved station diagram, recipe, ticket, modifier, or plate standard: ____________________________
• Equipment, portion tools, thermometer, timer, or communication system: __________________________
• Cooking, holding, quality, portion, or ticket-time limit: ________________________________________
• Allergy-order, special-request, remake, or expo verification role: _______________________________
• Manufacturer, fire-safety, food-code, or local equipment requirement reviewed: __________________

• Follow allergy order verification procedures.

7. Check portion appearance.
8. Check temperature if required.
9. Check packaging if order is to-go.
10. Confirm allergy and special-request controls when applicable, correct defects before release, and notify the manager or expo
about repeated quality failures.

53. Plate Presentation SOP

SOP ID V2-053 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To plate each item with the correct portion, components, temperature, garnish, cleanliness, and
approved presentation before release.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review the station assignment, tickets, recipes, portions, allergens, shortages, and expected volume.
2. Wash hands and prepare clean tools, thermometers, timers, sanitation controls, and safe equipment.
3. Inspect and organize dated product by safe storage, cook sequence, and service priority.
4. Confirm the correct plate or package, item, modifiers, sides, and garnish.
5. Place portions in the approved position without smears, spills, or unsafe contact.
6. Verify temperature, color, texture, cleanliness, stability, and completeness.
7. Correct the plate before the service window and communicate any delay.
8. Verify the complete item or station result against the ticket and quality standard.
9. Communicate and document remakes, delays, waste, shortages, equipment, and allergy exceptions.
10. Reset tools, product, sanitation, and station readiness for the next ticket or shift.

54. Portion Control on the Line SOP

SOP ID V2-054 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain approved portions on the cook line using specified tools, weights, counts, recipes, and
manager-verified corrections.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review the recipe card, approved portion, yield, plate standard, modifier, and ticket before plating.
2. Use only the assigned scoop, ladle, scale, count, slice, cup, or measuring tool for the item.
3. Verify the tool is clean, intact, and matched to the correct product and portion size.
4. Portion from a mixed or evenly distributed batch so the serving represents the recipe.
5. Level, drain, weigh, count, or finish the portion according to the standard rather than estimating.
6. Keep raw, ready-to-eat, and allergen-sensitive tools and containers separated.
7. Compare the plated portion with the recipe photo, weight, count, side, garnish, and container standard.
8. Correct over- or under-portioning before the plate leaves the station and record repeated variance.
9. Restock portion tools and product without combining batches or losing label and date control.
10. Verify high-cost and high-variance items during manager line checks and coaching.


54. Portion Control on the Line SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for
portion control on the line.
□ Use only the assigned scoop, ladle, scale, count, slice, cup, or measuring tool for the item.
□ Portion from a mixed or evenly distributed batch so the serving represents the recipe.
□ Keep raw, ready-to-eat, and allergen-sensitive tools and containers separated.
□ Correct over- or under-portioning before the plate leaves the station and record repeated variance.
□ Verify high-cost and high-variance items during manager line checks and coaching.
• Performing portion control on the line from memory instead of the approved recipe, yield, portion, and batch standard.
• Using unclean tools, wrong ingredients, unverified product, or an unprotected work surface.
• Losing time-temperature, raw-to-ready-to-eat, or allergen separation during production.
• Failing to record actual yield, waste, shortage, substitution, or overproduction.
• Storing completed product without correct cooling, labeling, dating, and manager verification.

55. Allergy Order SOP

SOP ID V2-055 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To execute allergy orders only after the reported allergen, manager control, clean preparation method,
ingredients, and verified delivery process are confirmed.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Notify the manager or designated food-safety leader before proceeding with an uncertain high-risk condition.
2. Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
3. Isolate affected food and prevent sale, use, movement, or disposal until authorized.
4. Read the allergy ticket aloud with the manager or designated allergy-order leader.
5. Wash hands and establish a cleaned and sanitized protected station with separate tools and ingredients.
6. Prepare the item without unapproved substitutions or contact with shared surfaces, fryers, utensils, or garnishes.
7. Mark, verify, and hand the completed order directly to the designated employee.
8. Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
9. Record all measurements, time, product, quantity, disposition, decision, and responsible people.
10. Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.


55. Allergy Order SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for
allergy order.
□ Identify the product, lot, time, temperature, exposure, and applicable approved limit.
□ Read the allergy ticket aloud with the manager or designated allergy-order leader.
□ Prepare the item without unapproved substitutions or contact with shared surfaces, fryers, utensils, or garnishes.
□ Management determines approved correction, discard, closure, or authority notification.
□ Verify the affected process is safe before resuming production or service.
• Beginning allergy order without direct confirmation of the request, recipe, ingredients, and designated leader.
• Allowing shared tools, surfaces, gloves, towels, fryers, or garnishes to compromise the order.
• Guessing about an ingredient or removing visible pieces instead of remaking.
• Losing the order's identity or direct handoff during a rush.
• Serving before designated verification of ingredients, preparation, plate, and destination.

56. Special Request Order SOP

SOP ID V2-056 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To execute approved special requests accurately without weakening allergy, food-safety, recipe,
pricing, timing, or quality controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Read the entire special request and identify exactly what is added, removed, substituted, cooked differently, or separated.
2. Confirm the request is available, safe, and within restaurant authority before promising it to the guest.
3. Clarify unclear wording through expo or the server and distinguish preference from allergy or medical concern.
4. Mark the ticket and communicate the request directly to every affected station.
5. Verify ingredients, recipe changes, portion, price, cook time, and equipment limitations.
6. Use clean tools and controlled handling when the request changes cross-contact or food-safety risk.
7. Call out the order at firing and at completion so it does not merge with the standard item.
8. Expo verifies the modification, plate, side, garnish, and seat/table before release.
9. Stop and remake the item when the request was missed or cannot be verified.
10. Record repeated unavailable requests, pricing issues, menu confusion, and training needs.

57. Rush Communication SOP

SOP ID V2-057 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure kitchen employees communicate clearly during busy periods.
APPLIES TO: Cooks, expo, kitchen lead, prep support, dish team, and manager on duty.

and corrections.
WHEN USED: During high-volume service, ticket backups, large parties, short staffing, product shortages,
equipment problems, or whenever normal communication is no longer sufficient.
• Kitchen communication standard
• Ticket system
• Expo leadership
• All-day count process if applicable
• Low product communication
• The kitchen communicates clearly, early, and professionally during high-volume periods to protect ticket times, food.
• Do not sacrifice food safety during rushes.
• Do not skip handwashing or sanitation steps.
• Do not ignore allergy procedures.
1. Call back important ticket information according to kitchen standard.
2. Communicate low product before running out.
3. Communicate delays early.
4. Ask for help before the station is overwhelmed.
5. Tell expo or manager when ticket times are at risk.
6. Communicate all-day counts if used.
7. Communicate equipment problems immediately.
8. Communicate food quality concerns before sending food.
9. Keep language respectful and direct.
10. Avoid blame during rushes. Focus on solving the immediate problem. Review breakdowns after the rush when needed.

58. Expo Communication SOP

SOP ID V2-058 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep expo and cook-line employees aligned on ticket sequence, missing items, timing, allergens,
remakes, quality, and table completion.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.

• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers

4. Track fired, working, delayed, completed, remade, and held items without creating duplicate calls.
5. Communicate changes immediately, including added items, cancellations, shortages, and revised timing.
6. Escalate allergy orders, severe delays, equipment failure, product shortages, and cross-station conflicts.
7. Coordinate course completion so hot and cold items reach the pass together at the correct quality.
8. Keep the pass organized by ticket and prevent employees from removing unverified plates.
9. Give the runner a complete handoff with table, seat, allergy, missing item, and timing information.
10. Record recurring communication failures and review them with the kitchen and floor managers.

59. Cook Line Restocking SOP

SOP ID V2-059 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure cook line stations stay stocked with the right amount of product during service without
over-pulling or creating waste.
APPLIES TO: Station cook, prep cook, kitchen lead, runner, manager, or assigned support employee.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: Before service, during service, before rush periods, after rush periods, during low product alerts, and
before shift handoff.
• Par list
• Backup product
• Labels
• Food containers
• Gloves if required
• The station stays stocked for service without over-pulling, using unsafe product, or creating unnecessary waste.
• Keep food at safe temperatures.
• Do not leave product out too long.
• Label and date product when required.
1. Check current product level at the station.
2. Compare product level to expected volume and par standard.
3. Communicate low product before running out.
4. Pull backup product only as needed.
5. Check label, date, and quality before using product.
6. Follow FIFO when selecting product.
7. Keep product at safe temperature during restocking.
8. Place product in correct station location.
9. Do not overfill pans or containers beyond restaurant standard.
10. Label newly prepared or transferred product when required and return safe unused backups to the correct storage location.

60. Hot Holding SOP

SOP ID V2-060 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control hot holding consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and exceptions
receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.
• Current regulatory and restaurant standard
• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The current approved regulatory and restaurant limit controls the process.
• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.
• Corrective action and disposition are recorded at the time of the event.
• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Verify the product was fully cooked or reheated using the approved process before it enters hot holding.
2. Preheat the holding unit and confirm it is clean, operating, and capable of maintaining the required limit.
3. Transfer food in clean covered containers using shallow batches, correct utensils, and protected handling.
4. Label the product with identity, start time, discard or review time, and any batch or allergen information.
5. Place containers to allow heat circulation; do not overload, combine old and new batches, or use holding to finish cooking.
6. Measure food temperature at the required frequency with a clean, calibrated thermometer.
7. Stir, rotate, cover, or adjust the unit only according to the recipe, equipment instructions, and approved corrective action.
8. Hold, rapidly reheat, discard, or escalate any product outside the approved limit based on documented time and temperature.
9. Record temperatures, times, employee initials, corrective actions, and manager verification.
10. Discard or properly cool remaining food at the approved end point; clean and reset the holding unit.

holding.
□ Preheat the holding unit and confirm it is clean, operating, and capable of maintaining the required limit.
□ Label the product with identity, start time, discard or review time, and any batch or allergen information.
□ Measure food temperature at the required frequency with a clean, calibrated thermometer.
□ Hold, rapidly reheat, discard, or escalate any product outside the approved limit based on documented time and
temperature.
□ Discard or properly cool remaining food at the approved end point; clean and reset the holding unit.
• Starting hot holding without the station, ticket, recipe, equipment, tools, product, and communication plan ready.
• Losing ticket sequence, modifiers, allergy information, portion, temperature, or plate standards.
• Continuing when equipment, product, staffing, or timing cannot support safe execution.
• Failing to communicate delays, remakes, shortages, and completed items.
• Ending without restocking, cleaning, product disposition, and manager verification.

61. Cold Holding SOP

SOP ID V2-061 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control cold holding consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and exceptions
receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Trained food employees, kitchen leads, managers, and designated food-safety leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The employee follows the approved high-risk control. Management verifies limits, disposition,
records, and required regulatory contact.
WHEN USED: Whenever the named high-risk process is performed or a required limit, control, or record is
uncertain or missed.

• Calibrated thermometer, timer, or approved test device
• Protected containers, labels, PPE, and sanitizer
• Corrective-action and food-disposition record

• Questionable food is isolated and not served until management determines disposition.

• Management contacts the proper authority when required by law, permit, policy, or incident severity.
1. Verify the product was cooled, received, or prepared within the approved process before it enters cold holding.
2. Confirm the cold unit is clean, organized, and operating within the restaurant's approved limit.
3. Use clean covered containers, labels, date marks, and storage placement that prevent contamination and allergen cross-
contact.
4. Keep product in refrigeration until needed and return working containers promptly between uses.
5. Do not overfill pans, block airflow, place hot food into overloaded units, or store product above raw-item hierarchy limits.
6. Measure food temperature at the required frequency with a clean, calibrated thermometer.
7. Use ice, smaller pans, alternate refrigeration, or another approved control only when the process is authorized and monitored.
8. Hold, rapidly cool, transfer, discard, or escalate any product outside the approved limit based on documented history.
9. Record temperatures, times, employee initials, corrective actions, and manager verification.
10. At closing, evaluate remaining product, protect labels and dates, and clean the unit and surrounding area.

62. Fryer Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-062 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the fryer station safely and consistently according to equipment instructions, recipes,
temperatures, ticket priorities, and cleaning controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review fryer assignments, oil type, product compatibility, cook settings, filtration status, and known maintenance issues.
2. Inspect the fryer, baskets, handles, guards, thermostat, drain valve, oil level, floor, and fire-suppression area.
3. Confirm the unit is dry and free of water, ice buildup, packaging, loose debris, and nearby trip hazards.
4. Heat oil only through the approved startup sequence and verify operating temperature before cooking.
5. Load dry product within basket capacity and lower it slowly to prevent boil-over and splash.
6. Use the approved timer and temperature and keep allergen or product-separation rules active.
7. Skim crumbs, monitor oil quality and level, and stop for smoke, foaming, leak, control failure, or abnormal odor.
8. Lift and drain baskets safely and transfer food to the approved holding or plating area.
9. Record waste, oil addition, filter need, temperature failure, spill, burn, or equipment concern.
10. Follow the approved idle, filtration, shutdown, and cleaning process at the end of use.


62. Fryer Operation SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the station, ticket or recipe standard, safety limit, quality result, and escalation point for fryer
operation.
□ Inspect the fryer, baskets, handles, guards, thermostat, drain valve, oil level, floor, and fire-suppression area.
□ Heat oil only through the approved startup sequence and verify operating temperature before cooking.
□ Use the approved timer and temperature and keep allergen or product-separation rules active.
□ Lift and drain baskets safely and transfer food to the approved holding or plating area.
□ Follow the approved idle, filtration, shutdown, and cleaning process at the end of use.
• Operating fryer operation without training, manufacturer instructions, pre-use inspection, and required PPE.
• Overloading equipment, bypassing guards, or using unapproved temperatures, tools, or containers.
• Mixing raw and ready-to-eat work or losing allergy-order controls.
• Continuing after abnormal flame, heat, sound, odor, leak, damage, or control failure.
• Leaving the station without shutdown, cleaning, product control, and manager handoff.

63. Grill Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-063 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the grill station safely and consistently according to equipment instructions, recipes,
temperatures, ticket priorities, and cleaning controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review grill assignments, recipes, doneness standards, zone layout, volume, and known equipment conditions.
2. Inspect grates, burners, ignition, flame, guards, grease tray, tools, ventilation, and surrounding floor.
3. Preheat the grill and establish approved heat zones before loading food.
4. Use separate clean tools and designated areas for raw, cooked, ready-to-eat, and allergen-sensitive items.
5. Place product according to zone and ticket sequence without overcrowding or creating unsafe flare-ups.
6. Turn, move, and finish product using recipe timing and a calibrated thermometer when required.
7. Prevent cross-contact by controlling brushes, towels, seasonings, pans, and shared surfaces.
8. Stop and report gas odor, failed ignition, uneven flame, excessive smoke, grease fire, or damaged grates.
9. Verify doneness, rest time, portion, quality, and correct ticket before transfer.
10. Scrape and reset the grill between products as required and complete approved shutdown and cleaning.

64. Saute Station Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-064 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the saute station safely and consistently according to equipment instructions, recipes,
temperatures, ticket priorities, and cleaning controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as

• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment

1. Review saute assignments, recipes, pan sizes, burner zones, ingredients, sauces, garnishes, and expected volume.
2. Inspect burners, ignition, pan handles, utensils, hood operation, refrigeration, and surrounding work area.
3. Stage measured ingredients in the approved sequence while protecting cold holding and allergen separation.
4. Select the correct clean pan and preheat only to the recipe requirement; never leave an empty pan unattended.
5. Add oil, ingredients, and liquids carefully to control flame, steam, splash, and boil-over.
6. Cook in ticket order using approved timing, agitation, reduction, and temperature checks.
7. Use dedicated or freshly sanitized tools and pans for allergy orders and verified special requests.
8. Stop for flare-up, gas odor, failed burner, damaged handle, excessive smoke, or uncontrolled spill.
9. Verify sauce consistency, doneness, portion, garnish, and ticket before plating.
10. Remove pans safely, control hot handles, clean the station continuously, and complete shutdown and handoff.

66. Oven Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-066 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the oven station safely and consistently according to equipment instructions, recipes,
temperatures, ticket priorities, and cleaning controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.
• Current tickets, recipes, and station assignment
• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication
• Tickets, recipes, modifiers, portions, temperatures, and plate standards are followed exactly.
• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review oven assignments, recipes, rack positions, preheat setting, cook time, batch load, and known maintenance issues.
2. Inspect door, gasket, racks, controls, thermometer, fan, lights, cords or gas connection, and ventilation.
3. Preheat to the approved setting and verify actual operating temperature before loading food.
4. Use approved pans, spacing, rack positions, and load limits so heat can circulate.
5. Label or identify timers and batches to prevent wrong product, missed rotation, or overcooking.
6. Open the door carefully, stand clear of steam or heat, and use required heat-resistant PPE.
7. Check internal temperature and quality at the recipe point rather than relying only on time or the display.
8. Stop and report failed heat, hot spots, smoke, gas odor, fan noise, damaged gasket, or control error.
9. Remove and transfer product safely and verify batch identity, yield, quality, and destination.
10. Clean spills when safe, record deviations, and follow the approved idle or shutdown process.

67. Pizza Station Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-067 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the pizza station safely and consistently according to approved dough, portions, toppings,
bake standards, ticket priorities, and cleaning controls.
APPLIES TO: Line cooks, expo employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: During line setup, active cooking, ticket execution, holding, restocking, cleaning, or closing as
applicable.

• Clean utensils, portion tools, and equipment
• Calibrated thermometer and timers
• Allergy and special-request communication tools
• Expo and manager communication

• Allergy and special-request orders receive direct communication and controlled handling.
• Unsafe, undercooked, overcooked, contaminated, or poor-quality food is not served.
• Delays, shortages, equipment problems, and remakes are communicated immediately.
1. Review dough, sauce, cheese, toppings, portion charts, oven settings, ticket sequence, and expected volume.

4. Apply sauce, cheese, proteins, vegetables, and garnishes with the approved tools, weights, and distribution pattern.
5. Keep raw proteins, ready-to-eat toppings, and allergy-order tools separated.
6. Identify the order, size, modifications, and oven position before loading.
7. Bake using the approved time and temperature and rotate only through the approved method.
8. Check crust, center, topping doneness, cheese, presentation, and required internal temperature.
9. Cut with a clean approved cutter, protect allergy orders, verify the ticket, and transfer to the correct package or serviceware.
10. Record remakes and waste, restock safely, and complete oven, line, tool, and floor cleaning.

69. Cook Line Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-069 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep the cook line clean, sanitary, organized, and safe during service and before closing.
APPLIES TO: Station cook, kitchen lead, prep support, dishwasher if assigned, or manager on duty.
RESPONSIBILITY: The cook executes the ticket and recipe. Expo or management verifies timing, quality, safety,
and corrections.
WHEN USED: Before service, during service, after rush periods, during shift handoff, and during closing.
• Approved sanitizer
• Clean towels
• Gloves if required
• Scrapers if applicable
• Broom
• The cook line remains clean, organized, safe, and sanitary throughout the shift and is properly cleaned before closing.
• Use chemicals according to label and restaurant policy.
• Do not mix chemicals.
• Keep chemicals away from food.
1. Set up sanitizer and clean towels.
2. Keep the station organized during service.
3. Remove trash, empty packaging, and unnecessary items.
4. Wipe spills quickly.
5. Keep cutting boards, tools, and food-contact surfaces clean according to policy.
6. Separate dirty tools from clean tools.
7. Remove dropped or contaminated food properly.
8. Sweep or clean floor hazards when safe to do so.
9. Clean during slow periods instead of waiting until close.
10. Protect food before chemical use and report pests, leaks, broken tools, unsafe equipment, or repeated cleaning failures to
the manager.

to the manager.

• Failing to communicate delays, remakes, shortages, and completed items.

PART 4

FOOD SAFETY & ALLERGEN CONTROL

25 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control employee hygiene, illness reporting, sanitizing, time and temperature, allergens,
contaminated food, pests, water notices, and inspections.


71. Handwashing SOP

SOP ID V2-071 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control handwashing consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and exceptions
receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Each employee follows the personal-health and hygiene standard. Management removes
unsafe work and follows required reporting or exclusion procedures.
WHEN USED: Before food work, whenever contamination may occur, and whenever an employee health or hygiene
concern is reported.
• Current employee-health and hygiene policy
• Hand sink, soap, warm water, and single-use towels
• Approved gloves or utensils when required
• Manager reporting and exclusion information
• Training and corrective-action record
• Employees report health conditions and follow exclusion or restriction decisions.
• Hands, gloves, utensils, clothing, and personal items are controlled to protect food.
• Gloves never replace required handwashing.
• Management stops unsafe food handling and documents required corrective action.
1. Stop food handling when the hygiene or health requirement cannot be met.
2. Review the current employee-health or hygiene standard and notify management when required.
3. Prepare approved handwashing, glove, utensil, clothing, or reporting controls.
4. Wet hands and exposed forearms with approved running water.
5. Apply soap and scrub all surfaces, including fingertips, thumbs, nails, and between fingers, for the approved time.
6. Rinse thoroughly and dry with a single-use towel or approved dryer.
7. Use the towel to avoid recontaminating hands when required and repeat whenever contamination occurs.
8. Discard contaminated barriers or food and clean and sanitize affected surfaces as required.
9. Report the condition and follow manager direction on restriction, retraining, or corrective action.
10. Document only required facts and protect employee privacy.

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for handwashing.
□ Review the current employee-health or hygiene standard and notify management when required.
□ Wet hands and exposed forearms with approved running water.
□ Rinse thoroughly and dry with a single-use towel or approved dryer.
□ Discard contaminated barriers or food and clean and sanitize affected surfaces as required.
□ Document only required facts and protect employee privacy.
• Treating handwashing as optional during busy periods or after contamination events.
• Using gloves as a substitute for handwashing or touching unclean surfaces and returning to food.
• Allowing an ill employee to work or return without required manager evaluation and clearance.
• Handling ready-to-eat food with unapproved bare-hand contact.
• Failing to document reporting, exclusion/restriction, corrective action, and retraining.

72. Glove Use SOP

SOP ID V2-072 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure gloves are used correctly when handling food or performing tasks that require gloves.
APPLIES TO: Cooks, prep cooks, dish employees when handling clean items, servers when handling food as
required, bartenders when handling.
RESPONSIBILITY: Each employee follows the personal-health and hygiene standard. Management removes
unsafe work and follows required reporting or exclusion procedures.
WHEN USED: When handling ready-to-eat food if required by policy
• Disposable gloves
• Approved glove storage area
• Handwashing sink
• Soap
• Paper towels or approved drying method
• Gloves are used only after handwashing, changed when needed, and never used as a replacement for clean hands.
• Gloves do not replace handwashing.
• Hands must be washed before putting on gloves.
• Gloves must be changed when contaminated.
1. Wash hands before putting on gloves.
2. Dry hands properly.
3. Select the correct glove size if available.
4. Put gloves on without contaminating the glove surface.
5. Use gloves only for the assigned task.
6. Change gloves when switching tasks.
7. Change gloves after touching raw food, trash, face, hair, phone, clothing, chemicals, money, or contaminated surfaces.
8. Change gloves if torn, dirty, or damaged.
9. Remove gloves properly.
10. Throw gloves away after use. Wash hands before putting on new gloves.

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for glove use.
□ Dry hands properly.
□ Put gloves on without contaminating the glove surface.
□ Change gloves when switching tasks.
□ Change gloves if torn, dirty, or damaged.
□ Throw gloves away after use. Wash hands before putting on new gloves.

73. Bare-Hand Contact Prevention SOP

SOP ID V2-073 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prevent bare-hand contact with ready-to-eat food by using approved utensils, gloves, deli tissue, and
immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Each employee follows the personal-health and hygiene standard. Management removes
unsafe work and follows required reporting or exclusion procedures.
WHEN USED: Before food work, whenever contamination may occur, and whenever an employee health or hygiene
concern is reported.
• Current employee-health and hygiene policy
• Hand sink, soap, warm water, and single-use towels
• Approved gloves or utensils when required
• Manager reporting and exclusion information
• Training and corrective-action record
• Employees report health conditions and follow exclusion or restriction decisions.
• Hands, gloves, utensils, clothing, and personal items are controlled to protect food.
• Gloves never replace required handwashing.
• Management stops unsafe food handling and documents required corrective action.
1. Stop food handling when the hygiene or health requirement cannot be met.
2. Review the current employee-health or hygiene standard and notify management when required.
3. Prepare approved handwashing, glove, utensil, clothing, or reporting controls.
4. Identify ready-to-eat food and use approved gloves, deli tissue, tongs, spatulas, or other utensils.
5. Keep utensil handles and dispensing points from contacting food or contaminated surfaces.
6. Change the barrier whenever it is contaminated, damaged, or used for another product.
7. Stop and discard affected food after unapproved bare-hand contact unless management confirms an allowed corrective action.
8. Discard contaminated barriers or food and clean and sanitize affected surfaces as required.
9. Report the condition and follow manager direction on restriction, retraining, or corrective action.
10. Document only required facts and protect employee privacy.

APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.

SOP ID V2-075 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control ill employee reporting consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Each employee follows the personal-health and hygiene standard. Management removes
unsafe work and follows required reporting or exclusion procedures.
WHEN USED: Before food work, whenever contamination may occur, and whenever an employee health or hygiene

5. Provide only the information required by the employee-health policy and local authority.

8. Discard contaminated barriers or food and clean and sanitize affected surfaces as required.
9. Report the condition and follow manager direction on restriction, retraining, or corrective action.

77. Sanitizer Bucket SOP

SOP ID V2-077 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control sanitizer bucket consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Prepare sanitizer only in a clean labeled container using the approved chemical and dilution system.
5. Test concentration with the correct test strip and record or correct the result.
6. Store wiping cloths fully submerged and keep the bucket away from food and guest access.
7. Replace solution when dirty, weak, strong, contaminated, or at the required interval.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.


77. Sanitizer Bucket SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for sanitizer bucket.
□ Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
□ Prepare sanitizer only in a clean labeled container using the approved chemical and dilution system.
□ Store wiping cloths fully submerged and keep the bucket away from food and guest access.
□ Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
□ Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.
• Performing sanitizer bucket without the current approved limit, clean tools, test method, timer, and record.
• Guessing temperature, concentration, contact time, or elapsed time instead of measuring.
• Continuing with an out-of-limit result without approved corrective action.
• Backfilling, altering, or omitting monitoring records.
• Failing to verify product disposition, sanitation, equipment condition, and manager review.
• Approved food-safety limit, monitoring frequency, or corrective action: ___________________________
• Thermometer, test strip, timer, sanitizer, label, or monitoring log: _____________________________

• Restaurant-specific product, process, equipment, or record adjustments: _________________________

79. Food Contact Surface Sanitizing SOP

SOP ID V2-079 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To wash, rinse, and sanitize food-contact surfaces with approved methods, concentrations, contact
times, records, and immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Remove food and soil, wash the surface, rinse when required, and apply approved sanitizer.
5. Maintain the required concentration, coverage, and contact time without wiping it off too soon.
6. Allow the surface to air-dry and protect it from recontamination.
7. Retest solution and repeat the process after contamination, task changes, or failed verification.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.

80. Food Temperature Check SOP

SOP ID V2-080 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control food temperature check consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Sanitize the calibrated probe and select the correct measurement location.
5. Measure the thickest, coldest, hottest, or most representative portion as required.
6. Wait for a stable reading and record product, time, temperature, employee, and corrective action.
7. Sanitize the probe and hold, reheat, chill, cook, or discard product under the approved rule.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.


80. Food Temperature Check SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for food temperature check.
□ Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
□ Sanitize the calibrated probe and select the correct measurement location.
□ Wait for a stable reading and record product, time, temperature, employee, and corrective action.
□ Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
□ Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.
• Checking food temperature check with an unclean, uncalibrated, or inappropriate thermometer.
• Measuring only the equipment display, container surface, or an unrepresentative location.
• Delaying the reading or recording a rounded, estimated, or backfilled result.
• Keeping product in service when the required limit or time history is uncertain.
• Failing to document corrective action, product disposition, and manager verification.
• Approved food-safety limit, monitoring frequency, or corrective action: ___________________________
• Thermometer, test strip, timer, sanitizer, label, or monitoring log: _____________________________
• Person in charge, food-safety leader, or authority contact: _____________________________________

81. Thermometer Calibration SOP

SOP ID V2-081 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control thermometer calibration consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Use the approved ice-point, boiling-point, or manufacturer calibration method.
5. Prepare the test medium correctly and keep the sensing area from touching the container.
6. Compare the stable reading to the expected reference and adjust when the device permits.
7. Tag and replace a thermometer that cannot be calibrated, then record the check.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.

82. Cooling Food SOP

SOP ID V2-082 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure food is cooled safely according to restaurant policy and food safety requirements.
APPLIES TO: Cook, prep cook, kitchen lead, manager, or certified food safety employee if required.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: Any time cooked or heated food is cooled for later storage or use.
• Food thermometer
• Cooling log
• Shallow pans if used
• Ice bath if used
• Labels
• Food is cooled safely, monitored, recorded if required, labeled, and stored according to restaurant policy.
• Follow restaurant and health department cooling standards.
• Do not cover food tightly before it is cooled if policy requires venting.
• Do not place large hot containers directly into storage without approved cooling method.
1. Identify food that requires cooling.
2. Use the approved cooling method.
3. Portion food into shallow containers if required.
4. Use ice bath, stirring, venting, or other approved method as required.
5. Check starting temperature if required.
6. Record time and temperature if required.
7. Monitor cooling progress.
8. Recheck temperature according to policy.
9. Label and date food when ready for storage.
10. Store food properly after cooling standard is met. Notify manager if food does not cool properly.


82. Cooling Food SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for cooling food.
□ Use the approved cooling method.
□ Use ice bath, stirring, venting, or other approved method as required.
□ Record time and temperature if required.
□ Recheck temperature according to policy.
□ Store food properly after cooling standard is met. Notify manager if food does not cool properly.
• Beginning cooling food without the approved time-temperature method, container depth, label, and storage space.
• Using room temperature, deep covered containers, standing water, or another unapproved method.
• Combining warm and cold product or overloading refrigeration and airflow.
• Failing to monitor and record required times and temperatures.
• Keeping product when the control history or limit is uncertain instead of escalating.

83. Reheating Food SOP

SOP ID V2-083 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure food is reheated safely and correctly before service.
APPLIES TO: Cook, prep cook, kitchen lead, manager, or certified food safety employee if required.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: Any time previously cooked or cooled food is reheated for hot holding or service.
• Food thermometer
• Reheating log if used
• Approved reheating equipment
• Clean utensils
• Timer if needed
• Food is reheated using an approved method, reaches required temperature, and is safe before service or hot holding.
• Follow restaurant and health department reheating standards.
• Use only approved reheating methods.
• Do not reheat food in equipment not approved for reheating if policy prohibits it.
1. Confirm the food is labeled, dated, and safe to reheat.
2. Use approved reheating equipment.
3. Reheat food according to restaurant standard.
4. Stir or rotate product if required for even heating.
5. Check internal temperature with clean thermometer.
6. Record temperature if required.
7. Continue reheating if temperature standard is not met.
8. Do not serve until standard is reached.
9. Move food to hot holding or service area only after approved temperature is reached.
10. Notify manager of any safety, quality, or temperature concern.

□ Stir or rotate product if required for even heating.

action.
• Guessing, averaging, backfilling, or altering results.
• Keeping food in service when identity, time, temperature, contamination, or control history is uncertain.
• Failing to stop work, isolate product, notify the person in charge, and complete required correction.
• Closing the record without product disposition, sanitation, documentation, and follow-up.

exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.

85. Time as a Public Health Control SOP

SOP ID V2-085 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To use time as a public health control only under an approved written procedure with marked discard
times, monitoring, and immediate disposal when limits expire.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Obtain prior management authorization and label the food with start and discard times.
5. Begin with food at the required temperature and keep it protected from contamination.
6. Monitor the marked time continuously and never return time-controlled food to temperature control for later use.
7. Serve, cook, or discard at the approved deadline and retain required written procedures.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.

86. Hot Holding Monitoring SOP

SOP ID V2-086 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control hot holding monitoring consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify every hot-held product, container, station, start time, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Confirm the thermometer is clean, calibrated, and appropriate for the product and container depth.
3. Measure the product in a representative location without contacting the pan or contaminating the food.
4. Record the actual temperature, time, employee initials, and product identity immediately.
5. Check unit setting, water level, lids, batch depth, stirring, and product turnover when a reading trends downward.
6. Recheck after any adjustment within the approved corrective-action window.
7. Rapidly reheat, transfer, discard, or hold for manager evaluation according to approved time-temperature history.
8. Do not average readings, backfill logs, or change a recorded result after the fact.
9. Notify the manager about repeated loss of control, equipment weakness, or uncertain product history.
10. Verify the final disposition and retain the completed monitoring record.
