88. Allergen Communication SOP

SOP ID V2-088 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure allergy concerns are communicated clearly between guests, servers, kitchen, bar, and
management.
APPLIES TO: Host, server, bartender, cook, expo, kitchen lead, manager, or any employee who hears or handles
allergy information.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiving employee communicates the allergy exactly. The designated leader controls
preparation, verification, and delivery.
WHEN USED: Any time a guest mentions an allergy, food sensitivity, ingredient concern, or cross-contact concern.
• POS allergy notes
• Manager communication
• Recipe / ingredient information
• Kitchen ticket system
• Allergy verification form if used
• Allergy information is received, recorded, communicated, repeated back, verified, and attached to the correct guest and order
before preparation begins.
• Never guess about allergies.
• Do not treat allergies like preferences.
• Notify a manager according to policy.
1. Listen carefully when a guest mentions an allergy.
2. Confirm the allergy concern clearly.
3. Thank the guest for telling the restaurant.
4. Do not guess about ingredients or safety.
5. Notify a manager according to policy.
6. Enter allergy information clearly in the POS or written order.
7. Communicate allergy concern directly to kitchen or expo according to policy.
8. Kitchen confirms whether the item can be prepared according to policy.
9. Use required cross-contact prevention steps.
10. Verify the item before it leaves the kitchen. Deliver the item only after verification. Document allergy concern if required.


88. Allergen Communication SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for allergen communication.
□ Confirm the allergy concern clearly.
□ Do not guess about ingredients or safety.
□ Enter allergy information clearly in the POS or written order.
□ Kitchen confirms whether the item can be prepared according to policy.
□ Verify the item before it leaves the kitchen. Deliver the item only after verification. Document allergy concern if required.
• Starting allergen communication without exact allergen information, current labels, and direct manager or kitchen
confirmation.
• Assuming a menu item or substitute is safe without checking all ingredients and shared equipment.
• Allowing shared tools, gloves, towels, surfaces, fryers, or garnishes to contact the order.
• Removing visible ingredients instead of remaking after possible cross-contact.
• Serving without designated verification and direct handoff.

89. Allergen Order Preparation SOP

SOP ID V2-089 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control allergen order preparation consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified, and
exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Servers, managers, expo employees, cooks, prep employees, and designated allergy-order leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: The receiving employee communicates the allergy exactly. The designated leader controls
preparation, verification, and delivery.
WHEN USED: Whenever a guest reports an allergy or an order, ingredient, surface, utensil, or handoff may involve
allergen risk.
• Current allergen matrix and ingredient resources
• Marked allergy ticket and communication method
• Clean and sanitized tools, surfaces, and PPE
• Separate ingredients or protected preparation area
• Manager and expo verification
• Allergen information is communicated exactly and never guessed.
• Cross-contact controls include clean hands, tools, surfaces, ingredients, and protected handling.
• A designated manager or leader verifies the order before release.
• The restaurant never promises an allergen-free result beyond its approved capability.
1. Stop and obtain the exact guest-reported allergen and the designated manager or allergy-order leader.
2. Review the current recipe, ingredient labels, manufacturer information, and restaurant capability.
3. Wash hands and establish a protected cleaned and sanitized work area with separate tools and ingredients.
4. Assign a designated leader and review the allergy ticket, recipe, ingredients, and station capability.
5. Wash hands and prepare clean and sanitized tools, surfaces, gloves, and protected ingredients.
6. Control shared equipment, fryers, grills, steam, garnishes, storage, and handoffs according to policy.
7. The designated leader verifies the complete plate and delivers or hands off directly.
8. The designated leader verifies ingredients, preparation, plate, markings, and direct handoff.
9. Stop and remake whenever cross-contact or ingredient identity is uncertain.
10. Document exceptions and update the guest only through the approved manager communication.

□ Review the current recipe, ingredient labels, manufacturer information, and restaurant capability.
□ Assign a designated leader and review the allergy ticket, recipe, ingredients, and station capability.
□ Control shared equipment, fryers, grills, steam, garnishes, storage, and handoffs according to policy.
□ The designated leader verifies ingredients, preparation, plate, markings, and direct handoff.
□ Document exceptions and update the guest only through the approved manager communication.
• Starting allergen order preparation without exact allergen information, current labels, and direct manager or kitchen
confirmation.
• Assuming a menu item or substitute is safe without checking all ingredients and shared equipment.
• Allowing shared tools, gloves, towels, surfaces, fryers, or garnishes to contact the order.
• Removing visible ingredients instead of remaking after possible cross-contact.
• Serving without designated verification and direct handoff.

• Clean and sanitized tools, surfaces, and PPE
• Separate ingredients or protected preparation area
• Manager and expo verification
• Allergen information is communicated exactly and never guessed.
• Cross-contact controls include clean hands, tools, surfaces, ingredients, and protected handling.
• A designated manager or leader verifies the order before release.
• The restaurant never promises an allergen-free result beyond its approved capability.
1. Stop and obtain the exact guest-reported allergen and the designated manager or allergy-order leader.
2. Review the current recipe, ingredient labels, manufacturer information, and restaurant capability.
3. Wash hands and establish a protected cleaned and sanitized work area with separate tools and ingredients.
4. Identify the allergen and every possible shared ingredient, tool, surface, glove, towel, container, and piece of equipment.
5. Schedule or locate the work to prevent contact and clean and sanitize before preparation.
6. Use protected ingredients and dedicated or freshly sanitized tools and containers.
7. Stop and remake the order whenever cross-contact may have occurred; do not remove visible pieces and serve it.
8. The designated leader verifies ingredients, preparation, plate, markings, and direct handoff.
9. Stop and remake whenever cross-contact or ingredient identity is uncertain.
10. Document exceptions and update the guest only through the approved manager communication.


90. Cross-Contact Prevention SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for cross-contact prevention.
□ Review the current recipe, ingredient labels, manufacturer information, and restaurant capability.
□ Identify the allergen and every possible shared ingredient, tool, surface, glove, towel, container, and piece of
equipment.
□ Use protected ingredients and dedicated or freshly sanitized tools and containers.
□ The designated leader verifies ingredients, preparation, plate, markings, and direct handoff.
□ Document exceptions and update the guest only through the approved manager communication.
• Starting cross-contact prevention without exact allergen information, current labels, and direct manager or kitchen
confirmation.
• Assuming a menu item or substitute is safe without checking all ingredients and shared equipment.
• Allowing shared tools, gloves, towels, surfaces, fryers, or garnishes to contact the order.
• Removing visible ingredients instead of remaking after possible cross-contact.
• Serving without designated verification and direct handoff.

91. Foodborne Illness Complaint SOP

SOP ID V2-091 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To receive and document a possible foodborne-illness complaint accurately, protect relevant food and
records, complete required notifications, and assign authorized follow-up.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty, general manager, owner, or approved supervisor.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: Any time a guest claims they became sick from restaurant food or reports illness symptoms
connected to a visit.
• Illness Complaint Form
• Guest contact information
• Receipt / order information if available
• Manager log
• Health department contact process if required
• The complaint is documented accurately and escalated without diagnosis, argument, blame, or admission of fault.
• Relevant food, records, labels, lot information, logs, and schedules are preserved under authorized control.
• Required health-department, insurer, legal, ownership, or company notifications are completed by authorized management.
• Private medical and employee information is protected, and immediate food-safety failures are corrected without altering the
original record.
1. Listen calmly and record the guest's report without arguing, diagnosing, admitting fault, or assigning blame.
2. Obtain the guest's name and contact information if voluntarily provided, plus visit date, time, party size, and check details.
3. Record each food or beverage consumed, symptoms reported, symptom onset, other affected people, and any medical or
authority contact.
4. Notify the person in charge and ownership or designated food-safety leader immediately.
5. Preserve relevant guest checks, recipes, ingredient labels, lot information, delivery records, temperature logs, and employee
schedules.
6. Hold related food only under authorized direction; do not discard, sample, relabel, or move evidence unnecessarily.
7. Review required health-department, insurer, legal, or company notifications and complete them through authorized
management.
8. Protect private medical and employee information and keep witness statements separate and objective.
9. Complete immediate corrective actions for any verified food-safety control failure without concealing the original condition.
10. Assign guest follow-up, regulatory follow-up, product disposition, SOP review, and record retention.

VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and

□ Obtain the guest's name and contact information if voluntarily provided, plus visit date, time, party size, and check
details.
□ Notify the person in charge and ownership or designated food-safety leader immediately.
□ Hold related food only under authorized direction; do not discard, sample, relabel, or move evidence unnecessarily.
□ Protect private medical and employee information and keep witness statements separate and objective.
□ Assign guest follow-up, regulatory follow-up, product disposition, SOP review, and record retention.
• Arguing, diagnosing, admitting fault, or promising a result before authorized review.
• Failing to collect visit, food, symptom, timing, party, and contact information.
• Discarding or altering food, labels, logs, checks, video, or other relevant records.
• Sharing private medical or employee information with uninvolved people.
• Closing the complaint without required notification, corrective action, and assigned follow-up.

92. Contaminated Food Disposal SOP

SOP ID V2-092 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control contaminated food disposal consistently so food remains safe, required limits are verified,
and exceptions receive immediate corrective action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• The restaurant uses approved limits, calibrated tools, and complete monitoring records.
• Food outside an approved control is held, evaluated, corrected, or discarded by authorized management.
• Employees do not conceal, backfill, or alter food-safety records.
• Required health-department, ownership, or emergency notification is completed promptly.
1. Identify the product, employee, location, required limit, and monitoring frequency.
2. Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
3. Protect or isolate food and work when the condition, history, or control is uncertain.
4. Stop use and isolate the exact food, container, lot, station, and related product.
5. Mark it clearly so it cannot be returned to service or mixed with usable food.
6. Determine disposition through approved food-safety and management authority; never donate or repurpose unsafe food.
7. Record identity, quantity, reason, method, witness or approval, and corrective action.
8. Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
9. Clean and sanitize tools and affected surfaces and prevent recurrence.
10. Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.

VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and

□ Use a clean calibrated thermometer, timer, test strip, or other approved device.
□ Stop use and isolate the exact food, container, lot, station, and related product.
□ Determine disposition through approved food-safety and management authority; never donate or repurpose unsafe
food.
□ Complete the approved corrective action and record product, time, result, disposition, and manager.
□ Management reviews trends and assigns retraining, maintenance, vendor, or regulatory follow-up.
• Beginning contaminated food disposal without the approved food-safety limit, calibrated tool, monitoring frequency, and
corrective action.
• Guessing, averaging, backfilling, or altering results.
• Keeping food in service when identity, time, temperature, contamination, or control history is uncertain.
• Failing to stop work, isolate product, notify the person in charge, and complete required correction.
• Closing the record without product disposition, sanitation, documentation, and follow-up.

RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.

94. Boil Water Notice SOP

SOP ID V2-094 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect food, water-contact systems, employees, and guests during a boil-water notice or advisory
and restore operations only after approved clearance and sanitation.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response with ownership or health department guidance.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: Any time a boil water notice, advisory, or unsafe water alert affects the restaurant.
• Health department guidance
• Company policy
• Emergency contact list
• Approved safe water source if available
• Signage if required
• Affected water is not used for food, ice, beverages, handwashing, warewashing, or sanitation unless an approved alternative is
in place.
• The restaurant follows the issuing authority and local regulatory authority for restrictions, restoration, flushing, cleaning, and
reopening.
• Affected ice, food, beverages, filters, and water-contact equipment are identified and controlled.
• Operations close or remain restricted whenever required hygiene, sanitation, or safe-water controls cannot be maintained.
1. Confirm the official notice, affected area, start time, restrictions, and issuing authority.
2. Notify ownership, the person in charge, and the local regulatory authority when required.
3. Stop using affected water for drinking, ice, beverages, food preparation, produce washing, handwashing, warewashing, and
sanitation unless an approved alternative exists.
4. Isolate or discard affected ice, beverages, food, filters, and water-contact product according to authority direction.
5. Evaluate whether approved bottled, hauled, boiled, or otherwise treated water can support restricted operations.
6. Provide approved handwashing and restroom controls or close when required hygiene and sanitation cannot be maintained.
7. Suspend dishwashing, beverage, ice, produce, and food processes that lack an approved safe-water method.
8. Communicate restrictions to employees, guests, delivery partners, and vendors without making unsupported reopening
promises.
9. After official clearance, flush lines, clean and sanitize equipment, replace filters, discard initial ice, and restore systems as
directed.
10. Document the notice, decisions, product disposition, authority contacts, restoration actions, and manager approval.


94. Boil Water Notice SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for boil water notice.
□ Notify ownership, the person in charge, and the local regulatory authority when required.
□ Isolate or discard affected ice, beverages, food, filters, and water-contact product according to authority direction.
□ Provide approved handwashing and restroom controls or close when required hygiene and sanitation cannot be
maintained.
□ Communicate restrictions to employees, guests, delivery partners, and vendors without making unsupported reopening
promises.
□ Document the notice, decisions, product disposition, authority contacts, restoration actions, and manager approval.
• Continuing normal ice, beverage, food, handwashing, or warewashing processes during the notice.
• Using an alternate water source or boiling method that has not been approved for the specific use.
• Forgetting affected filters, beverage lines, ice bins, produce, and water-contact equipment.
• Promising reopening before official clearance and restoration steps are complete.
• Failing to document product disposition, authority contacts, flushing, sanitation, and approval.

95. Health Department Inspection Response SOP

SOP ID V2-095 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To cooperate professionally with a health-department inspection, preserve truthful records, correct
immediate hazards, and complete every required follow-up action.
APPLIES TO: All food employees, trainers, kitchen leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees complete required controls and records. Management stops unsafe work, evaluates
affected food, and completes corrective action.
WHEN USED: At each required monitoring point and whenever a limit, complaint, contamination concern, or
inspection issue occurs.
• Current food-safety standard and monitoring log
• Calibrated thermometer or approved test device
• Sanitizer, test strips, and cleaning supplies
• Labels, timers, discard controls, and PPE
• Manager and regulatory contact information
• Inspectors receive professional cooperation, truthful answers, requested access, and accurate records without obstruction or
concealment.
• Immediate hazards are corrected safely while the original condition and required documentation remain clear.
• Every observation, deadline, product action, repair, and responsible person is recorded.
• Service restriction, closure, ownership notification, and follow-up occur when required by the report or operating condition.
1. Greet the inspector professionally, verify identification when appropriate, and notify the person in charge without delaying
lawful access.
2. Assign one authorized manager to accompany the inspection and one employee to coordinate immediate corrections.
3. Answer questions truthfully and provide requested records, permits, logs, labels, policies, and access.
4. Do not hide food, move violations, backfill records, coach false answers, or obstruct observation.
5. Correct immediate hazards when safe while allowing the inspector to observe the original condition.
6. Record each observation, code reference, correction, deadline, responsible person, and any product placed on hold or
discarded.
7. Obtain and review the written report before the inspector leaves when available.
8. Notify ownership and required company contacts and determine whether service restriction, closure, or specialized support is
necessary.
9. Complete corrective actions, repairs, retraining, and required written responses by the stated deadline.
10. Verify sustained compliance through follow-up inspections and retain the report and supporting records.


95. Health Department Inspection Response SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can state the approved food-safety limit, monitoring method, corrective action, documentation, and
person-in-charge role for health department inspection response.
□ Assign one authorized manager to accompany the inspection and one employee to coordinate immediate corrections.
□ Do not hide food, move violations, backfill records, coach false answers, or obstruct observation.
□ Record each observation, code reference, correction, deadline, responsible person, and any product placed on hold or
discarded.
□ Notify ownership and required company contacts and determine whether service restriction, closure, or specialized
support is necessary.
□ Verify sustained compliance through follow-up inspections and retain the report and supporting records.
• Delaying or obstructing lawful access while employees attempt to hide or correct conditions.
• Guessing, arguing, coaching false answers, or providing backfilled records.
• Correcting an issue without recording the original observation and required deadline.
• Failing to notify ownership or restrict service when immediate safety controls cannot be maintained.
• Treating the report as complete before repairs, retraining, response, and follow-up verification.

PART 5

DISH & UTENSIL OPERATIONS

15 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control dish-area readiness, sorting, warewashing, chemical checks, air-drying, storage, trash,
cleaning, and closing.


96. Dish Pit Opening SOP

SOP ID V2-096 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure the dish area is clean, safe, stocked, and ready before dishes, pans, glassware, and
utensils begin arriving.
APPLIES TO: Dishwasher, opening dishwasher, kitchen lead, shift lead, or manager on duty.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: Before service begins, before dish volume starts, or before the dishwasher begins the assigned shift.
• Dish machine
• Dish racks
• Glass racks
• Silverware racks or baskets
• Approved chemicals
• The dish pit is clean, safe, organized, and ready before dish volume begins.
• Check that the dish machine is operating correctly.
• Use chemicals according to label and restaurant policy.
• Do not mix chemicals.
1. Arrive at the dish area before dish volume begins.
2. Check that the dish area is clean and free of old food, trash, broken glass, and clutter.
3. Check that the dish machine is assembled and ready according to restaurant procedure.
4. Check chemical levels if required.
5. Check water temperature or machine readiness according to restaurant policy.
6. Set up dish racks, glass racks, silverware baskets, and pot/pan areas.
7. Check the dirty dish drop area.
8. Check the clean dish landing area.
9. Make sure clean and dirty areas are clearly separated.
10. Prepare trash, floors, drains, racks, and clean-storage areas and obtain manager release before the dish pit opens.


96. Dish Pit Opening SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the dirty-to-clean flow, wash/rinse/sanitize control, chemical or temperature limit, and failure
escalation for dish pit opening.
□ Check that the dish area is clean and free of old food, trash, broken glass, and clutter.
□ Check chemical levels if required.
□ Set up dish racks, glass racks, silverware baskets, and pot/pan areas.
□ Check the clean dish landing area.
□ Prepare trash, floors, drains, racks, and clean-storage areas and obtain manager release before the dish pit opens.
• Performing dish pit opening without dirty-to-clean separation, correct racks, approved chemicals, and required PPE.
• Skipping scraping, washing, rinsing, sanitizing, testing, or air-drying steps.
• Stacking or storing wet, chipped, soiled, or contaminated wares.
• Continuing after chemical, temperature, pressure, drainage, machine, or breakage failure.
• Ending without cleaning the area, documenting checks, and manager verification.

97. Dirty Dish Sorting SOP

SOP ID V2-097 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To sort dirty dishes, utensils, glassware, and cookware by type and hazard while preserving a clear
dirty-to-clean warewashing flow.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Establish separate landing zones for plates, glassware, silverware, cups, pans, trash, and sharps.
2. Receive dirty wares without allowing them to cross into the clean side or block the machine path.
3. Remove knives and sharp tools immediately into the approved clearly marked container.
4. Separate broken or chipped items and handle glass through the approved breakage process.
5. Empty liquids and food waste into the correct containers without splashing or clogging drains.
6. Stack like items only to the safe approved height and keep heavy wares below fragile items.
7. Route soak items, pots, glassware, silverware, and machine-ready racks to the correct next step.
8. Keep chemicals, towels, personal items, and trash out of ware-sorting zones.
9. Clear backups before they block exits, hand sinks, floor drains, or employee movement.
10. Clean and reset the sorting area and report recurring breakage, sharps, or flow problems.

98. Pre-Scraping and Soaking SOP

SOP ID V2-098 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To remove food debris and soak approved wares safely before washing without creating chemical,
sharps, lifting, or cross-contamination hazards.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Move sorted wares to the scraping area while preserving dirty-to-clean separation.
2. Scrape food into the approved waste container using the designated tool rather than hands or spray pressure.
3. Empty cups, pans, and hollow items before placing them into soak or racks.
4. Use only approved soaking solution, container, dilution, and maximum soak time.
5. Keep knives, blades, glass, aluminum, wood, and chemical-sensitive items out of incompatible soak solutions.
6. Change soak water when cold, greasy, overloaded, contaminated, or outside the approved chemical range.
7. Loosen heavy soil without nesting items or creating hidden sharp hazards.
8. Rinse or transfer items according to the next warewashing step and do not treat soaking as washing or sanitizing.
9. Clean the scrape table, waste opening, soak sink, tools, floor, and splash zone as buildup occurs.
10. Report clogged drains, chemical problems, sharp objects, broken glass, or persistent soil.


98. Pre-Scraping and Soaking SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the dirty-to-clean flow, wash/rinse/sanitize control, chemical or temperature limit, and failure
escalation for pre-scraping and soaking.
□ Scrape food into the approved waste container using the designated tool rather than hands or spray pressure.
□ Use only approved soaking solution, container, dilution, and maximum soak time.
□ Change soak water when cold, greasy, overloaded, contaminated, or outside the approved chemical range.
□ Rinse or transfer items according to the next warewashing step and do not treat soaking as washing or sanitizing.
□ Report clogged drains, chemical problems, sharp objects, broken glass, or persistent soil.
• Performing pre-scraping and soaking without dirty-to-clean separation, correct racks, approved chemicals, and required
PPE.
• Skipping scraping, washing, rinsing, sanitizing, testing, or air-drying steps.
• Stacking or storing wet, chipped, soiled, or contaminated wares.
• Continuing after chemical, temperature, pressure, drainage, machine, or breakage failure.
• Ending without cleaning the area, documenting checks, and manager verification.

99. Dish Machine Operation SOP

SOP ID V2-099 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To operate the dish machine with correct loading, temperatures or chemical concentration, contact time,
air-drying, records, and corrective action.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Inspect the dirty-to-clean flow, sinks, machine, chemicals, drains, racks, tools, and clean storage.
2. Put on required PPE and prepare approved detergent, sanitizer, test supplies, and water conditions.
3. Separate sharp, fragile, chemical, heavily soiled, and special-handling items.
4. Verify chemical supply, temperatures, pressure, screens, spray arms, drains, and test results.
5. Pre-scrape and rack items so all surfaces receive wash and rinse action.
6. Run the full approved cycle and inspect items for soil, film, damage, and sanitizer result.
7. Rewash failed items and stop the machine for unresolved chemical, temperature, or mechanical failure.
8. Inspect finished items and return only clean, dry, intact ware to protected storage.
9. Correct failed temperature, chemical, soil, damage, plumbing, or machine conditions.
10. Clean and reset the area and complete the required log and manager verification.

□ Put on required PPE and prepare approved detergent, sanitizer, test supplies, and water conditions.
□ Verify chemical supply, temperatures, pressure, screens, spray arms, drains, and test results.
□ Run the full approved cycle and inspect items for soil, film, damage, and sanitizer result.
□ Inspect finished items and return only clean, dry, intact ware to protected storage.

• Stacking or storing wet, chipped, soiled, or contaminated wares.

100. Three-Compartment Sink Warewashing SOP

SOP ID V2-100 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To apply and document the approved food-safety limits, measuring tools, monitoring frequency,
corrective actions, and manager verification for three-compartment sink warewashing.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Clean and set the wash, rinse, and sanitize compartments in the approved left-to-right or posted sequence.
2. Fill each compartment to the required level and verify wash temperature and sanitizer concentration.
3. Scrape and pre-rinse wares before placing them in the wash solution.
4. Wash each item completely with approved detergent and clean tools, changing water when soiled or ineffective.
5. Rinse away detergent and loosened soil in clean rinse water.
6. Fully immerse or expose each item to the approved sanitizer for the required contact time.
7. Test and record sanitizer concentration at opening, required intervals, and after solution changes.
8. Place sanitized wares on the clean drainboard or rack and allow complete air-drying.
9. Never towel-dry, stack wet items, mix dirty and clean wares, or use the sink for food preparation during warewashing.
10. Drain, clean, sanitize, and reset the sink and record corrective action or manager verification.


100. Three-Compartment Sink Warewashing SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the dirty-to-clean flow, wash/rinse/sanitize control, chemical or temperature limit, and failure
escalation for three-compartment sink warewashing.
□ Fill each compartment to the required level and verify wash temperature and sanitizer concentration.
□ Wash each item completely with approved detergent and clean tools, changing water when soiled or ineffective.
□ Fully immerse or expose each item to the approved sanitizer for the required contact time.
□ Place sanitized wares on the clean drainboard or rack and allow complete air-drying.
□ Drain, clean, sanitize, and reset the sink and record corrective action or manager verification.
• Performing three-compartment sink warewashing without the current approved limit, clean tools, test method, timer, and
record.
• Guessing temperature, concentration, contact time, or elapsed time instead of measuring.
• Continuing with an out-of-limit result without approved corrective action.
• Backfilling, altering, or omitting monitoring records.
• Failing to verify product disposition, sanitation, equipment condition, and manager review.

104. Dish Rack Loading SOP

SOP ID V2-104 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To load dish racks so water and sanitizer reach all surfaces while preventing nesting, breakage,
blocked spray, and unsafe handling.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Select the correct rack for plates, bowls, cups, glassware, utensils, pans, or specialty items.
2. Remove heavy food and debris before loading and keep sharp objects in the approved container.
3. Place items so spray reaches all surfaces and water can drain without nesting or shielding.
4. Keep delicate glassware and breakable items separate from heavy wares and metal utensils.
5. Face soiled surfaces toward the spray pattern and avoid overloading or stacking pieces together.
6. Load utensils and hollow items according to the approved orientation without creating hand hazards.
7. Keep the rack stable, within capacity, and clear of machine arms, curtains, and sensors.
8. Rewash items that shift, nest, retain soil, or block cleaning during the cycle.
9. Unload only from the clean side with clean hands and allow complete air-drying.
10. Inspect the rack and wares for soil, chips, breakage, standing water, and correct storage before reuse.

□ Remove heavy food and debris before loading and keep sharp objects in the approved container.
□ Keep delicate glassware and breakable items separate from heavy wares and metal utensils.
□ Load utensils and hollow items according to the approved orientation without creating hand hazards.

air-drying, storage, and corrective action.

• Gloves if required

• Dumpster access
• Broom
• Trash is removed before overflow, disposed of properly, and the area is left clean and safe.
• Do not overfill bags.
• Use caution with heavy bags.
• Watch for broken glass or sharp items.
1. Check trash level.
2. Replace trash before it overflows.
3. Tie bag securely.
4. Use proper lifting technique.
5. Check for leaks or sharp items.
6. Carry or cart trash to approved disposal area.
7. Place trash in dumpster or approved container.
8. Close dumpster lid if required.
9. Clean trash can area if needed.
10. Replace liner. Wash hands after trash handling. Report broken glass, pest concerns, overflowing dumpsters, or unsafe.

108. Dish Machine Chemical Check SOP

SOP ID V2-108 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To verify dish-machine chemicals, concentration, supply, feed lines, and test results before and during
use and stop operation when control is lost.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Review the machine chemical requirements, product labels, SDS, test method, and approved operating range.
2. Confirm detergent, rinse aid, and sanitizer containers are correct, connected, labeled, upright, and not empty.
3. Inspect pickup tubes, caps, lines, pumps, alarms, and injection points for leaks, air gaps, kinks, or damage.
4. Run the approved priming or test cycle according to manufacturer instructions.
5. Test the final rinse or sanitizer using the approved strip, device, location, and timing.
6. Record the actual result and do not estimate from smell, color, or machine display alone.
7. Stop warewashing when chemical delivery or required results are outside the approved limit.
8. Notify the manager and use the approved three-compartment-sink fallback only when it can be controlled safely.
9. Replace chemicals or request service only through trained and authorized employees.
10. Retest, document corrective action, and obtain manager release before returning the machine to service.


108. Dish Machine Chemical Check SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the dirty-to-clean flow, wash/rinse/sanitize control, chemical or temperature limit, and failure
escalation for dish machine chemical check.
□ Confirm detergent, rinse aid, and sanitizer containers are correct, connected, labeled, upright, and not empty.
□ Run the approved priming or test cycle according to manufacturer instructions.
□ Record the actual result and do not estimate from smell, color, or machine display alone.
□ Notify the manager and use the approved three-compartment-sink fallback only when it can be controlled safely.
□ Retest, document corrective action, and obtain manager release before returning the machine to service.
• Performing dish machine chemical check without dirty-to-clean separation, correct racks, approved chemicals, and
required PPE.
• Skipping scraping, washing, rinsing, sanitizing, testing, or air-drying steps.
• Stacking or storing wet, chipped, soiled, or contaminated wares.
• Continuing after chemical, temperature, pressure, drainage, machine, or breakage failure.
• Ending without cleaning the area, documenting checks, and manager verification.

110. Dish Pit Closing SOP

SOP ID V2-110 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To close dish pit through a verified sequence that protects food, equipment, chemicals, records, and
next-shift readiness.
APPLIES TO: Dish employees, utility employees, kitchen leads, trainers, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Dish employees follow the approved sequence. Management verifies chemicals, temperatures,
air-drying, storage, and corrective action.
WHEN USED: During opening, active warewashing, chemical or temperature checks, storage, cleaning, and
closing.
• Current warewashing procedure and machine instructions
• Approved detergent, sanitizer, and test supplies
• Racks, brushes, scrapers, and protective equipment
• Temperature or chemical monitoring log
• Manager and maintenance contact information
• Items move from dirty to clean without cross-contamination.
• Required wash, rinse, sanitize, temperature, concentration, and contact-time controls are verified.
• Clean items air-dry completely and are stored protected from contamination.
• Machine, chemical, plumbing, or cleanliness failures stop affected use until corrected.
1. Inspect the dirty-to-clean flow, sinks, machine, chemicals, drains, racks, tools, and clean storage.
2. Put on required PPE and prepare approved detergent, sanitizer, test supplies, and water conditions.
3. Separate sharp, fragile, chemical, heavily soiled, and special-handling items.
4. Finish all ware and return clean items to protected storage.
5. Drain, disassemble, clean, rinse, and sanitize the machine, sinks, screens, arms, and removable parts.
6. Clean floors, walls, drains, carts, racks, trash areas, and chemical containers.
7. Reassemble, leave equipment in the approved state, and obtain manager verification.
8. Inspect finished items and return only clean, dry, intact ware to protected storage.
9. Correct failed temperature, chemical, soil, damage, plumbing, or machine conditions.
10. Clean and reset the area and complete the required log and manager verification.


110. Dish Pit Closing SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the dirty-to-clean flow, wash/rinse/sanitize control, chemical or temperature limit, and failure
escalation for dish pit closing.
□ Put on required PPE and prepare approved detergent, sanitizer, test supplies, and water conditions.
□ Finish all ware and return clean items to protected storage.
□ Clean floors, walls, drains, carts, racks, trash areas, and chemical containers.
□ Inspect finished items and return only clean, dry, intact ware to protected storage.
□ Clean and reset the area and complete the required log and manager verification.
• Performing dish pit closing without dirty-to-clean separation, correct racks, approved chemicals, and required PPE.
• Skipping scraping, washing, rinsing, sanitizing, testing, or air-drying steps.
• Stacking or storing wet, chipped, soiled, or contaminated wares.

PART 6

CLEANING & SANITATION

20 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control scheduled cleaning, dining and kitchen sanitation, restrooms, storage areas, high-touch
surfaces, spills, bodily fluids, and chemical response.


111. Master Cleaning Schedule SOP

SOP ID V2-111 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain a complete master cleaning schedule with assigned areas, frequencies, methods,
chemicals, verification, and documented completion.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Review the current master cleaning schedule by area, surface, equipment, frequency, method, and responsible position.
2. Confirm each task lists the approved cleaner or sanitizer, tools, PPE, contact time, and verification standard.
3. Assign daily, weekly, monthly, seasonal, and shutdown tasks around service and food-production risk.
4. Coordinate tasks that require equipment shutdown, vendor access, ladders, ventilation, or restricted areas.
5. Check completed work through direct inspection rather than accepting initials alone.
6. Reassign missed or failed tasks with an owner, deadline, and reinspection time.
7. Record chemical, equipment, pest, damage, drainage, and access issues discovered during cleaning.
8. Compare repeated failures with staffing, training, tools, schedule timing, and maintenance needs.
9. Update the schedule when menus, equipment, layout, regulations, or operating hours change.
10. Retain completion records and review overdue and recurring items at the manager checkpoint.


111. Master Cleaning Schedule SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the approved chemical or method, PPE, area-control step, contact time, and reopening
standard for master cleaning schedule.
□ Confirm each task lists the approved cleaner or sanitizer, tools, PPE, contact time, and verification standard.
□ Coordinate tasks that require equipment shutdown, vendor access, ladders, ventilation, or restricted areas.
□ Reassign missed or failed tasks with an owner, deadline, and reinspection time.
□ Compare repeated failures with staffing, training, tools, schedule timing, and maintenance needs.
□ Retain completion records and review overdue and recurring items at the manager checkpoint.
• Starting master cleaning schedule without closing the area, protecting food and guests, and gathering the approved
chemical, tools, PPE, and signs.
• Using the wrong dilution, surface, temperature, contact time, or incompatible chemical.
• Moving soil from dirty areas onto clean areas or using the same tools across zones.
• Leaving wet floors, residue, waste, odors, blocked access, or unresolved damage.
• Reopening without inspection, required sanitation/disinfection, documentation, and manager release.
• Approved cleaner, sanitizer, disinfectant, dilution, or contact-time standard: ____________________
• Area tools, PPE, warning signs, spill kit, or cleanup equipment: _________________________________
• Cleaning frequency, master schedule, inspection, or reopening target: ___________________________
• Food, guest, employee, ventilation, drainage, or access protection steps: _______________________
• Chemical label/SDS, health code, bloodborne-pathogen, or local disposal requirement reviewed: ____
• Restaurant-specific surface, zone, sequence, or corrective-action steps: ________________________

• Do not mix chemicals.

6. Mop floor thoroughly.
7. Focus on corners, under equipment edges if assigned, and high-traffic areas.
8. Remove grease buildup using approved method.
9. Allow floor to dry or squeegee according to procedure.
10. Return cleaning tools to proper location. Empty and clean mop bucket according to policy. Report damaged floors, standing.

115. Restroom Deep Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-115 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete restroom deep cleaning safely using the approved chemical, concentration, contact time,
PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies

• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.

• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.

4. Allow required dwell time and use dedicated color-coded tools that never enter food areas.
5. Scrub bowls, undersides, grout, corners, vents, walls, bases, and hard-to-reach buildup.
6. Rinse or remove residue as required and disinfect approved surfaces at the labeled concentration and contact time.
7. Clean mirrors, stainless surfaces, dispensers, baby-changing equipment, and accessibility fixtures.
8. Clean and dry floors from the farthest point toward the exit and keep warning signs in place.
9. Restock supplies and report leaks, damage, pests, odors, lighting, ventilation, or accessibility problems.
10. Manager-inspect cleanliness, odor, dryness, supplies, and function before reopening.

area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.

• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Secure guest information, keys, radios, tablets, pagers, cash-related records, and lost property before cleaning.
2. Remove outdated signs, papers, menus, personal items, trash, and loose debris.
3. Clean and disinfect approved high-touch points including phones, tablets, pens, handles, counters, and pager surfaces.
4. Clean menu storage, shelves, drawers, podium surfaces, door glass, nearby rails, and waiting-area fixtures.
5. Protect electronics from overspray and use only manufacturer-approved products on screens and devices.
6. Clean floors, mats, corners, baseboards, entry glass, and the guest path without blocking access.
7. Inspect menus and guest materials and remove damaged, sticky, or outdated items.
8. Return devices and supplies to the approved layout and reconnect chargers and communication tools.
9. Report damaged doors, trip hazards, lighting, accessibility, pest, or equipment concerns.
10. Manager-verify security, cleanliness, organization, current materials, and guest readiness.

118. Server Station Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-118 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete server station cleaning safely using the approved chemical, concentration, contact time,
PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.

• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log

• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Remove food, ice, beverages, utensils, linens, condiments, trash, and personal items from the server station.
2. Discard or store open product according to policy and separate clean serviceware from dirty items.
3. Wash, rinse, and sanitize counters, cutting or garnish areas, shelves, trays, pitchers, and food-contact tools.
4. Clean beverage nozzles, drip trays, ice-bin exteriors, handles, dispensers, POS devices, and high-touch points.
5. Protect ice and clean supplies from chemical spray and never place wiping tools in food or utensil areas.
6. Clean cabinets, doors, walls, baseboards, floors, mats, drains, and areas under equipment.
7. Inspect glassware, silverware, trays, and smallwares and remove damaged or soiled items.
8. Restock only approved quantities after surfaces are clean, sanitized, dry, and organized.
9. Report leaks, pests, damaged equipment, temperature, drainage, or recurring soil problems.
10. Manager-verify sanitation, supply levels, safe access, and readiness before release.

119. Walk-In Cooler Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-119 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete walk-in cooler cleaning safely using the approved chemical, concentration, contact time,
PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Move or protect food in sections so required temperature control is maintained during cleaning.
2. Remove expired, leaking, spoiled, unlabeled, or manager-held product according to the disposition process.
3. Clean shelves, racks, walls, doors, gaskets, handles, fan guards, floors, drains, and condensate areas.
4. Use only approved chemicals and keep them away from exposed food and absorbent packaging.
5. Work from cleanest/highest areas downward and prevent dirty water from splashing product or containers.
6. Clean spills beneath containers and remove cardboard, broken packaging, standing water, and blocked airflow.
7. Rinse or remove residue as required and sanitize food-contact or high-touch surfaces.
8. Dry surfaces, return food to approved hierarchy, and restore labels, dates, spacing, and airflow.
9. Verify unit temperature and report leaks, ice, mold, pests, gasket, fan, drain, or door problems.
10. Record discarded product, corrections, maintenance needs, and manager verification.

• Storing raw, ready-to-eat, allergen, chemical, or guest-use items in an unsafe relationship.
• Blocking airflow, drains, sprinklers, access, cleaning, or temperature monitoring.
• Leaving open, leaking, damaged, or unprotected containers in service.
• Failing to correct and document repeated organization, temperature, pest, or rotation problems.

PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.

2. Remove damaged, thawed, leaking, unlabeled, freezer-burned, or manager-held product for evaluation.
3. Shut down or defrost only according to manufacturer and manager authorization.
4. Remove ice buildup with approved tools; never use knives, heat, or force that can damage coils or panels.
5. Clean shelves, walls, doors, gaskets, floors, fan guards, drains, and package-debris areas.
6. Use approved low-temperature cleaning methods and protect food and packaging from chemical exposure.
7. Dry surfaces fully to prevent new ice and restore racks, spacing, labels, dates, and storage hierarchy.
8. Return product promptly and verify doors close, gaskets seal, airflow is clear, and temperature recovers.
9. Report thawing, repeated frost, leaks, damaged panels, door, fan, alarm, or refrigeration problems.
10. Record product disposition, defrost time, temperature recovery, maintenance, and manager verification.

121. Dry Storage Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-121 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean dry storage while protecting food and packaging, maintaining labels and rotation, controlling
chemicals, and restoring organized storage.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies

• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Remove or protect food, single-use items, packaging, and clean supplies before cleaning each storage section.
2. Discard or hold damaged, wet, contaminated, pest-affected, expired, or unidentified product.
3. Clean shelves, dunnage, containers, walls, corners, doors, vents, floors, and areas beneath racks.
4. Keep chemicals and janitorial supplies separated from food and food-contact items throughout the process.
5. Remove cardboard debris, dust, spills, webbing, droppings, moisture, and clutter without spreading contamination.
6. Use approved tools and chemicals and avoid saturating packaging or creating standing water.
7. Dry the area and inspect for pests, leaks, wall openings, damaged shelving, lighting, and blocked access.
8. Return product at approved height and spacing using FIFO, labels, dates, categories, and allergen controls.
9. Record discarded product and assign pest, building, vendor, or storage corrections.
10. Manager-verify cleanliness, dryness, organization, safe access, and traceability.

122. Trash Area Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-122 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep the trash area clean, safe, odor-controlled, and pest-resistant.
APPLIES TO: Dishwasher, closing employee, kitchen lead, manager, or assigned cleaning employee.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: During closing, after heavy trash use, after spills or leaks, when odors appear, when trash overflows,
or according to restaurant.
• Trash bags
• Broom
• Mop or hose if approved
• Approved cleaner
• Gloves if required
• The trash area is free of loose trash, major spills, odors, overflow, and unsafe conditions.
• Use caution with heavy bags, broken glass, sharp items, grease, and leaking trash.
• Do not leave back doors unsecured.
• Wash hands after handling trash.
1. Close or control the trash area and put on required PPE before moving or cleaning waste.
2. Tie, close, and move bags without overloading, dragging, leaking, or carrying them through exposed-food routes.
3. Remove loose waste, broken glass, cardboard, grease residue, and blocked access using approved tools.
4. Clean container lids, handles, walls, pads, gates, drains, and surrounding ground.
5. Apply approved cleaner or sanitizer and prevent runoff into unauthorized drains or neighboring areas.
6. Keep dumpster lids and doors closed and verify containers are not leaking, damaged, or overfilled.
7. Separate grease, recycling, cardboard, glass, chemicals, and regulated waste into approved streams.
8. Remove standing water and food residue that can attract pests or create slips and odors.
9. Report missed pickups, illegal dumping, pests, damaged enclosures, lighting, access, or drainage problems.
10. Wash hands, document completion, and obtain manager verification before reopening the route.

SOP ID V2-123 VERSION 1.0

PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.

3. Allow hot grease or equipment to reach the approved handling condition before cleaning.
4. Scrape and collect grease with approved tools without using water on hot grease or creating splashes.
5. Apply only approved degreaser at the labeled dilution and protect food, ignition sources, and storm drains.
6. Clean floors, walls, equipment exteriors, containers, pads, handles, and transfer routes.
7. Remove residue and leave surfaces dry and slip-resistant; maintain warning signs until safe.
8. Secure used oil and grease waste and record any transfer, spill, overflow, or vendor issue.
9. Report leaks, blocked traps, damaged containers, pests, fire hazards, or unauthorized dumping.
10. Manager-verify cleanliness, containment, drainage, fire safety, and documentation.

SOP ID V2-124 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete mop sink and janitorial area cleaning safely using the approved chemical, concentration,
contact time, PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.

4. Clean the mop sink basin, faucet, walls, backsplash, shelves, floor, and drain without splashing nearby areas.
5. Do not pour grease, food solids, hazardous chemicals, or incompatible mixtures into the sink.
6. Apply approved cleaner or disinfectant at the labeled dilution and contact time.
7. Rinse or remove residue as required and leave the basin, floor, and tools clean and dry.
8. Hang mops and tools to air-dry and keep chemicals below and separate from paper and clean equipment.
9. Report leaks, clogs, damaged backflow devices, unlabeled chemicals, pests, or ventilation problems.
10. Manager-verify chemical storage, drainage, dryness, sanitation, and restricted access.

125. High-Touch Surface Disinfection SOP

SOP ID V2-125 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete high-touch surface disinfection safely using the approved chemical, concentration, contact
time, PPE, area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Identify high-touch surfaces, required frequency, approved product, compatible material, and area-control needs.
2. Clean visible soil before disinfection; disinfectant is not a substitute for required cleaning.
3. Put on required PPE and prevent spray or chemical contact with food, ice, utensils, guests, and electronics.
4. Apply the product at the labeled concentration and fully wet the approved surface.
5. Maintain the required contact time without wiping the surface dry too soon.
6. Rinse food-contact surfaces when the label requires it and allow surfaces to air-dry as directed.
7. Use separate cloths or tools by zone and replace contaminated solution or materials promptly.
8. Include handles, switches, railings, devices, payment surfaces, doors, dispensers, and shared equipment as assigned.
9. Stop and report chemical reaction, damaged surface, exposure, missing label/SDS, or inadequate ventilation.
10. Record completion and manager-verify coverage, contact time, residue control, and safe reopening.


125. High-Touch Surface Disinfection SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The employee can identify the approved chemical or method, PPE, area-control step, contact time, and reopening
standard for high-touch surface disinfection.
□ Clean visible soil before disinfection; disinfectant is not a substitute for required cleaning.
□ Apply the product at the labeled concentration and fully wet the approved surface.
□ Rinse food-contact surfaces when the label requires it and allow surfaces to air-dry as directed.
□ Include handles, switches, railings, devices, payment surfaces, doors, dispensers, and shared equipment as assigned.
□ Record completion and manager-verify coverage, contact time, residue control, and safe reopening.
• Starting high-touch surface disinfection without closing the area, protecting food and guests, and gathering the approved
chemical, tools, PPE, and signs.
• Using the wrong dilution, surface, temperature, contact time, or incompatible chemical.
• Moving soil from dirty areas onto clean areas or using the same tools across zones.
• Leaving wet floors, residue, waste, odors, blocked access, or unresolved damage.
• Reopening without inspection, required sanitation/disinfection, documentation, and manager release.

126. Broken Glass Cleanup SOP

SOP ID V2-126 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure broken glass is handled safely, cleaned completely, and documented when needed.
APPLIES TO: Any employee who discovers broken glass, with manager involvement when needed.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: Any time glass breaks in the dining room, bar, kitchen, dish pit, ice bin, service station, storage area,
restroom, walkway, or trash.
• Broom
• Dustpan
• Broken glass container if used
• Gloves if required
• Wet floor sign if needed
• Broken glass is cleaned safely, fully removed, disposed of properly, and reported when required.
• Do not pick up broken glass with bare hands.
• Do not use towels to wipe broken glass.
• Do not place broken glass loose in regular trash unless approved by policy.
1. Stop and secure the area.
2. Keep guests and employees away from the broken glass.
3. Notify a manager if the break is in a guest area, food area, ice area, or walkway.
4. Use approved tools to clean up glass.
5. Check surrounding area carefully for small pieces.
6. Dispose of glass according to restaurant policy.
7. If glass broke near food, ice, or clean items, follow discard or inspection procedure.
8. Clean the area after glass is removed.
9. Report injury immediately if anyone is cut or hurt.
10. Complete incident documentation if required.

127. Spill Cleanup SOP

SOP ID V2-127 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To make sure spills are cleaned quickly, safely, and completely.
APPLIES TO: Any employee who sees or causes a spill, with manager support when needed.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: Any time there is a spill in the dining room, kitchen, bar, dish pit, restroom, walkway, storage area,
entrance, or back area.
• Wet floor sign
• Towels
• Mop
• Bucket
• Approved cleaner
• Spills are addressed immediately, cleaned completely, and the area is made safe before normal traffic resumes.
• Place wet floor sign when required.
• Do not leave spill unattended.
• Use correct cleaning chemical.
1. Stop and identify the spill.
2. Warn nearby guests or employees.
3. Place wet floor sign or block off area if needed.
4. Get proper cleanup supplies.
5. Clean spill completely.
6. Use approved cleaner if required.
7. Dry area as much as possible.
8. Check surrounding area for tracking or spread.
9. Remove cleanup materials properly.
10. Wash hands after cleanup and notify the manager when the spill caused injury risk, guest impact, chemical exposure,
product loss, or a repeated facility problem.

128. Bodily Fluid Cleanup SOP

SOP ID V2-128 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To complete bodily fluid cleanup safely using the approved chemical, concentration, contact time, PPE,
area control, food protection, and manager verification.
APPLIES TO: Assigned cleaning employees, department employees, shift leads, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee cleans and sanitizes safely. Management verifies the area, chemical
use, records, and unresolved hazards.
WHEN USED: At the scheduled frequency, after contamination or spills, and before the area is released for normal
use.
• Current cleaning schedule and area checklist
• Approved labeled chemicals and SDS access
• Required PPE, caution signs, and barriers
• Clean tools, cloths, brushes, and waste supplies
• Manager verification and issue log
• Employees use only approved chemicals, labels, dilution, PPE, and application methods.
• Food, utensils, guests, employees, and clean areas are protected during cleaning.
• Cleaning tools do not move contamination between restrooms, floors, food areas, and dining areas.
• The area is reopened only after hazards, residue, moisture, waste, and signage are resolved.
1. Review the current schedule, area standard, chemical label, SDS, PPE, and required contact time.
2. Place signs or barriers and protect guests, food, clean utensils, equipment, and unaffected areas.
3. Remove loose soil and waste using tools assigned to the correct sanitation zone.
4. Block access and put on approved PPE before approaching bodily fluids.
5. Use the designated kit and follow the approved cleanup and disinfectant contact-time procedure.
6. Bag contaminated materials and dispose of them according to policy without compressing or sorting.
7. Remove PPE safely, wash hands, secure the kit, and document exposure or follow-up.
8. Inspect for soil, residue, moisture, odor, damage, pests, and missed high-touch points.
9. Remove tools, waste, PPE, signs, and barriers only when the area is safe and dry.
10. Complete the schedule or issue log and obtain manager verification.

129. Chemical Safety SOP

SOP ID V2-129 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To store, label, dilute, use, and dispose of chemicals safely according to the product label, SDS, PPE,
food-protection, and access requirements.
APPLIES TO: Trained employees, managers, and designated spill-response or maintenance contacts.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees isolate the area and avoid unapproved cleanup. Management directs response
using the label, SDS, PPE, and required reporting.
WHEN USED: Whenever a chemical is handled, stored, spilled, misused, released, or associated with possible
exposure.
• Chemical label, SDS, and emergency instructions
• Required PPE and spill-control materials
• Caution signs, barriers, and ventilation controls
• Emergency, poison-control, vendor, and manager contacts
• Incident and disposal documentation
• The area is isolated and the product label and SDS direct the response.
• Employees do not mix chemicals or perform cleanup beyond their training and PPE.
• Food, utensils, drains, ventilation, and occupied areas are protected.
• Exposure, release, disposal, and required outside notification are documented.
1. Keep people away and notify management before approaching the release or exposure.
2. Identify the product from the label or records without touching or smelling unknown material.
3. Review the SDS and emergency instructions and obtain only the required PPE and spill materials.
4. Restrict access and identify the chemical, label, hazard, quantity, and exposure from a safe position.
5. Notify management and use the SDS and emergency instructions to select PPE and response limits.
6. Contain and clean only within training; protect food, drains, ventilation, and occupied areas.
7. Dispose of material properly and document exposure, cleanup, notification, and follow-up.
8. Arrange approved disposal, ventilation, decontamination, medical evaluation, or outside response.
9. Restock and secure chemicals and keep the area closed until management confirms it is safe.
10. Document product, quantity, exposure, response, contacts, disposal, and corrective action.


129. Chemical Safety SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the approved chemical or method, PPE, area-control step, contact time, and reopening
standard for chemical safety.
□ Identify the product from the label or records without touching or smelling unknown material.
□ Restrict access and identify the chemical, label, hazard, quantity, and exposure from a safe position.
□ Contain and clean only within training; protect food, drains, ventilation, and occupied areas.
□ Arrange approved disposal, ventilation, decontamination, medical evaluation, or outside response.
□ Document product, quantity, exposure, response, contacts, disposal, and corrective action.
• Responding to chemical safety without the product label, SDS, PPE, ventilation, and approved spill method.
• Mixing chemicals, using food containers, or applying an unapproved neutralizer.
• Allowing guests, food, utensils, drains, or ignition sources into the affected area.
• Cleaning beyond training instead of evacuating and contacting emergency or hazardous-material support.
• Reopening without disposal, decontamination, documentation, and manager verification.

using the label, SDS, PPE, and required reporting.

area.
• Chemical label
• Safety data sheet if required
• Gloves / protective equipment if required
• Spill cleanup supplies
• Phone
• Chemical spill is isolated, handled safely, cleaned according to policy, and documented when required.
• Do not mix chemicals.
• Do not clean unknown chemicals without manager direction.
• Move food and people away from affected area.
1. Move away from spill if unsafe.
2. Notify manager immediately.
3. Keep guests and employees away.
4. Identify chemical if safe.
5. Follow label and safety guidance.
6. Use required protective equipment if trained.
7. Clean spill only if safe and allowed.
8. Remove contaminated food, tools, or supplies according to policy.
9. Ventilate area if directed and safe.
10. Document spill and exposure if applicable.

EQUIPMENT & PREVENTIVE
