15 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control equipment inspections, shutdown, cleaning, refrigeration, failures, preventive
maintenance, smallwares, knives, and unsafe-equipment lockout.


131. Equipment Opening Inspection SOP

SOP ID V2-131 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To inspect equipment before opening for cleanliness, guards, utilities, damage, operating condition,
food-safety impact, and required service restrictions.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Review the equipment opening checklist, overnight notes, maintenance holds, service tags, and manufacturer instructions.
2. Inspect cords, plugs, gas connections, hoses, guards, doors, seals, blades, controls, vents, casters, and mounting points.
3. Confirm the area is clean, dry, unobstructed, ventilated, and free of leaks, grease buildup, pests, or stored items.
4. Verify utilities, suppression systems, drains, filters, water, and required clearances before startup.
5. Check that safety devices, shields, interlocks, thermostats, alarms, and emergency shutoffs are present and functional.
6. Start equipment only in the approved sequence and observe sound, odor, heat, pressure, vibration, flame, and display
behavior.
7. Verify operating temperature or performance before loading food or assigning production.
8. Stop, disconnect when authorized, tag out, and notify management when any unsafe or abnormal condition is found.
9. Record the inspection result, defect, temporary restriction, service request, and person responsible.
10. Obtain manager release before employees use equipment that was previously out of service or repaired.

VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

and release requirement for equipment opening inspection.
□ Inspect cords, plugs, gas connections, hoses, guards, doors, seals, blades, controls, vents, casters, and mounting

□ Verify utilities, suppression systems, drains, filters, water, and required clearances before startup.
□ Start equipment only in the approved sequence and observe sound, odor, heat, pressure, vibration, flame, and display
behavior.
□ Stop, disconnect when authorized, tag out, and notify management when any unsafe or abnormal condition is found.
□ Obtain manager release before employees use equipment that was previously out of service or repaired.
• Performing equipment opening inspection without manufacturer instructions, training, PPE, shutdown control, and an
out-of-service process.
• Cleaning, adjusting, or reaching into energized, hot, pressurized, bladed, or moving equipment.
• Restarting after abnormal sound, odor, flame, leak, temperature, guard, cord, or control failure.
• Allowing an unqualified employee to repair or bypass a safety device.
• Returning equipment to service without testing, records, and manager or authorized-service release.

132. Equipment Shutdown SOP

SOP ID V2-132 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To shut down equipment in the approved sequence so heat, pressure, gas, electricity, water, moving
parts, product, and cleaning risks are controlled.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Review the approved shutdown sequence, manufacturer instructions, production status, and equipment that must remain
operating.
2. Stop feeding product and allow the current cycle, pressure, heat, or moving parts to reach the approved safe state.
3. Remove, protect, cool, hold, or discard food according to the product and time-temperature requirements.
4. Turn controls off in the correct order and close gas, water, steam, or other utilities only when authorized.
5. Disconnect power or apply lockout/tagout only through trained and authorized employees.
6. Allow equipment to cool and depressurize before cleaning, disassembly, or access.
7. Remove approved parts and complete required cleaning, draining, filtering, or sanitation.
8. Reassemble or leave components in the approved drying or service position.
9. Inspect for leaks, damage, abnormal wear, residue, and conditions requiring an out-of-service tag.
10. Record shutdown, exceptions, maintenance needs, and manager verification.

133. Fryer Filtering and Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-133 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To filter and clean fryers safely while controlling hot oil, burns, ignition, product quality, waste oil,
equipment condition, and documentation.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Follow the fryer manufacturer instructions and confirm the unit is cool enough and safe for filtering.
2. Put on required PPE, secure the area, remove nearby water, and verify the correct filter equipment and oil container.
3. Turn off or place the fryer in the approved filter mode; never drain hot oil into an unapproved container.
4. Remove baskets and skim loose debris without splashing or contacting hot surfaces.
5. Drain oil through the approved filter system while monitoring hoses, valves, seals, and container capacity.
6. Clean crumbs and sediment from the fry pot using approved tools; do not use water on hot oil or energized components.
7. Inspect oil quality, level, odor, color, and service life and obtain approval before topping off or discarding.
8. Return filtered oil, close valves, verify correct level, and restart only according to manufacturer instructions.
9. Wipe exterior surfaces, clean the floor, store filter tools, and move used oil through the approved route.
10. Record filtering, oil change, defect, spill, burn, or service need and obtain manager verification.

134. Flat-Top Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-134 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean the flat-top safely after controlled cooldown using approved tools and chemicals without
damaging the surface or contaminating food areas.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Follow manufacturer instructions and allow the flat-top to reach the approved cleaning temperature or cool-down state.
2. Put on required PPE, post the area, remove food, and protect nearby clean surfaces and ingredients.
3. Scrape food residue and grease toward the trough using an approved scraper held away from the body.
4. Empty and clean the grease drawer or trough without overfilling, spilling, or mixing water with hot grease.
5. Apply only the approved cleaner or method; never use chemicals or abrasives prohibited by the manufacturer.
6. Scrub the cooking surface, corners, splash guards, front edge, knobs, and surrounding nonfood-contact areas.
7. Remove residue and rinse or wipe as required so no chemical or debris remains on the cooking surface.
8. Dry, season, or protect the surface according to the approved process and reinstall clean components.
9. Clean the floor and nearby wall surfaces and report warping, cracks, hot spots, loose controls, or gas concerns.
10. Record completion and obtain manager verification before the unit is returned to service.

135. Grill Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-135 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean grills safely by controlling heat, grease, sharp tools, chemicals, removable parts, waste, and
final equipment readiness.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Follow manufacturer instructions, shut down the grill as required, and confirm a safe cleaning condition.
2. Put on required PPE, ventilate the area, remove food, and protect nearby clean equipment and ingredients.
3. Remove grates, racks, shields, trays, and grease components only when safe and approved.
4. Scrape carbon and food residue with approved tools; avoid damaging coatings, burners, sensors, or igniters.
5. Clean grates and removable parts using the approved chemical, soak, rinse, and drying process.
6. Clean burner covers, drip areas, splash guards, knobs, exterior surfaces, and grease collection points.
7. Do not introduce water or chemical into burners, gas openings, electrical parts, or hot grease.
8. Reassemble correctly, verify guards and parts, and test ignition or operation according to manufacturer instructions.
9. Clean surrounding floors and walls and report flame, gas, heat, structural, or control problems.
10. Record completion and obtain manager verification before the grill returns to service.

136. Oven Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-136 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean ovens safely according to manufacturer instructions while controlling heat, electricity or gas,
chemicals, removable parts, residue, and restart approval.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Follow manufacturer instructions, shut down the oven, and allow it to reach the approved cleaning condition.
2. Put on required PPE, post the area, remove food, racks, pans, stones, and loose debris as permitted.
3. Select only the approved oven cleaner or self-clean cycle and protect sensors, lights, gaskets, and electrical components.
4. Apply cleaner to approved surfaces without overspray onto food, utensils, floors, or adjacent equipment.
5. Allow required dwell time while maintaining ventilation and preventing unauthorized use.
6. Scrub interior surfaces, racks, doors, glass, handles, and exterior controls using approved non-damaging tools.
7. Remove all chemical residue and rinse or wipe according to the product label and manufacturer instructions.
8. Dry and reinstall components, inspect gaskets and doors, and complete any required burn-off before food use.
9. Report damaged seals, controls, heating elements, gas odor, uneven heat, or failed self-clean operation.
10. Record completion and manager verification before the oven returns to service.

137. Ice Machine Cleaning SOP

SOP ID V2-137 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To clean and sanitize the ice machine on the approved schedule while protecting ice, water-contact
surfaces, filters, chemicals, and restart verification.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Follow the manufacturer cleaning and sanitation procedure and place the ice machine out of service.
2. Protect or discard ice as required; never clean over usable ice or place tools inside the bin.
3. Wash hands, put on required PPE, and gather the approved cleaner, sanitizer, brushes, and test materials.
4. Turn off water or power only as instructed and remove approved parts without damaging sensors, tubes, or seals.
5. Remove scale, slime, soil, and mineral buildup from food-contact and water-contact components.
6. Rinse completely and sanitize the machine, bin, scoop holder, chute, and removable parts at the approved concentration and
contact time.
7. Allow surfaces to air-dry when required and reassemble all parts correctly.
8. Restart the machine, discard the required initial ice, and verify water flow, drainage, temperature, and ice quality.
9. Store the scoop correctly and report leaks, mold, recurring buildup, damaged parts, or treatment-system problems.
10. Record the cleaning date, chemical, concentration, employee, and manager verification.

138. Refrigeration Temperature Review SOP

SOP ID V2-138 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To review refrigeration temperatures, product conditions, alarms, doors, airflow, records, and corrective
actions before food safety is lost.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Identify every refrigeration unit, assigned temperature limit, product type, and required review time.
2. Inspect doors, gaskets, airflow, fan operation, displays, alarms, condensation, ice, and unit loading.
3. Read the unit display and measure representative food temperatures with a clean calibrated thermometer.
4. Record actual unit and product temperatures, time, employee, and any recent door, delivery, or defrost activity.
5. Recheck unexpected readings and verify the thermometer before concluding that the unit failed.
6. Move or protect food only through the approved alternate refrigeration or ice-control process.
7. Evaluate affected product using documented time, temperature, and product history under manager authority.
8. Adjust loading or door use only when safe; do not alter controls or repair equipment without authorization.
9. Contact service for repeated or material failure and record food disposition and corrective action.
10. Verify recovery and sustained control before returning the unit to normal use.

139. Refrigeration Failure SOP

SOP ID V2-139 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To respond to refrigeration failure by protecting product, verifying temperatures and time, restricting
access, arranging repair, and documenting disposition.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags

• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Review manufacturer instructions, the current SOP, service history, and authorized task.
2. Inspect the work area and control heat, electricity, gas, water, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts.
3. Shut down, cool, unplug, isolate, or tag equipment as required before contact.
4. Confirm actual product temperatures, duration, door status, load, airflow, and equipment condition.
5. Keep doors closed, move food only to verified cold storage, and protect product from contamination.
6. Tag the unit out of service when it cannot hold safely and contact approved service.
7. Evaluate and document every affected food item, time, temperature, movement, and disposition.
8. Clean and sanitize as required, reassemble correctly, and test only within authorization.
9. Record condition, service, parts, food impact, downtime, and release decision.
10. Return equipment to use only after manager or qualified service approval.

SOP ID V2-140 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To provide a clear process when the dish machine stops working or cannot safely clean and sanitize
items.
APPLIES TO: Dishwasher, kitchen lead, manager, or assigned employee.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Any time the dish machine fails, loses heat, loses sanitizer, leaks, stops cycling, or does not clean
items properly.
• Three-compartment sink
• Test strips if required
• Dish machine support contact
• Maintenance contact
• Backup dish procedure
• Unsafe dish machine use stops, backup sanitation process begins if approved, and service/repair is documented.
• Do not use the dish machine if it is not cleaning or sanitizing properly.
• Use approved backup warewashing procedure.
• Do not serve guests with unclean or unsanitized items.
1. Stop using the machine if it is not working safely.
2. Notify manager immediately.
3. Check issue according to training.
4. Contact maintenance or service provider if required.
5. Switch to approved three-compartment sink process if allowed.
6. Prioritize essential items.
7. Communicate delays to FOH and BOH.
8. Monitor sanitizer and manual wash process.
9. Do not resume machine use until approved.
10. Document issue and repair follow-up.

9. Document issue and repair status.
10. Do not return equipment to use until approved.

142. Preventive Maintenance Log SOP

SOP ID V2-142 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain a preventive-maintenance log that identifies equipment, required work, frequency,
completion, service provider, cost, and follow-up status.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.

• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags

• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Review manufacturer intervals, service history, inspections, warranty requirements, and recurring equipment failures.
2. List each equipment asset, location, model, identifier, responsible manager, and authorized service provider.
3. Schedule cleaning, lubrication, filter, calibration, inspection, replacement, and professional-service tasks.
4. Coordinate shutdown, product relocation, access, permits, and service timing to protect operations and safety.
5. Record work performed, parts used, readings, technician, cost, findings, and recommended follow-up.
6. Verify that guards, panels, utilities, controls, alarms, and safety devices are restored after service.
7. Test equipment under the approved operating condition before releasing it to employees.
8. Tag unresolved defects and define operating restrictions, repair deadline, and alternate process.
9. Review overdue work and repeated failures with ownership, purchasing, and department leaders.
10. Retain service records and update the next-due date and SOP when equipment or instructions change.

143. Smallwares Inspection SOP

SOP ID V2-143 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To inspect smallwares for cleanliness, damage, correct quantity, safe storage, and removal or
replacement of unsafe items.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Gather knives, utensils, pans, boards, containers, scoops, racks, and other assigned smallwares for inspection.
2. Separate clean items from dirty, damaged, unapproved, or employee-owned items.
3. Inspect for cracks, chips, sharp edges, rust, loose handles, worn coatings, warping, missing parts, and difficult-to-clean
surfaces.
4. Verify food-contact items are clean, dry, odor-free, and made of approved material.
5. Check that measuring, portioning, and temperature tools are accurate and matched to the intended use.
6. Remove damaged items from service immediately and place them in the approved hold or disposal area.
7. Record the item, quantity, defect, station, and replacement or repair need.
8. Restock each station to the approved par without mixing damaged or unverified items back into service.
9. Escalate repeated breakage, shortage, unsafe substitutions, or purchasing concerns to management.
10. Complete the smallwares count and manager verification before opening or shift release.


143. Smallwares Inspection SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The employee can identify the manufacturer instruction, shutdown control, performance limit, authorized service role,
and release requirement for smallwares inspection.
□ Separate clean items from dirty, damaged, unapproved, or employee-owned items.
□ Verify food-contact items are clean, dry, odor-free, and made of approved material.
□ Remove damaged items from service immediately and place them in the approved hold or disposal area.
□ Restock each station to the approved par without mixing damaged or unverified items back into service.
□ Complete the smallwares count and manager verification before opening or shift release.
• Performing smallwares inspection without manufacturer instructions, training, PPE, shutdown control, and an out-of-
service process.
• Cleaning, adjusting, or reaching into energized, hot, pressurized, bladed, or moving equipment.
• Restarting after abnormal sound, odor, flame, leak, temperature, guard, cord, or control failure.
• Allowing an unqualified employee to repair or bypass a safety device.
• Returning equipment to service without testing, records, and manager or authorized-service release.

144. Knife Safety and Storage SOP

SOP ID V2-144 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To use, transport, clean, and store knives safely with approved cutting surfaces, handling methods,
racks or sheaths, and damage controls.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Use only approved knives matched to the task and inspect the blade, tip, handle, rivets, and guard before use.
2. Select a stable approved cutting board and place a damp towel or nonslip base underneath when required.
3. Keep the cutting area clear, use required cut-resistant PPE, and maintain a controlled body position.
4. Cut away from the body with the guiding hand protected and fingers curled out of the blade path.
5. Do not catch a falling knife, carry it hidden, leave it in a sink, or place it under towels or food.
6. Carry a knife point down at the side and announce movement through crowded or blind areas.
7. Clean one knife at a time using the approved method and never reach into cloudy or filled sinks.
8. Sharpen or hone only with training and approved equipment and remove damaged knives from service.
9. Store clean dry knives in the approved rack, sheath, or secured location with blades protected.
10. Report cuts, near misses, damage, loss, and unauthorized knives and obtain manager follow-up.

145. Unsafe Equipment Lockout and Manager
Escalation SOP

SOP ID V2-145 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To remove unsafe equipment from service immediately through clear lockout identification, access
control, manager notification, repair tracking, and approved release.
APPLIES TO: Trained equipment users, kitchen leads, maintenance contacts, and managers.
RESPONSIBILITY: The operator uses and cleans equipment as approved. Management removes unsafe
equipment from service and controls repair and return.
WHEN USED: Before use, during operation, at shutdown or cleaning, and whenever damage, failure, or unsafe
performance is observed.
• Manufacturer instructions and current equipment SOP
• Required cleaning tools, chemicals, and PPE
• Lockout or out-of-service tags
• Inspection and preventive-maintenance log
• Manager, service, and vendor contacts
• Only trained employees use, clean, adjust, or inspect equipment.
• Guards, cords, gas, heat, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts are controlled.
• Unsafe equipment is shut down, tagged, and not returned to use without authorization.
• Cleaning, service, failure, and preventive-maintenance records are completed accurately.
1. Review manufacturer instructions, the current SOP, service history, and authorized task.
2. Inspect the work area and control heat, electricity, gas, water, blades, pressure, chemicals, and moving parts.
3. Shut down, cool, unplug, isolate, or tag equipment as required before contact.
4. Stop use, disconnect or isolate energy when trained and safe, and keep others away.
5. Attach an out-of-service tag that identifies equipment, hazard, time, and reporting employee.
6. Notify management and prevent removal of the tag except through the authorized repair and release process.
7. Document repair, testing, sanitation, and manager approval before return to service.
8. Clean and sanitize as required, reassemble correctly, and test only within authorization.
9. Record condition, service, parts, food impact, downtime, and release decision.
10. Return equipment to use only after manager or qualified service approval.

□ Notify management and prevent removal of the tag except through the authorized repair and release process.
□ Clean and sanitize as required, reassemble correctly, and test only within authorization.
□ Return equipment to use only after manager or qualified service approval.

PART 8

FACILITY, WASTE & PEST CONTROL

15 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs control exterior and entry safety, patios, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, waste, oil, dumpsters,
pests, and vendor access.


146. Building Exterior Walkthrough SOP

SOP ID V2-146 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To inspect the building exterior for lighting, access, damage, drainage, waste, pest evidence, security,
and conditions requiring correction or vendor support.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Walk the full building perimeter before opening, at the scheduled review, and before final lock-up.
2. Inspect doors, windows, walls, roofline, utility entries, vents, locks, cameras, and signs of forced access.
3. Check sidewalks, curbs, ramps, stairs, handrails, drainage, mats, and accessible routes for hazards.
4. Look for broken glass, needles, waste, standing water, ice, grease, pests, graffiti, and unauthorized storage.
5. Verify exterior lighting, address numbers, exits, fire lanes, utility areas, and emergency access remain clear.
6. Inspect grease, dumpster, delivery, propane, gas, electrical, and employee-entry zones for leaks or damage.
7. Do not enter or disturb an area that suggests crime, fire, gas, electrical, structural, or hazardous-material danger.
8. Correct minor housekeeping issues and isolate larger hazards with barriers or restricted access.
9. Contact management, property, vendor, utility, security, police, or emergency services as appropriate.
10. Record findings, photos, owner, deadline, temporary control, and reinspection.

□ Inspect doors, windows, walls, roofline, utility entries, vents, locks, cameras, and signs of forced access.

□ Correct minor housekeeping issues and isolate larger hazards with barriers or restricted access.
□ Record findings, photos, owner, deadline, temporary control, and reinspection.
• Completing building exterior walkthrough from a desk or checklist without inspecting the actual area and surrounding
conditions.
• Leaving a hazard, leak, pest source, waste problem, access issue, or vendor action without an owner and deadline.
• Allowing contractors or waste movement to contaminate food areas or bypass access controls.
• Failing to protect guests, employees, traffic, drainage, lighting, and emergency access.
• Closing the record without reinspection, documentation, and manager verification.

147. Parking Lot and Entry Safety SOP

SOP ID V2-147 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep parking and entry areas safe, lighted, accessible, unobstructed, and free of hazards that could
injure guests or employees.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Inspect the parking lot and entry routes for traffic, pedestrian, lighting, surface, weather, and security conditions.
2. Check potholes, curbs, wheel stops, ramps, stairs, crosswalks, drains, ice, water, grease, and loose debris.
3. Verify accessible spaces, access aisles, curb ramps, and entry paths are clear and not used for storage or delivery.
4. Confirm signs, lane markings, speed controls, exterior lights, cameras, and emergency access are visible and working.
5. Remove or isolate broken glass, trip hazards, abandoned items, and spills without entering vehicle danger.
6. Coordinate deliveries, employee parking, rideshare, curbside, and guest pickup so routes do not conflict.
7. Do not direct employees into active traffic, severe weather, criminal activity, or unsafe cleanup conditions.
8. Notify property management, vendors, towing, utility, security, or emergency services when needed.
9. Communicate temporary closures or alternate routes to hosts, curbside staff, employees, and guests.
10. Document the hazard, location, temporary control, responsible party, and reinspection.

148. Patio Opening and Closing SOP

SOP ID V2-148 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To open and close the patio through verified furniture, cleanliness, weather, lighting, access, service,
safety, and security checks.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Review weather, temperature, wind, lighting, staffing, reservations, occupancy, and local patio restrictions.
2. Inspect the surface, railings, gates, umbrellas, heaters, furniture, planters, exits, and accessible route.
3. Secure or remove unstable, wet, damaged, hot, sharp, or wind-sensitive items before seating guests.
4. Verify tables and chairs are clean, level, spaced, and arranged within approved capacity and egress.
5. Test approved lighting, fans, heaters, music, POS, communication, and service-station equipment.
6. Establish weather-monitoring, closing authority, guest-relocation, and employee communication responsibilities.
7. Stop seating and clear the patio for lightning, dangerous wind, extreme temperature, smoke, flooding, or another unsafe
condition.
8. During closing, account for guests and checks and move food, alcohol, cash, devices, and supplies indoors.
9. Shut off and secure heaters, umbrellas, gates, furniture, lighting, and equipment according to policy.
10. Document damage, weather closure, guest relocation, maintenance, and manager verification.

149. Restroom Supply Control SOP

SOP ID V2-149 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep restrooms supplied with soap, hand-drying materials, tissue, liners, and other required items
and escalate any condition that prevents safe use.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Review the restroom supply par, approved products, dispenser types, storage location, and check frequency.
2. Inspect toilet tissue, towels or dryers, soap, sanitizer when provided, liners, seat covers, and baby-changing supplies.
3. Refill only clean approved product without touching dispensing surfaces with dirty hands or packages.
4. Do not place supplies on floors, toilets, sinks, trash lids, or chemical-storage shelves.
5. Check dispensers for jams, damage, leaks, sharp edges, loose mounting, and accessibility.
6. Keep backup stock secured, dry, labeled, and separate from chemicals and employee personal items.
7. Record unusually high use, theft, recurring shortages, vendor substitutions, and dispenser failures.
8. Notify the manager immediately when soap, hand drying, toilet function, or another required condition cannot be maintained.
9. Restrict a fixture or restroom when supplies or equipment create an unsafe or unusable condition.
10. Complete the check log and manager verification before reopening or ending the shift.

151. HVAC Comfort and Ventilation Check SOP

SOP ID V2-151 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain safe and comfortable HVAC and ventilation conditions by checking temperatures, airflow,
odors, hoods, exhaust, complaints, and dangerous-atmosphere triggers.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Review indoor temperature, humidity, odor, smoke, condensation, hood, makeup-air, and guest or employee complaints.
2. Check thermostats, vents, returns, filters, doors, fans, kitchen hoods, restroom exhaust, and known problem areas.
3. Verify kitchen ventilation operates before heat-, smoke-, steam-, or gas-producing equipment is used.
4. Keep vents and returns clear and do not alter controls, filters, dampers, or equipment without authorization.
5. Separate comfort concerns from smoke, gas, carbon monoxide, refrigerant, electrical, or fire hazards.
6. Stop affected equipment and evacuate or contact emergency services when a dangerous atmosphere is suspected.
7. Adjust approved settings or service zones only within manager authority and monitor the result.
8. Communicate realistic limitations and seating or work-area changes to employees and guests.
9. Contact the landlord or qualified HVAC service provider for repeated or material failure.
10. Record readings, complaints, equipment status, actions, service contact, and recheck.

□ Record readings, complaints, equipment status, actions, service contact, and recheck.
• Completing HVAC comfort and ventilation check from a desk or checklist without inspecting the actual area and
surrounding conditions.

152. Plumbing Leak Response SOP

SOP ID V2-152 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control plumbing leaks quickly by identifying the source, protecting people and food, restricting
affected areas, stopping water when authorized, and arranging repair.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Identify the source, location, flow, water condition, electrical exposure, food impact, and whether the leak is controlled.
2. Stop nearby work and protect guests and employees with barriers and wet-floor controls.
3. Shut off the local fixture or water supply only when trained and authorized.
4. Move or protect food, packaging, utensils, chemicals, records, and electrical equipment from water exposure.
5. Do not use contaminated water, ceiling water, sewage, or unknown leakage for any cleaning or food process.
6. Contact management, property, plumber, utility, or emergency services based on severity.
7. Collect water only with approved containers and prevent flow into food areas, electrical systems, and public routes.
8. Discard or hold affected food and single-use items and clean and sanitize exposed areas after repair.
9. Verify plumbing, water quality, floors, walls, ceilings, equipment, and slip hazards before reopening.
10. Document the leak, cause, damage, product disposition, repair, sanitation, and follow-up.

□ Discard or hold affected food and single-use items and clean and sanitize exposed areas after repair.

conditions.

• Allowing contractors or waste movement to contaminate food areas or bypass access controls.

153. Flooding and Plumbing Backup SOP

SOP ID V2-153 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To respond safely to flooding, sewage backup, major leaks, overflowing drains, or plumbing failure.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads response. Employees follow direction.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: Any time water leaks, drains overflow, sewage backs up, restrooms fail, floors flood, or plumbing
creates unsafe conditions.
• Emergency contact list
• Plumber / maintenance contact
• Wet floor signs
• Mop / squeegee if safe
• Gloves / protective equipment if required
• Affected area is isolated, contamination risk is controlled, proper help is contacted, and the area is reopened only.
• Do not walk through contaminated water unless trained and protected.
• Stop service in affected unsafe areas.
• Protect food, clean dishes, utensils, and equipment.
1. Identify source and affected area if safe.
2. Notify manager immediately.
3. Keep guests and employees away from affected area.
4. Stop food prep or service in affected areas if needed.
5. Protect food, clean items, and equipment.
6. Use wet floor signs or block area.
7. Contact plumber, maintenance, ownership, or emergency support.
8. Contact health department if required.
9. Clean only according to approved procedure.
10. Do not reopen area until safe and approved. Document incident and follow-up.

154. Waste Separation SOP

SOP ID V2-154 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To separate waste, recycling, cardboard, food, oil, chemicals, and other streams according to
restaurant policy and local requirements.
APPLIES TO: Utility, dish, prep, kitchen, closing, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees separate and move waste safely. Management verifies containment, vendor
requirements, records, and site condition.
WHEN USED: During collection, separation, transport, storage, vendor pickup, spill response, and closing
verification.
• Approved containers, liners, lids, and labels
• Required PPE and spill-cleanup materials
• Carts, dollies, or used-oil transfer tools
• Waste, recycling, or vendor schedule
• Manager verification record
• Waste, cardboard, grease, oil, recycling, and food are separated as required.
• Containers remain covered, leak-free, labeled, and located away from food and clean equipment.
• Employees use safe lifting, transport, and spill controls.
• Management verifies vendor service, site condition, and required records.
1. Identify each approved waste stream including landfill, recycling, cardboard, glass, compost, grease, chemicals, and regulated
waste.
2. Confirm containers are correctly labeled, lined, located, intact, and not used for food or clean supplies.
3. Remove liquids, food residue, contaminants, and prohibited materials as required before separation.
4. Place each item into the correct stream without reaching into bags or compacting by hand.
5. Keep broken glass, sharps, chemicals, batteries, lamps, and hazardous items in their approved containers.
6. Do not overfill containers or allow waste to block exits, fire equipment, drains, or food routes.
7. Move waste through the approved route and schedule to prevent guest, food, and clean-item exposure.
8. Correct contamination of recycling or compost streams before pickup when safe.
9. Record unusual volume, prohibited items, vendor rejection, spill, injury, or recurring sorting failure.
10. Clean the area, replace liners, wash hands, and obtain manager verification.

155. Cardboard Breakdown and Recycling SOP

SOP ID V2-155 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To break down and store cardboard safely without blocking exits, contaminating food areas, attracting
pests, or exceeding recycling capacity.
APPLIES TO: Utility, dish, prep, kitchen, closing, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees separate and move waste safely. Management verifies containment, vendor
requirements, records, and site condition.
WHEN USED: During collection, separation, transport, storage, vendor pickup, spill response, and closing
verification.
• Approved containers, liners, lids, and labels
• Required PPE and spill-cleanup materials
• Carts, dollies, or used-oil transfer tools
• Waste, recycling, or vendor schedule
• Manager verification record
• Waste, cardboard, grease, oil, recycling, and food are separated as required.
• Containers remain covered, leak-free, labeled, and located away from food and clean equipment.
• Employees use safe lifting, transport, and spill controls.
• Management verifies vendor service, site condition, and required records.
1. Remove food, grease, plastic liners, waxed material, and other nonaccepted contents from cardboard.
2. Keep boxes away from food preparation, clean storage, exits, electrical panels, and ignition sources.
3. Break boxes down using the approved safe method and cutting tool away from the body.
4. Flatten cardboard fully and remove or control staples, bands, and sharp edges.
5. Stack or bale within the approved height, weight, capacity, and equipment rules.
6. Keep the storage area dry and inspect for pests, moisture, product residue, and hidden trash.
7. Do not climb, jump on, or hand-compress cardboard in compactors or balers.
8. Operate balers or compactors only with training, guards, interlocks, and lockout procedures.
9. Move cardboard at the scheduled time and secure exterior containers against wind and unauthorized access.
10. Record equipment, vendor, pest, injury, overflow, or contamination problems and manager follow-up.

156. Used Oil Handling SOP

SOP ID V2-156 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To transfer used cooking oil safely into approved containers while preventing burns, spills, drain
contamination, unauthorized access, and unrecorded loss.
APPLIES TO: Utility, dish, prep, kitchen, closing, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees separate and move waste safely. Management verifies containment, vendor
requirements, records, and site condition.
WHEN USED: During collection, separation, transport, storage, vendor pickup, spill response, and closing
verification.
• Approved containers, liners, lids, and labels
• Required PPE and spill-cleanup materials
• Carts, dollies, or used-oil transfer tools
• Waste, recycling, or vendor schedule
• Manager verification record
• Waste, cardboard, grease, oil, recycling, and food are separated as required.
• Containers remain covered, leak-free, labeled, and located away from food and clean equipment.
• Employees use safe lifting, transport, and spill controls.
• Management verifies vendor service, site condition, and required records.
1. Confirm the approved used-oil container, capacity, lid, transfer route, pickup schedule, and spill supplies.
2. Allow oil to reach the approved handling condition and wear required heat-, slip-, and splash-resistant PPE.
3. Use only approved closed containers, carts, hoses, and fittings; never use open buckets through guest or crowded routes.
4. Keep water, ice, chemicals, food debris, and incompatible materials out of used oil.
5. Move oil slowly through the designated route and stop if the floor, container, valve, or path is unsafe.
6. Transfer oil without overfilling, splashing, or leaving lids and valves unsecured.
7. Clean drips immediately using the approved grease-spill method and keep drains protected.
8. Record quantity, date, employee, pickup, spill, loss, or vendor issue when required.
9. Report leaks, damage, overflow, theft, pests, or unsafe vendor equipment.
10. Secure the area, wash hands, and obtain manager verification of containment and documentation.

157. Dumpster and Trash Area Control SOP

SOP ID V2-157 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To keep dumpster and trash areas closed, clean, drained, pest-resistant, accessible to approved
vendors, and separated from food and guest traffic.
APPLIES TO: Utility, dish, prep, kitchen, closing, and management employees.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees separate and move waste safely. Management verifies containment, vendor
requirements, records, and site condition.
WHEN USED: During collection, separation, transport, storage, vendor pickup, spill response, and closing
verification.
• Approved containers, liners, lids, and labels
• Required PPE and spill-cleanup materials
• Carts, dollies, or used-oil transfer tools

• Management verifies vendor service, site condition, and required records.
1. Inspect dumpster and trash containers, lids, gates, locks, pads, drains, lighting, and surrounding access.
2. Verify bags are tied, containers are not overfilled, and waste is separated into approved streams.
3. Keep lids and gates closed except during active use and prevent loose waste, leaks, and windblown debris.
4. Place bags inside containers without hand-compressing, climbing, or leaving waste on the ground.
5. Keep grease, chemicals, sharps, glass, pallets, and bulky items in their approved locations.
6. Clean spills and residue promptly and remove standing water and pest attractants.
7. Maintain clear fire, vehicle, vendor, and emergency access and prevent employee exposure to traffic.
8. Report missed pickups, illegal dumping, damaged containers, pests, odors, leaks, and drainage issues.
9. Contact the approved waste vendor or property manager and assign temporary controls when service fails.
10. Document inspection, corrective action, vendor contact, and manager verification.

158. Pest Prevention SOP

SOP ID V2-158 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To prevent pests by controlling food, water, shelter, entry points, waste, deliveries, cleaning, and timely
licensed-vendor follow-up.
APPLIES TO: All employees, managers, and approved pest-control providers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees prevent access and report evidence. Management protects food, documents
findings, and coordinates licensed treatment.
WHEN USED: During routine prevention checks and immediately when evidence, entry points, damage, or a
sighting is found.
• Pest-prevention checklist and sighting log
• Food-protection and cleaning supplies
• Flashlight and approved monitoring points
• Licensed pest-provider and manager contacts
• Photos or evidence record when safe
• Food, packaging, water, shelter, and entry points are controlled to prevent pests.
• Employees never apply unapproved pesticides or move pest evidence into food areas.
• Affected food and surfaces are protected, evaluated, cleaned, and documented.
• Management coordinates corrective action with an approved licensed provider.
1. Review pest-control reports, prior sightings, service recommendations, and unresolved structural or sanitation issues.
2. Inspect exterior doors, sweeps, windows, drains, pipes, roof or wall openings, receiving points, and waste areas.
3. Keep food, packaging, and utensils off the floor, covered, dated, rotated, and protected from contamination.
4. Remove standing water, crumbs, grease, clutter, cardboard, and inaccessible buildup that provide food or shelter.
5. Maintain tight waste lids, clean dumpsters, dry mop areas, screened openings, and working door closures.
6. Inspect traps and monitoring devices only as authorized; do not move or apply pesticides without approval.
7. Correct or assign sanitation, storage, building, vendor, and maintenance actions with deadlines.
8. Train employees to report evidence immediately and never conceal, sweep away, or discard evidence before documentation.
9. Escalate active infestation, food contamination, or unsafe pesticide concerns to management and the licensed provider.
10. Record the inspection, findings, corrective actions, provider contact, and follow-up date.

□ Inspect traps and monitoring devices only as authorized; do not move or apply pesticides without approval.
□ Train employees to report evidence immediately and never conceal, sweep away, or discard evidence before
documentation.
□ Record the inspection, findings, corrective actions, provider contact, and follow-up date.

• Leaving a hazard, leak, pest source, waste problem, access issue, or vendor action without an owner and deadline.

159. Pest Sighting Documentation SOP

SOP ID V2-159 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To document pest sightings and evidence accurately, protect affected food and areas, notify
management, and support licensed pest-control correction.
APPLIES TO: All employees, managers, and approved pest-control providers.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees prevent access and report evidence. Management protects food, documents
findings, and coordinates licensed treatment.
WHEN USED: During routine prevention checks and immediately when evidence, entry points, damage, or a
sighting is found.
• Pest-prevention checklist and sighting log
• Food-protection and cleaning supplies
• Flashlight and approved monitoring points
• Licensed pest-provider and manager contacts
• Photos or evidence record when safe
• Food, packaging, water, shelter, and entry points are controlled to prevent pests.
• Employees never apply unapproved pesticides or move pest evidence into food areas.
• Affected food and surfaces are protected, evaluated, cleaned, and documented.
• Management coordinates corrective action with an approved licensed provider.
1. Stop work in the immediate area and protect food, utensils, packaging, and guests from possible contamination.
2. Notify the manager and record the exact date, time, location, species or description, number, and observed behavior.
3. Photograph or preserve evidence when safe without touching droppings, insects, traps, or contaminated material
unnecessarily.
4. Identify affected food, surfaces, packages, equipment, drains, openings, and nearby pest-monitoring devices.
5. Hold or discard contaminated product only under manager direction and document the quantity and reason.
6. Clean and sanitize the area after evidence is documented and according to pest-provider or health requirements.
7. Inspect adjacent areas for entry points, moisture, food sources, nesting, gnawing, droppings, or damaged packaging.
8. Contact the licensed pest-control provider and provide the sighting record and requested access.
9. Complete building, sanitation, storage, or vendor corrective actions and schedule reinspection.
10. Close the record only after management verifies corrective action and follow-up results.

160. Vendor and Contractor Access SOP

SOP ID V2-160 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control vendor and contractor access through identity verification, approved scope and schedule,
escorts, food protection, safety rules, and completion records.
APPLIES TO: Opening or closing employees, utility employees, managers, and approved contractors.
RESPONSIBILITY: The assigned employee inspects and reports conditions. Management assigns correction,
restricts unsafe areas, and verifies completion.
WHEN USED: During opening and closing inspections and whenever a building, utility, access, or environmental
condition changes.
• Current facility inspection or opening checklist
• Flashlight, caution signs, barriers, and PPE
• Maintenance and vendor contact information
• Photo, work-order, or issue documentation
• Manager communication
• Guest and employee access is restricted whenever a condition is unsafe.
• Utilities, exits, lighting, ventilation, plumbing, surfaces, and access routes are kept serviceable.
• Temporary controls do not replace timely repair.
• Management records the condition, owner, deadline, vendor status, and final verification.
1. Review the inspection route, prior work orders, weather, events, and opening or closing status.
2. Inspect from a safe position and keep guests and employees away from immediate hazards.
3. Photograph or document the condition and notify the responsible manager.
4. Verify identity, company, purpose, appointment, authorization, and required credentials before access.
5. Sign the visitor in and explain restricted areas, hygiene, safety, escort, and emergency requirements.
6. Control keys, doors, roof, utility, office, food, and after-hours access throughout the visit.
7. Inspect completed work, recover credentials, record departure, and document damage or unfinished work.
8. Maintain barriers or restrictions until repair, cleaning, or approved temporary control is complete.
9. Reinspect actual conditions and verify safe access and normal operation.
10. Close the work order with evidence, dates, responsible parties, and remaining follow-up.


160. Vendor and Contractor Access SOP -

□ The employee can identify the inspected area, safety or environmental limit, vendor or manager contact, record, and
recheck for vendor and contractor access.
□ Inspect from a safe position and keep guests and employees away from immediate hazards.
□ Verify identity, company, purpose, appointment, authorization, and required credentials before access.
□ Control keys, doors, roof, utility, office, food, and after-hours access throughout the visit.
□ Maintain barriers or restrictions until repair, cleaning, or approved temporary control is complete.
□ Close the work order with evidence, dates, responsible parties, and remaining follow-up.
• Completing vendor and contractor access from a desk or checklist without inspecting the actual area and surrounding
conditions.
• Leaving a hazard, leak, pest source, waste problem, access issue, or vendor action without an owner and deadline.
• Allowing contractors or waste movement to contaminate food areas or bypass access controls.
• Failing to protect guests, employees, traffic, drainage, lighting, and emergency access.
• Closing the record without reinspection, documentation, and manager verification.
• Facility area, route, vendor, waste stream, pest zone, or inspection map: ________________________
• Required tools, PPE, locks, lighting, signage, containers, or service contacts: _________________
• Inspection, pickup, cleaning, response, or contractor-access schedule: __________________________
• Safety, accessibility, environmental, waste, plumbing, or pest threshold: ______________________
• Local code, lease, insurance, environmental, or licensed-vendor requirement reviewed: __________

PART 9

EMPLOYEE & GUEST SAFETY /
EMERGENCY RESPONSE

10 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs guide immediate response to injuries, choking, fire, gas, outages, severe weather, violence,
robbery, and missing children.


161. Employee Injury SOP

SOP ID V2-161 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect an injured employee, obtain appropriate emergency or medical help, secure the hazard,
complete required reporting, and prevent recurrence.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response. Employees notify the manager immediately when an injury
happens.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Any time an employee is cut, burned, falls, slips, strains, becomes ill, is exposed to chemicals, or is
otherwise injured at work.
• First aid kit
• Employee Injury Form
• Emergency contact information
• Phone
• Manager log
• Life-threatening or uncertain injuries receive immediate emergency help and care only within current training.
• The area, equipment, food, chemical, spill, or other hazard is secured to prevent additional injury.
• Required workers' compensation, OSHA, insurer, and company reporting is routed through authorized management.
• Medical privacy, good-faith reporting, nonretaliation, corrective action, and return-to-work routing are protected.
1. Stop work, protect the injured employee from additional harm, and notify the manager immediately.
2. Call 911 for serious, life-threatening, or uncertain conditions and follow dispatcher instructions.
3. Provide first aid only within current training, available equipment, and company policy.
4. Do not move the employee unless remaining creates immediate danger or trained responders direct movement.
5. Isolate equipment, chemicals, food, broken glass, blood, spills, or other hazards that could cause another injury.
6. Arrange authorized medical evaluation or transportation and do not require the employee to drive when unsafe.
7. Preserve the scene, equipment, video, photos, witness information, and work records when appropriate.
8. Complete required injury, workers' compensation, OSHA, insurer, or company notifications through authorized management.
9. Protect private medical information and prohibit retaliation for good-faith reporting.
10. Assign corrective action, return-to-work routing, equipment release, sanitation, retraining, and follow-up.


161. Employee Injury SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for employee injury.
□ Call 911 for serious, life-threatening, or uncertain conditions and follow dispatcher instructions.
□ Do not move the employee unless remaining creates immediate danger or trained responders direct movement.
□ Arrange authorized medical evaluation or transportation and do not require the employee to drive when unsafe.
□ Complete required injury, workers' compensation, OSHA, insurer, or company notifications through authorized
management.
□ Assign corrective action, return-to-work routing, equipment release, sanitation, retraining, and follow-up.
• Moving the employee, providing care, or transporting them beyond training and policy.
• Leaving the original hazard exposed or allowing equipment back into service.
• Delaying emergency help, medical evaluation, or required reporting.
• Recording blame, diagnosis, or unnecessary medical details instead of objective facts.
• Failing to assign corrective action, return-to-work routing, and follow-up.

162. Guest Injury SOP

SOP ID V2-162 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To assist an injured guest promptly, secure and preserve the scene, protect private information, and
complete authorized incident follow-up without admitting fault.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response. Employees notify the manager immediately.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Whenever a guest is injured, reports an injury, or alleges that an injury occurred in or near the
restaurant.
• Guest Injury Form
• Phone
• First aid kit
• Witness information
• Manager log
• The guest receives prompt assistance and emergency services when needed without argument, admission of fault, or
unsupported promises.
• The scene and suspected hazard are secured, preserved, documented, and corrected before reopening.
• Witnesses, video, receipts, product, equipment, and statements are preserved objectively and separately.
• Private information and required ownership, insurer, security, property, or authority notifications are protected.
1. Notify the manager and approach the guest calmly without admitting fault or debating responsibility.
2. Call 911 for serious, life-threatening, or uncertain conditions and follow dispatcher instructions.
3. Offer only assistance within training and policy; do not move the guest unless immediate danger requires it.
4. Secure the area and isolate the spill, object, furniture, equipment, food, chemical, or other suspected hazard.
5. Obtain the guest's name and contact information if voluntarily provided and record the exact reported location and time.
6. Identify witnesses separately and preserve video, photos, receipts, product, equipment, and scene information.
7. Do not ask the guest to sign a release, minimize symptoms, or promise payment or a specific outcome.
8. Notify ownership, insurer, security, property management, or authorities according to policy.
9. Complete objective incident documentation and protect medical, payment, and personal information.
10. Correct the hazard, verify the area before reopening, and assign guest and management follow-up.


162. Guest Injury SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for guest injury.
□ Call 911 for serious, life-threatening, or uncertain conditions and follow dispatcher instructions.
□ Secure the area and isolate the spill, object, furniture, equipment, food, chemical, or other suspected hazard.
□ Identify witnesses separately and preserve video, photos, receipts, product, equipment, and scene information.
□ Notify ownership, insurer, security, property management, or authorities according to policy.
□ Correct the hazard, verify the area before reopening, and assign guest and management follow-up.
• Arguing, admitting fault, minimizing symptoms, or promising payment or a specific outcome.
• Cleaning or reopening the scene before evidence and the hazard are documented.
• Combining witness statements or failing to preserve video, receipts, product, or equipment.
• Exposing private guest information or discussing the incident publicly.
• Closing the record without hazard correction, required notification, and guest follow-up.

163. Choking Emergency SOP

SOP ID V2-163 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To coordinate an immediate, trained response to suspected choking while contacting emergency
medical services and protecting the person until care is transferred.
APPLIES TO: All employees, managers, designated emergency leaders, and outside responders.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the emergency or safety condition is observed or reported.
• Posted emergency address and 911 information
• Trained choking-response and CPR/AED procedures
• First-aid kit and AED location information
• Employee communication and responder-meeting assignment
• Guest incident and witness documentation form
• Emergency services and a trained responder are contacted immediately for severe or uncertain choking.
• Employees perform only actions included in current approved training or dispatcher instructions.
• The person is not left alone and is not given food, drink, or unapproved care during the emergency.
• Care transfers to responders and the incident is documented with relevant food, object, check, video, and witness information.
1. Recognize possible choking, alert the manager, and call for a trained responder immediately.
2. Call 911 for severe or uncertain choking and provide the restaurant name, exact address, and location.
3. Follow current approved training and dispatcher instructions for a responsive adult, child, or infant.
4. Only trained responders perform choking care; untrained employees clear the area and guide emergency responders.
5. Do not give food or drink, perform blind finger sweeps, or use an unapproved technique.
6. If the person becomes unresponsive, trained responders follow current CPR/AED training and dispatcher instructions.
7. Send an employee to meet responders and bring first-aid or AED equipment if directed.
8. Note the time, known food or object, actions taken, and changes in condition without diagnosing.
9. Transfer care to emergency responders and preserve relevant food, object, check, video, and witness information.
10. Complete incident documentation, replace used emergency supplies, and assign manager follow-up.


163. Choking Emergency SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for choking emergency.
□ Call 911 for severe or uncertain choking and provide the restaurant name, exact address, and location.
□ Only trained responders perform choking care; untrained employees clear the area and guide emergency responders.
□ If the person becomes unresponsive, trained responders follow current CPR/AED training and dispatcher instructions.
□ Note the time, known food or object, actions taken, and changes in condition without diagnosing.
□ Complete incident documentation, replace used emergency supplies, and assign manager follow-up.
• Delaying 911 or trained-response activation while searching for a manager.
• Performing an untrained or unapproved technique or giving food or drink.
• Leaving the person alone or crowding the area and blocking responders.
• Failing to follow dispatcher instructions when the person's condition changes.
• Discarding the object or failing to document actions, witnesses, and transfer of care.

• Evacuation, shelter, assembly, accountability, or responder-access plan: ________________________

164. Fire SOP

SOP ID V2-164 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect guests and employees during a fire through immediate alarm, emergency notification,
evacuation, accountability, responder support, and controlled recovery.
APPLIES TO: All employees, managers, designated emergency leaders, and outside responders.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the emergency or safety condition is observed or reported.
• Fire alarm and posted 911 information
• Current evacuation map and assembly locations
• Correctly classified fire extinguishers and kitchen Class K equipment
• Employee and guest accountability method
• Fire incident, utility, and reopening checklist
• Alarm, 911 notification, evacuation, and accountability are not delayed to protect cash, food, equipment, or property.
• Only trained employees may use the correct extinguisher on a small controllable fire with a clear exit.
• Guests and employees remain outside until fire officials authorize reentry.
• Managers account for people and provide responders with utility, chemical, equipment, suppression-system, and missing-
person information.
1. Activate the fire alarm, call 911, and state the restaurant name, exact address, and known fire location.
2. Direct guests and employees to approved exits and assembly points without delaying for cash, product, or personal property.
3. Shut off equipment or utilities only when trained, authorized, and able to do so without delaying evacuation.
4. Use an extinguisher only if trained, the fire is small and controllable, the correct extinguisher is available, and a clear exit
remains behind the user.
5. Do not enter smoke, open hot doors, fight a spreading fire, use elevators, or block responder access.
6. Assist people with mobility, sensory, or communication needs using the approved evacuation plan.
7. Managers account for employees, guests, contractors, and known missing people at the assembly point.
8. Remain outside, prevent reentry, and follow fire-department instructions.
9. Tell responders about gas, fryers, suppression systems, chemicals, utilities, and anyone unaccounted for.
10. Notify ownership, preserve records, document the incident, and reopen only after official and management authorization.


164. Fire SOP - VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for fire.
□ Direct guests and employees to approved exits and assembly points without delaying for cash, product, or personal
property.
□ Use an extinguisher only if trained, the fire is small and controllable, the correct extinguisher is available, and a clear
exit remains behind the user.
□ Assist people with mobility, sensory, or communication needs using the approved evacuation plan.
□ Remain outside, prevent reentry, and follow fire-department instructions.
□ Notify ownership, preserve records, document the incident, and reopen only after official and management
authorization.
• Delaying alarm or evacuation to collect cash, finish orders, or save equipment.
• Fighting a spreading fire, entering smoke, or using the wrong extinguisher without a clear exit.
• Failing to assist people who need help or account for employees and guests.
• Reentering before fire officials authorize it.
• Reopening without documented inspection, repair, notification, and management approval.
