165. Gas Leak SOP

SOP ID V2-165 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect life during a suspected gas leak by preventing ignition, evacuating, contacting emergency
and utility responders, and prohibiting reentry until clearance.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response when safe. All employees follow emergency direction.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Any time gas odor, suspected gas leak, hissing gas sound, gas equipment issue, or carbon
monoxide concern is reported.
• Emergency contact list
• Phone used from safe area
• Emergency exits
• Utility provider contact
• Incident Form
• Suspected gas leaks trigger immediate evacuation and emergency/utility notification from a safe location.
• No one creates sparks, flames, electrical switching, phone use, or other ignition risk in the suspected area.
• Gas is shut off only by trained and authorized people when it can be done without entering danger or delaying evacuation.
• Reentry, restart, and reopening occur only after qualified authority clearance and documented testing.
1. Treat any gas odor, hissing, alarm, failed ignition, or suspected leak as an immediate hazard.
2. Do not create sparks or flames, operate electrical switches, start vehicles nearby, or use a phone in the suspected area.
3. Warn nearby people and begin evacuation using the approved route without attempting to locate the leak.
4. Shut off gas only if trained, authorized, and able to do so without entering danger or delaying evacuation.
5. Call 911 and the gas utility from a safe location and provide the exact address and known conditions.
6. Account for employees, guests, contractors, and anyone believed to remain inside.
7. Keep people away from the building, meters, tanks, vents, and ignition sources.
8. Do not reenter, restart equipment, or restore gas until the fire department, utility, or qualified authority clears the site.
9. Notify ownership, landlord, insurer, and service vendors according to the emergency plan.
10. Document the timeline, observations, actions, repairs, clearance, testing, and reopening approval.


165. Gas Leak SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for gas leak.
□ Do not create sparks or flames, operate electrical switches, start vehicles nearby, or use a phone in the suspected area.
□ Shut off gas only if trained, authorized, and able to do so without entering danger or delaying evacuation.
□ Account for employees, guests, contractors, and anyone believed to remain inside.
□ Do not reenter, restart equipment, or restore gas until the fire department, utility, or qualified authority clears the site.
□ Document the timeline, observations, actions, repairs, clearance, testing, and reopening approval.
• Using switches, phones, flames, vehicles, or equipment in the suspected area.
• Searching for the leak or shutting gas off without training and safe access.
• Allowing people to remain near the building, meter, tank, vent, or ignition source.
• Reentering or restarting equipment before qualified clearance.
• Failing to document repair, testing, authority clearance, and reopening approval.

166. Power Outage SOP

SOP ID V2-166 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To control a power outage so the restaurant protects life safety, food, refrigeration, ventilation,
payments, security, and an orderly closure or recovery.
APPLIES TO: All employees, managers, designated emergency leaders, and outside responders.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Immediately when the emergency or safety condition is observed or reported.
• Utility, electrician, landlord, and ownership contacts
• Flashlights and approved emergency lighting
• Manual order, payment, and guest-communication materials
• Calibrated thermometers, temperature logs, and timers
• Closure, food-disposition, and restart checklist
• Open flames, candles, unsafe wiring, and unapproved generators are prohibited.
• Operations stop when required lighting, egress, ventilation, refrigeration, sanitation, payment security, or building safety cannot
be maintained.
• Refrigeration doors remain closed and affected food is evaluated using documented time and temperature.
• Equipment, utilities, systems, food, transactions, and alarms are verified before reopening.
1. Confirm the outage scope and start time and identify any injury, fire, gas, electrical, elevator, or security hazard.
2. Notify management and contact the utility, landlord, electrician, or approved service provider from a safe location.
3. Stop unsafe cooking, ventilation-dependent equipment, powered doors, elevators, and other affected operations.
4. Use only approved emergency lighting; do not use candles, open flames, unsafe wiring, or unapproved generators.
5. Keep refrigeration doors closed and begin documented time-temperature monitoring for affected food.
6. Shift to approved manual order, payment, communication, and guest-accounting procedures only when safe.
7. Restrict or close operations when lighting, egress, ventilation, handwashing, refrigeration, payment, or security cannot be
maintained.
8. Communicate clearly with employees and guests and account for people during closure or evacuation.
9. After restoration, verify equipment, refrigeration, alarms, POS, ventilation, utilities, and food disposition before reopening.
10. Record the outage, temperatures, transactions, losses, service contacts, decisions, and manager approval.


166. Power Outage SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for power outage.
□ Notify management and contact the utility, landlord, electrician, or approved service provider from a safe location.
□ Use only approved emergency lighting; do not use candles, open flames, unsafe wiring, or unapproved generators.
□ Shift to approved manual order, payment, communication, and guest-accounting procedures only when safe.
□ Communicate clearly with employees and guests and account for people during closure or evacuation.
□ Record the outage, temperatures, transactions, losses, service contacts, decisions, and manager approval.
• Using candles, open flames, unsafe wiring, personal generators, or unapproved devices.
• Opening refrigeration repeatedly or failing to track product time and temperature.
• Continuing service without safe lighting, egress, ventilation, sanitation, payment, or security.
• Losing control of manual checks, payments, online orders, or employee/guest accountability.
• Restarting before equipment, alarms, food, systems, and utilities are verified.

167. Tornado and Severe Weather SOP

SOP ID V2-167 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect guests and employees during tornado warnings, severe storms, dangerous winds, lightning,
flooding risk, or other serious weather.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response. All employees follow manager direction.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: When severe weather warnings, tornado warnings, dangerous weather alerts, or unsafe weather
conditions affect the restaurant.
• Weather alert source
• Phone
• Emergency contact list
• Designated shelter area
• Flashlights if available
• Official alerts and the site-specific shelter plan control the response.
• Outdoor work stops before conditions become unsafe, and people move away from windows and exterior hazards.
• Employees, guests, contractors, and people needing assistance are accounted for as conditions permit.
• Movement and reopening wait for official all-clear information and a building safety inspection.
1. Monitor official alerts whenever severe weather is possible and confirm the designated shelter and communication method.
2. Stop patio, curbside, delivery, or outdoor work before conditions become unsafe.
3. Notify guests and employees clearly and move them away from windows, glass, exterior doors, and unsecured objects.
4. Direct everyone to the approved interior shelter area when a warning or manager decision requires shelter.
5. Assist people with mobility, sensory, communication, or medical needs using the site plan.
6. Account for employees, guests, contractors, and known missing people as conditions permit.
7. Do not send employees outside, allow avoidable departure, or rely on rumor instead of official information.
8. Remain sheltered until official information and management confirm the immediate threat has passed.
9. Inspect for structural damage, gas odor, water intrusion, electrical hazards, blocked exits, and injuries before movement or
reopening.
10. Contact responders when required and document alerts, shelter actions, accountability, damage, and follow-up.

• Sending employees outside or allowing avoidable departure during a warning.
• Sheltering near windows, exterior doors, unsecured equipment, or unapproved areas.
• Relying on rumor or social media instead of official alerts.
• Resuming movement or service before an all-clear and building inspection.

168. Active Threat or Violent Guest SOP

SOP ID V2-168 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect guests and employees during an active threat or violent incident through immediate escape
or secure shelter, emergency notification, accountability, and law-enforcement cooperation.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads when safe. All employees follow safety procedures and emergency direction.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Whenever a guest, employee, or outside person threatens violence, becomes physically aggressive,
displays a weapon, or creates a reasonable fear of immediate harm.
• Phone
• Emergency contact list
• Safe exits
• Incident Form
• Security contact if applicable
• Employees prioritize escape from danger, emergency notification, and law-enforcement instructions.
• No employee is expected to confront, pursue, film, negotiate with, or physically intervene against an active threat.
• Secure shelter is used only when escape is not possible, and people are not directed toward uncertain routes.
• The scene, evidence, private information, accountability, and post-incident support are protected.
1. Recognize the threat and prioritize immediate escape from danger when a safe route is available.
2. Call 911 when safe and provide the exact location, description, direction, weapon information, and known injuries.
3. Warn others without drawing attention to yourself and follow current law-enforcement or dispatcher instructions.
4. If escape is not possible, move to a secure location, lock or block access when appropriate, silence devices, and stay out of
view.
5. Do not confront, pursue, negotiate, film, or attempt physical intervention unless necessary to protect life and consistent with
training.
6. Keep guests and employees away from the threat and do not direct people toward an uncertain route.
7. When police arrive, keep hands visible, follow commands, and do not run toward officers.
8. Provide first aid only when the area is safe and within current training.
9. Preserve the scene and do not share unverified information publicly or on social media.
10. Account for people, notify ownership, complete objective records, and arrange employee and guest support.


168. Active Threat or Violent Guest SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for active threat or violent guest.
□ Call 911 when safe and provide the exact location, description, direction, weapon information, and known injuries.
□ If escape is not possible, move to a secure location, lock or block access when appropriate, silence devices, and stay
out of view.
□ Keep guests and employees away from the threat and do not direct people toward an uncertain route.
□ Provide first aid only when the area is safe and within current training.
□ Account for people, notify ownership, complete objective records, and arrange employee and guest support.
• Attempting to confront, follow, film, or negotiate with the threatening person.
• Directing people toward an exit or hallway without knowing whether it is safe.
• Using unsecured shelter, leaving devices audible, or drawing attention unnecessarily.
• Interfering with law enforcement or approaching officers with hidden hands.
• Sharing unverified details publicly or failing to preserve the scene and accountability record.

169. Robbery SOP

SOP ID V2-169 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect people first during a robbery or attempted robbery and preserve accurate evidence and
reporting after the immediate danger ends.
APPLIES TO: All employees follow safety rules. Manager leads response after the immediate danger has passed.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Any time robbery, attempted robbery, theft with threat, or forced cash demand occurs.
• Phone
• Emergency contact list
• Incident Form
• Camera / security process if applicable
• Ownership contact
• Life safety comes before cash or property; employees do not resist, argue, chase, or attempt detention.
• Alarms and emergency calls are made only when they can be completed without increasing danger.
• The scene, video, transaction records, touched objects, and separate witness accounts are preserved.
• Reopening and public discussion wait for law-enforcement and authorized management direction.
1. Stay calm, prioritize life safety, and do not resist, argue, chase, or make sudden movements.
2. Follow demands when directly threatened without volunteering additional access or information.
3. Activate an alarm only if it can be done without increasing danger and according to training.
4. Observe only details that can be noticed safely, such as appearance, voice, direction, vehicle, and weapon.
5. Do not block exits, attempt to detain the person, or follow outside.
6. When safe, lock access as directed, call 911, and provide the exact address and known facts.
7. Protect injured people and provide first aid only within training and when the area is safe.
8. Preserve the scene, notes, video, transaction records, touched objects, and separate witness accounts.
9. Notify ownership, security, bank, insurer, and other required contacts through authorized management.
10. Complete objective documentation and follow law-enforcement direction before reopening or discussing the event.


169. Robbery SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for robbery.
□ Follow demands when directly threatened without volunteering additional access or information.
□ Observe only details that can be noticed safely, such as appearance, voice, direction, vehicle, and weapon.
□ When safe, lock access as directed, call 911, and provide the exact address and known facts.
□ Preserve the scene, notes, video, transaction records, touched objects, and separate witness accounts.
□ Complete objective documentation and follow law-enforcement direction before reopening or discussing the event.
• Resisting, arguing, chasing, blocking the exit, or trying to detain the person.
• Activating an alarm or making a call when it increases immediate danger.
• Allowing employees to disturb touched objects, video, notes, or transaction evidence.
• Combining witness accounts before law-enforcement interviews.
• Reopening or discussing details publicly before authorized direction.

170. Missing Child or Lost Child SOP

SOP ID V2-170 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To coordinate a rapid, controlled response when a child is missing, separated from a guardian, or found
without a verified guardian.
APPLIES TO: Manager leads response. Hosts, servers, bussers, bartenders, and all employees assist as directed.
RESPONSIBILITY: Employees protect life, notify management, and follow trained emergency actions. Management
contacts responders, controls accountability, and documents.
WHEN USED: Any time a parent reports a missing child, a child is found alone, or employees suspect a child is
separated from a guardian.
• Phone
• Manager communication
• Building layout
• Incident Form
• Emergency contact process
• Management coordinates one immediate search plan and emergency-services contact when the child is not found promptly or
danger is suspected.
• A found child remains supervised in a visible safe area and is released only after identity is confirmed through the approved
process.
• Employees do not physically detain people, conduct uncoordinated off-property searches, or publicly share unnecessary child
information.
• Timing, zones searched, witnesses, video, guardian communication, and authority contact are documented.
1. Notify the manager immediately and obtain the child's name, age, clothing, description, last known location, and guardian
information.
2. Assign specific employees to search approved zones while maintaining supervision of their current safety responsibilities.
3. Check restrooms, dining areas, patio, lobby, entrance, pickup zones, and other safe areas without creating additional risk.
4. Observe exits and parking areas only as allowed by policy and safety conditions; do not physically detain people.
5. Call 911 promptly when the child is not found within the approved immediate-search period, danger is suspected, or the
guardian requests emergency help.
6. If a separated child is found, keep the child with the manager and a second employee in a visible safe area.
7. Release the child only after the manager and authorities, when involved, confirm the guardian according to policy.
8. Preserve video, witness, timing, and communication records and avoid broadcasting unnecessary personal information.
9. Do not post the child's information publicly or allow employees to conduct uncoordinated searches off property.
10. Complete the incident record, guardian communication, authority contact, and post-incident review.


170. Missing Child or Lost Child SOP - VERIFICATION
& CUSTOMIZATION

□ Employees can state the alarm/reporting method, evacuation or shelter action, accountability point, responder contact,
and prohibited actions for missing child or lost child.
□ Assign specific employees to search approved zones while maintaining supervision of their current safety
responsibilities.
□ Observe exits and parking areas only as allowed by policy and safety conditions; do not physically detain people.
□ If a separated child is found, keep the child with the manager and a second employee in a visible safe area.
□ Preserve video, witness, timing, and communication records and avoid broadcasting unnecessary personal information.
□ Complete the incident record, guardian communication, authority contact, and post-incident review.
• Delaying manager notification or allowing an uncoordinated search.
• Releasing a child without approved identity confirmation.
• Sending employees into unsafe parking, traffic, or off-property areas.
• Physically detaining people or publicly sharing unnecessary child information.
• Failing to document timing, search zones, witnesses, video, guardian, and authority contact.

PART 10

BUSINESS CONTINUITY & CRISIS
RECOVERY

5 ready-to-customize SOPs
These SOPs guide water and technology outages, emergency closure, reopening, incident documentation,
and recovery review.


171. Water Outage SOP

SOP ID V2-171 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To protect food safety, sanitation, hygiene, restrooms, and guest service during a water outage and
restore operations only after approved clearance.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads the response. All employees follow manager direction.
RESPONSIBILITY: Management activates the approved continuity plan, protects people and food, assigns
communication, and authorizes reopening or recovery.
WHEN USED: Any time water service stops, water pressure is lost, water is unsafe, or water use is restricted.
• Emergency contact list
• Utility contact
• Health department contact if required
• Manager Incident Form
• Food safety policy
• Food service does not continue without approved water for employee hygiene, sanitation, warewashing, restrooms, and
affected food processes.
• Local regulatory authority and utility direction control alternate methods, restrictions, restoration, and reopening.
• Food, ice, beverages, clean utensils, equipment, and transactions affected by the outage are identified and controlled.
• Lines, filters, ice equipment, plumbing, and sanitation are restored and verified before normal service resumes.
1. Confirm the outage or unsafe-water condition, affected fixtures, start time, cause, and estimated restoration.
2. Notify ownership, the utility, landlord, and local regulatory authority when required.
3. Stop food preparation, handwashing, warewashing, beverage, ice, restroom, and cleaning processes that lack safe water.
4. Evaluate whether approved alternate water, handwashing, restroom, and warewashing methods can support restricted
operations.
5. Protect food, ice, beverages, equipment, and clean utensils from contamination or unapproved water use.
6. Restrict service or close when employee hygiene, sanitation, plumbing, restrooms, or food-safety requirements cannot be
maintained.
7. Communicate the operating decision and limitations to employees, guests, vendors, and delivery partners.
8. Track affected food, temperatures, transactions, labor, and closure time while awaiting restoration.
9. After restoration, flush lines, clean and sanitize equipment, replace filters, discard affected ice or product, and verify plumbing.
10. Obtain required clearance and manager approval before reopening and retain the outage record.


171. Water Outage SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The manager can state the backup or closure trigger, operating authority, communication plan, required records, and
recovery approval for water outage.
□ Notify ownership, the utility, landlord, and local regulatory authority when required.
□ Evaluate whether approved alternate water, handwashing, restroom, and warewashing methods can support restricted
operations.
□ Restrict service or close when employee hygiene, sanitation, plumbing, restrooms, or food-safety requirements cannot
be maintained.
□ Track affected food, temperatures, transactions, labor, and closure time while awaiting restoration.
□ Obtain required clearance and manager approval before reopening and retain the outage record.
• Continuing normal service without safe handwashing, restrooms, warewashing, or sanitation.
• Using unapproved water or failing to control affected ice, beverages, filters, and utensils.
• Failing to notify the utility, ownership, or regulatory authority when required.
• Reopening before flushing, sanitation, plumbing, ice, filters, and product disposition are complete.
• Losing the outage timeline, temperature, transaction, closure, and approval record.

172. Internet Down SOP

SOP ID V2-172 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain controlled orders, payments, communications, and records during an internet or network
outage without weakening payment security.
APPLIES TO: Manager on duty leads response. Employees follow adjusted procedures.
RESPONSIBILITY: Management activates the approved continuity plan, protects people and food, assigns
communication, and authorizes reopening or recovery.
WHEN USED: Any time internet service is down or unstable.
• Internet provider contact
• Router / modem access if approved
• POS support contact
• Backup payment process
• Phone
• Only approved backup devices, networks, order methods, and payment processes are used.
• Credentials and payment data remain protected, and prohibited card information is never recorded.
• Active checks, online orders, tabs, gift cards, payments, and duplicate-transaction risk remain traceable.
• Affected sales channels stop when accurate orders, payment security, or audit records cannot be maintained.
1. Confirm whether the outage affects internet, local network, one device, payment processing, phones, online orders, or all
systems.
2. Notify management and contact the approved internet, network, POS, payment, or property support provider.
3. Protect credentials and payment data; do not use personal hotspots or unapproved devices without authorization.
4. Activate the approved manual order, guest-check, online-order, reservation, and communication process.
5. Determine whether card payments can be processed securely under the approved backup method; never record prohibited
card data.
6. Reconcile active checks, tabs, delivery orders, gift cards, discounts, and duplicate-payment risk.
7. Communicate service limits and delays to employees, guests, and delivery partners.
8. Restrict or stop affected sales channels when accurate orders, payment security, or audit records cannot be maintained.
9. After restoration, verify systems and enter or reconcile offline transactions without duplicating charges or orders.
10. Document the outage, support contacts, manual transactions, losses, variances, and manager review.


172. Internet Down SOP - VERIFICATION &

□ The manager can state the backup or closure trigger, operating authority, communication plan, required records, and
recovery approval for internet down.
□ Notify management and contact the approved internet, network, POS, payment, or property support provider.
□ Activate the approved manual order, guest-check, online-order, reservation, and communication process.
□ Reconcile active checks, tabs, delivery orders, gift cards, discounts, and duplicate-payment risk.
□ Restrict or stop affected sales channels when accurate orders, payment security, or audit records cannot be
maintained.
□ Document the outage, support contacts, manual transactions, losses, variances, and manager review.
• Using personal hotspots, devices, credentials, or payment methods without approval.
• Writing prohibited card data or losing control of offline checks and payments.
• Continuing online or card sales when orders or transactions cannot be traced securely.
• Reentering transactions after restoration without checking for duplicates.
• Ending the incident without reconciling checks, payments, orders, variances, and support actions.

173. Kitchen Display System Down SOP

SOP ID V2-173 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To maintain one accurate kitchen order queue when the kitchen display system fails and reconcile all
orders before normal processing resumes.
APPLIES TO: Manager leads response. Servers, bartenders, cooks, hosts, and cash handlers follow backup
procedure.
RESPONSIBILITY: Management activates the approved continuity plan, protects people and food, assigns
communication, and authorizes reopening or recovery.
WHEN USED: Any time POS system, terminals, printers, handhelds, kitchen display, or payment system stops
working.
• Backup order pads
• Pens
• Manual check forms
• Calculator
• Manager log
• One designated backup queue controls all paper or verbal kitchen orders.
• Every order retains table/server identity, time, modifiers, allergy information, firing status, and completion status.
• Allergy and high-risk special-request orders receive direct confirmation and controlled handoff.
• Restored system orders are reconciled before reentry so duplicate production and duplicate charges are prevented.
1. Confirm whether the failure affects one display, one station, printers, the POS interface, or the entire kitchen system.
2. Notify the manager, expo, cooks, servers, bar, to-go team, and approved technical support.
3. Activate the approved paper-ticket or verbal backup process with one designated control point.
4. Number and time every order and record table, server, course, modifiers, allergies, and special requests clearly.
5. Require direct confirmation for allergy and high-risk special-request orders before cooking.
6. Maintain one master order queue and mark fired, working, completed, delivered, cancelled, and remade items.
7. Prevent duplicate production by controlling reprints, verbal calls, and ticket handoffs.
8. Communicate realistic delays and menu restrictions to the floor and stop orders the kitchen cannot track safely.
9. After restoration, reconcile open checks and system orders before reentering or closing tickets.
10. Document the failure, backup period, missed or duplicate items, support contact, and corrective action.


173. Kitchen Display System Down SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager can state the backup or closure trigger, operating authority, communication plan, required records, and
recovery approval for kitchen display system down.
□ Notify the manager, expo, cooks, servers, bar, to-go team, and approved technical support.
□ Number and time every order and record table, server, course, modifiers, allergies, and special requests clearly.
□ Maintain one master order queue and mark fired, working, completed, delivered, cancelled, and remade items.
□ Communicate realistic delays and menu restrictions to the floor and stop orders the kitchen cannot track safely.
□ Document the failure, backup period, missed or duplicate items, support contact, and corrective action.
• Allowing multiple paper, printer, and verbal queues to compete without one control point.
• Sending tickets without times, table/server identity, modifiers, or allergy information.
• Firing duplicate orders because status was not marked and communicated.
• Continuing volume the kitchen cannot track safely.
• Reentering restored orders without reconciling completed, open, cancelled, and paid checks.

174. Emergency Closure and Reopening SOP

SOP ID V2-174 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To close and reopen the restaurant in a controlled manner when an emergency or operating failure
makes normal service unsafe, illegal, or unreliable.
APPLIES TO: Managers, keyholders, department leads, system contacts, vendors, and designated crisis leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: Management activates the approved continuity plan, protects people and food, assigns
communication, and authorizes reopening or recovery.
WHEN USED: When a utility, system, facility, or crisis condition interrupts normal operations or requires closure or
recovery.
• Emergency closure and reopening checklist
• Employee, guest, vendor, authority, and ownership contacts
• Food, utility, equipment, cash, access, and temperature records
• Approved closure and reopening communication templates
• Inspection, repair, sanitation, and manager-approval records
• Authorized management closes or restricts operations whenever required safety and legal controls cannot be maintained.
• Employees and guests receive clear, consistent instructions without unsupported reopening promises.
• The building, cash, food, equipment, records, keys, and utilities are secured before departure.
• Reopening requires verified utilities, sanitation, food disposition, equipment, staffing, access, and any required regulatory
clearance.
1. Assess life safety, utilities, food safety, sanitation, staffing, access, systems, weather, and official direction.
2. The authorized manager decides whether to restrict service, stop new orders, evacuate, shelter, or close.
3. Notify employees, guests, delivery partners, vendors, ownership, landlords, and authorities using approved messages.
4. Resolve or document active orders, payments, refunds, reservations, employee time, deliveries, and guest property.
5. Protect or dispose of food based on documented time, temperature, contamination, water, and utility conditions.
6. Shut down or secure equipment, chemicals, cash, records, keys, alarms, doors, utilities, and the building.
7. Maintain the incident timeline, responsible people, pending repair, inspection, and communication assignments.
8. Before reopening, verify utilities, water, ventilation, refrigeration, sanitation, pest control, equipment, and safe access.
9. Verify staffing, product, systems, cash, records, permits, authority clearance, and employee briefing.
10. The authorized manager signs the reopening checklist and communicates the approved reopening time.


174. Emergency Closure and Reopening SOP -
VERIFICATION & CUSTOMIZATION

□ The manager can state the backup or closure trigger, operating authority, communication plan, required records, and
recovery approval for emergency closure and reopening.
□ The authorized manager decides whether to restrict service, stop new orders, evacuate, shelter, or close.
□ Resolve or document active orders, payments, refunds, reservations, employee time, deliveries, and guest property.
□ Shut down or secure equipment, chemicals, cash, records, keys, alarms, doors, utilities, and the building.
□ Before reopening, verify utilities, water, ventilation, refrigeration, sanitation, pest control, equipment, and safe access.
□ The authorized manager signs the reopening checklist and communicates the approved reopening time.
• Continuing emergency closure and reopening without an approved backup, restriction, closure, or recovery plan.
• Losing control of people, food, payments, orders, equipment, access, or required records.
• Giving inconsistent messages or promising restoration/reopening before verification.
• Restarting systems before utilities, safety, sanitation, transactions, and data are reconciled.
• Closing the incident without documented decisions, losses, corrective action, and management approval.

175. End-of-Incident Documentation and Recovery
Review SOP

SOP ID V2-175 VERSION 1.0

PURPOSE: To create an accurate end-of-incident record, complete recovery work, identify preventable failures, and
verify that corrective actions are sustained.
APPLIES TO: Managers, keyholders, department leads, system contacts, vendors, and designated crisis leaders.
RESPONSIBILITY: Management activates the approved continuity plan, protects people and food, assigns
communication, and authorizes reopening or recovery.
WHEN USED: When a utility, system, facility, or crisis condition interrupts normal operations or requires closure or
recovery.
• Incident reports, logs, messages, photos, video, and witness records
• Food, temperature, transaction, labor, vendor, repair, and loss records
• Insurance, regulatory, ownership, and legal-notification requirements
• Corrective-action and responsibility tracker
• SOP revision, training, and follow-up verification records
• The review separates confirmed facts, estimates, decisions, and unresolved questions.
• Medical, employee, guest, payment, security, and legal information is protected and shared only as authorized.
• Every corrective action has a named owner, deadline, priority, and verification method.
• The incident is not closed until required reports, repairs, reconciliation, SOP updates, and retraining are complete.
1. Assign an authorized review leader and secure all incident records and evidence.
2. Build a timeline of warnings, decisions, actions, communications, responder activity, closure, and recovery.
3. Separate confirmed facts from estimates and record the source of each material fact.
4. Document injuries, guest impact, food loss, sales loss, labor, refunds, damage, repair, vendor, and insurance information.
5. Confirm required notifications, reports, claims, authority contact, and record-retention requirements.
6. Identify what worked, what failed, contributing system conditions, and controls that were missing or unclear.
7. Assign corrective actions, owners, priorities, deadlines, resources, and objective verification methods.
8. Update affected SOPs, emergency plans, contacts, equipment, forms, and training materials.
9. Retrain affected employees and verify repairs and corrective actions during actual operations.
10. Complete management sign-off, schedule follow-up audits, and retain the final recovery record securely.

□ Document injuries, guest impact, food loss, sales loss, labor, refunds, damage, repair, vendor, and insurance
information.
□ Identify what worked, what failed, contributing system conditions, and controls that were missing or unclear.
□ Update affected SOPs, emergency plans, contacts, equipment, forms, and training materials.
□ Complete management sign-off, schedule follow-up audits, and retain the final recovery record securely.
• Continuing end-of-incident documentation and recovery review without an approved backup, restriction, closure, or
recovery plan.
• Losing control of people, food, payments, orders, equipment, access, or required records.
• Giving inconsistent messages or promising restoration/reopening before verification.
• Restarting systems before utilities, safety, sanitation, transactions, and data are reconciled.

OPERATIONAL GLOSSARY
Use these definitions as practical operating language for this SOP Library. They explain how the terms are used
inside The Gravity System; they do not replace applicable law, code, manufacturer instructions, professional advice,
or the restaurant's approved policies.
Approved Version - The current SOP version authorized for training and use. Superseded versions should be
removed from active use or clearly marked as retired.
Calibration - The check or adjustment used to confirm that a thermometer, scale, test device, or other measuring
tool is producing an acceptable reading before the result is relied on.
Corrective Action - A documented response used to correct a failure, assign responsibility, reduce repeat risk, and
verify that the required follow-up was completed.
Critical Standards - The non-negotiable conditions that define safe, accurate, compliant, or acceptable
performance for the procedure.
Cross-Contact - The unintended transfer of an allergen from one food, surface, utensil, hand, glove, or piece of
equipment to another.
Disposition - The documented decision about what happens to questionable, unsafe, damaged, recalled, or
uncontrolled product, such as hold, return, rework when approved, or discard.
Effective Date - The date an approved SOP becomes the restaurant's active operating standard.
Escalation Authority - The manager, owner, person in charge, or other authorized role that must make a decision
when the employee cannot safely or properly resolve an exception.
Exception - A condition that falls outside the approved procedure or limit and requires documentation, correction, or
management review.
FIFO - First In, First Out. A stock-rotation method that uses older approved product before newer product when
dates and product condition allow.
Hold - A temporary control that keeps product, equipment, or an area from normal use until an authorized person
evaluates the condition and releases, corrects, returns, or discards it.
Manager Verification - Documented confirmation that the employee or process met the required standard during
actual operations, not only during explanation or classroom training.


OPERATIONAL GLOSSARY - CONTINUED
Next Review Date - The scheduled date for management to confirm that the SOP is still accurate, usable, and
consistent with current operations and requirements.
Owner / SOP Owner - The person or role responsible for maintaining the SOP, routing revisions, and making sure
the current approved version remains available.
Person in Charge - The manager or other authorized individual responsible for required operational and food-
safety decisions during the shift, as defined by restaurant policy and applicable requirements.
PPE - Personal protective equipment required for a task, such as gloves, eye protection, aprons, or other approved
protection appropriate to the hazard.
Restaurant-Specific Customization - The fields that must be completed so a model SOP matches the restaurant's
actual equipment, policies, layout, authority, limits, records, and local requirements.
Revision Note - A reminder to record material changes in the restaurant master SOP index and retrain affected
employees when the approved process changes.
SDS - Safety Data Sheet. Manufacturer or supplier information describing chemical hazards, handling, storage,
protective equipment, and emergency response information.
SOP ID - The unique identification code assigned to a procedure so the restaurant can control versions, training,
approvals, and records without relying only on the title.
Training Sign-Off - The record showing who was trained, who trained or verified the employee, and when the
training occurred.
Training Verification - The internal readiness result used in this library: Not Ready, Retrain, or Approved. It
confirms restaurant training status only and is not a professional credential.
Version - The identifier used to distinguish the current approved SOP from earlier or later revisions.
